# xEdit-rust build plan

## Goal

Port xEdit to Rust with 1:1 functionality, expose every operation through a CLI, and make that CLI the native control surface for AI agents. Keep the port mergeable with upstream Delphi changes. Improve performance through concurrency and SIMD once parity holds.

## What is being ported

Baseline: upstream [TES5Edit/TES5Edit](https://github.com/TES5Edit/TES5Edit), release tag `xedit-4.1.5q` (commit `fd1e360`, recorded in `upstream-map.toml`). The baseline is always an official release, because the release binaries are the parity oracle. About 260,000 lines of Pascal in `Core` and `xEdit`, plus BSArch and Sniff.

| Area | Upstream units | Size (lines) | Notes |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Element and definition model | `wbInterface`, `wbImplementation` | 50,000 | 103 interfaces. The heart of the port. |
| Game record definitions | `wbDefinitions{TES3,TES4,FO3,FNV,TES5,FO4,FO76,SF1,Common,Signatures,Reflection}` | 97,000 | Declarative `wbRecord`/`wbStruct` calls plus callbacks. 14 game modes. |
| Save game definitions | `wbDefinitions*Saves`, `wbSaveInterface` | 34,000 | Tool source `tsSaves`. |
| Load order and session data | `wbLoadOrder`, `wbModGroups`, `wbLocalization`, `wbSteamVDFParser`, `wbHelpers` | 5,000 | |
| Conflict detection | `wbConflict` | 1,000 | |
| Archives and textures | `wbBSA`, `wbBSArchive`, `wbDDS`, `wbCompression`, `wbHash`, BSArch | 8,000 | BSA, BA2, libdeflate, lz4, xxHash. |
| Asset formats | `wbDataFormat*`, `wbNif*`, `wbMeshOptimize`, Sniff | 38,000 | NIF, materials, Wwise. |
| LOD generation | `wbLOD` | 3,700 | |
| Scripting | `xEdit/JvI/*`, `xeScriptHost` | 6,000 | JvInterpreter Pascal. 604 host registrations. 171 shipped scripts in `Build/Edit Scripts`. |
| GUI | `xeMainForm` and 21 other forms | 31,000 | 214 menu handlers on the main form. |
| Startup and tool modes | `xeInit`, `wbCommandLine` | 1,700 | 17 tool modes, more than 60 command-line switches. |

Upstream uses three thread classes in total. Loading, reference building and conflict detection are mostly single-threaded, which is where the concurrency gains are.

## Design rules

1. **One operation layer.** Every operation is a typed command in `xedit-session` with a serde request, a serde response and a JSON Schema. The CLI, the daemon, the MCP server and the GUI all call these commands and nothing else. A feature is not done until its command exists. This is what makes CLI coverage and agent control complete by construction and not a follow-up.
2. **Upstream-shaped source.** Each Pascal unit maps to one Rust module with matching names (`wbLoadOrder.pas` to `load_order.rs`, `wbRecord(` to `wb_record(`). `upstream-map.toml` records the unit, the Rust module and the upstream commit last merged. An upstream diff then maps onto Rust lines directly.
3. **Parity is measured, not asserted.** The official Delphi release build of the baseline is the oracle. A differential harness compares Rust output with oracle output on the same inputs. Each phase has a parity gate.
4. **Safe by default for agents.** Mutating commands support `--dry-run`, return structured outcomes, and require an explicit edit flag. Save is atomic (write temp file, rename), which removes the deferred-save problem of the Delphi daemon.
5. **Machine-first output.** Every CLI command has `--json`. Errors have stable codes. `xedit schema` prints the full command catalogue so an agent can discover the surface without documentation.

## Workspace layout

| Crate | Content |
| --- | --- |
| `xedit-io` | Memory-mapped files, streams, compression, hashes, string encodings. |
| `xedit-core` | Element tree, definition model, records, groups, files, FormIDs. |
| `xedit-defs` | Definition builder API plus one module per game and per save format. |
| `xedit-loadorder` | Game detection, load order, mod groups, localization. |
| `xedit-analysis` | Conflicts, reference index, filters, error checks, cleaning. |
| `xedit-archive` | BSA, BA2, DDS. Not a crate of its own: the archive code is the `archive` module of `xedit-io` (reading was there from phase 1), with the `bsarch` binary in its own crate. |
| `xedit-assets` | NIF, materials, Wwise, mesh optimization, LOD generation. |
| `xedit-script` | JvInterpreter-compatible Pascal interpreter and host API. |
| `xedit-session` | Session state, command registry, jobs, mutation policy, schemas. |
| `xedit-cli` | `xedit` binary: one-shot subcommands, `serve` (JSON-RPC over stdio and named pipe), `mcp`. |
| `xedit-gui` | GUI on top of `xedit-session`. |
| `bsarch`, `sniff`, `xdump` | Binaries matching the upstream tools (`bsarch` exists since phase 5 step 1). |
| `xtask` | Definition transpiler, parity harness, upstream-diff tooling, coverage ledger. |

## Parity harness

- **Oracle:** the official xEdit release binaries of the baseline tag (`xDump.exe`, `xFOEdit.exe`, `xTESEdit.exe`, `xSFEdit.exe`, `BSArch.exe`, `BSArchPro.exe`, `Sniff.exe`). The project has no Delphi compiler, so the oracle cannot be rebuilt and the port source must match the release tag exactly. The harness finds the binaries through the `XEDIT_ORACLE_DIR` environment variable.
- **Secondary oracle:** the `xEdit-llm` automation build returns conflict, reference and cleaning results as JSON, which is easier to compare than GUI state. It is a fork at a different upstream commit, so it is used only to cross-check and never to close a gate.
- **Corpus:** vanilla masters for each game from the local game installs (never committed), plus small synthetic plugins committed under `tests/fixtures`. The harness finds each game's `Data` directory through an environment variable (`XEDIT_FO4_DATA`, `XEDIT_SSE_DATA`, and one per later game). Fallout 4 and Skyrim Special Edition are installed on the development machine; every other game needs its masters before its gate can close.
- **Checks:** full element dump equality, byte-identical round-trip save, conflict status equality, reference index equality, script output equality, archive listing and extraction equality.
- **Coverage ledger:** `coverage/ledger.toml` lists every GUI event binding, game mode, tool mode, command-line switch and script host function with its status and the command that covers it. `cargo xtask sync <upstream checkout>` generates it from the upstream source and adds new upstream entries as `pending`. `cargo xtask check` validates it in CI. The `xEdit-llm` inventory (`Tools/AgentCoverage`) is a reference for which actions are presentation-only.

## Phases

Each phase lists the port work, the CLI surface it adds, the agent skill work, and the gate that closes it.

### How a phase is run

1. **One pull request per phase.** Each phase is developed on its own branch (`phase-1-read-path` and so on) and opened as one pull request. Open it as a draft when the phase starts, so that CI runs on every push. Upstream sync work is not part of a phase and gets its own pull requests.
2. **Suggested model.** Each phase names the Claude model suggested for the implementation work. The suggestion weighs how much of the phase is new design against how much is volume work that the parity harness checks mechanically. Escalate to the next model up when a problem resists two attempts.
3. **Review by Fable.** When the phase gate passes, a fresh Fable 5.1 session reviews the whole pull request before it is merged. The session must be fresh, also for phases that Fable implemented, so that the review does not inherit the implementer's assumptions. Findings are fixed in the same pull request, and the review is repeated on the fixes.

The review checks:

- The gate evidence is real: the harness ran on the full corpus, and no expected output was edited to match the port.
- Ported code follows the upstream unit and keeps its quirks. Deviations are marked `UPSTREAM-QUIRK:` or justified.
- Every operation added in the phase is a session command with a schema, and `coverage/ledger.toml` and `upstream-map.toml` match what was ported.
- Correctness and safety: error handling, `unsafe` blocks with `// SAFETY:` comments, no output that depends on thread count or CPU features.
- License: file headers, upstream origin lines, dependency licenses.
- The agent skills changed in the phase describe what the code now does.

### Phase 0: Foundations

- **Model:** Fable 5.1. Done.
- **Port:** Workspace, command registry and CI (format, clippy, test, `cargo deny check licenses`, `cargo xtask check`). Pin the upstream release in `upstream-map.toml`. Generate the unit map and the coverage ledger. Build the parity harness skeleton.
- **CLI:** `xedit --version`, `xedit schema`, `xedit call <command>`, global `--json` and error code conventions.
- **Skills:** `porting-pascal-unit` (the procedure for porting one unit: headers, naming, map entry, parity test). `checking-parity` (run the harness and read its report).
- **Gate:** CI green. Oracle produces a dump of one vanilla master.
- **Status:** Done. The dump check of the harness (`cargo xtask parity dump`) was added at the start of phase 1. The 4.1.5q `xDump.exe` dumps a vanilla Fallout 4 master (`-FO4`) and a vanilla Skyrim Special Edition master (`-SSE`) on the development machine.

### Phase 1: Read path for the first games

- **Model:** Fable 5.1. The element and definition model and the transpiler set the patterns every later phase copies, and mistakes here are the most expensive to undo.
- **Port:** `xedit-io`, the element and definition model, record and group parsing, compressed records, localized strings, game detection, load order. Write `xtask port-defs`, a transpiler from the Pascal definition calls to the Rust builder API; callbacks are ported by hand. Port the Fallout 4 and Skyrim SE definitions first.
- **CLI:** `xedit session info`, `xedit files list`, `xedit records get|find|list`, `xedit elements get`, `xedit dump` (xDump equivalent).
- **Skills:** `using-xedit-cli` version 1, covering read-only inspection. `porting-definitions` (run the transpiler, port callbacks, verify).
- **Performance:** Memory-map files. Add criterion benchmarks for load time and dump time against the Delphi numbers. Parallel parsing with rayon moves to phase 3: the game globals are process-wide statics and the element model was not written for concurrent initialisation (issue #4), which the write path has to settle first.
- **Gate:** Dump of every vanilla Fallout 4 master and every vanilla Skyrim Special Edition master equals the oracle dump. Both games close the gate together; neither is a follow-up.
- **Status:** Done (pull request #3, merged 2026-10-06). `cargo xtask parity dump` reports 190 of 190 files equal: 170 Fallout 4 and 10 Skyrim SE files byte for byte, and 10 Fallout 4 files equal up to the point where the oracle itself crashes (`wbConditionAliasToStr` on INFO alias conditions, see the `checking-parity` skill). Dump performance was taken on in issue #4 (pull request #5): `Update.esm` loads in 0.6 s and dumps in 19 s against the oracle's 2.4 s and 98 s; `Skyrim.esm` dumps in 182 s. The Fable review of the phase was skipped at merge time and is owed.

### Phase 2: All games and saves

- **Model:** Sonnet 5.5. High-volume, mechanical work driven by the transpiler and checked by dump parity. Hand a definition callback or a parity difference that resists two attempts to Opus 5.5.
- **Port:** Remaining definitions (TES3, TES4, FO3, FNV, TES5, Enderal, VR variants, FO76, SF1 including reflection data) and the save game definitions.
- **CLI:** `--game` for all 14 game modes, `xedit saves dump`.
- **Skills:** Extend `using-xedit-cli` with per-game notes. First draft of `syncing-upstream` (see below), exercised on definition-only upstream commits, which are the most common kind.
- **Gate:** Dump parity for every game mode and every save format.
- **Status:** Gate met 2026-10-07 on branch `phase-2-all-games` (pull request #8). `cargo xtask parity dump` over all 11 games: 246 of 249 files equal or equal-prefix; the 3 others are the Morrowind masters, which the oracle cannot dump (#9). `cargo xtask parity saves` over 58 saves of 6 games: all equal, equal-prefix or equal-error. The Skyrim LE saves are prefix checks against a 15 minute oracle run (`--oracle-timeout`), because the oracle raises an exception per unresolved FormID and writes 1 KB per second on them; the Oblivion saves end with the same header magic error in the oracle and the port, because upstream's Oblivion save definitions expect `FO3SAVEGAME`. Enderal is not installed (#7).

### Phase 3: Write path and daemon

- **Model:** Opus 5.5. Byte-identical saving and FormID and master rewriting are subtle but follow the upstream code closely.
- **Port:** Element editing, add and remove, copy as override and as new record, master management, FormID change and renumber, ESL/ESM/ESP flag handling, sort, save.
- **Performance:** Parse files in parallel with rayon, once the globals and the element initialisation are safe to share between threads (moved here from phase 1). Build and format records on worker threads for the dump (issue #4, item 3). Done in step 8; the threading model is below.
- **CLI:** `xedit elements set|add|remove`, `xedit records copy|delete`, `xedit masters add|clean|sort`, `xedit formids change|renumber`, `xedit files flags`, `xedit save`, `xedit batch`, all mutating ones with `--dry-run`. `xedit serve` keeps a session loaded and accepts the same commands as JSON-RPC. `xedit mcp` exposes the command registry as MCP tools generated from the schemas.
- **Skills:** `using-xedit-cli` version 2 with the mutation rules (edit flag, dry run, save, readback). `adding-a-command` (command, schema, CLI subcommand, test, ledger entry).
- **Gate:** Load then save is byte-identical for the corpus. A scripted edit sequence gives the same saved bytes as the oracle. Met 2026-10-07 for every plugin the oracle saves and for 3 of 4 edit sequences (see Status).
- **Steps** (one pull request, `phase-3-write-path`, #12; each step lands with its own check):
  1. **Round-trip save** (done): the modified states, `PrepareSave`, `WriteToStream`, the CRC32, `files.save` with `--dry-run` and the `--edit` gate, `cargo xtask parity roundtrip`.
  2. **Element editing** (done): the writeable storage of a record (`dcDataStorage`, `UpdateStorageFromElements`, `MergeStorage`), `SetEditValue`, `SetNativeValue` and `SetToDefault` for every definition type, every `AfterSet` and `AfterLoad` callback of the definitions, the required members added on init, the full `PrepareSave` (`HEDR`, `INCC`, `ONAM`, the flags that follow the extension, the FormID clamp), the record header read from `mrStruct`; `xedit elements set`, `xedit batch`.
  3. **Add, remove and copy** (done): `Add`, `Assign`, `RemoveElement`, `CopyInto` as override and as new record; `xedit elements add|remove`, `xedit records copy|delete`.
  4. **Masters** (done): `AddMaster`, `CleanMasters`, `SortMasters`, `MastersUpdated` with the FormID remap; `xedit masters add|clean|sort`.
  5. **FormIDs and flags** (done): `SetLoadOrderFormID`, renumbering, the ESL/ESM/ESP flag handling of `TwbFile`, `SortRecords`; `xedit formids change|renumber`, `xedit files flags`.
  6. **Oracle save** (done): the harness drives the oracle GUI build headlessly (its script mode, `-script:`, on private copies of the plugins) to produce the bytes xEdit saves, for the files the round trip cannot judge by the input alone (dropped `OFST` data, rebuilt `ONAM`, resorted groups, recompressed records) and for the scripted edit sequences of the gate; `cargo xtask parity oracle-save` and `cargo xtask parity oracle-edit`.
  7. **Daemon** (done): `xedit serve` (JSON-RPC over stdio and a named pipe) and `xedit mcp`.
  8. **Parallel loading** (done): files parsed with rayon, records built and formatted on worker threads for the dump (issue #4, item 3), once the globals are safe to share.
  9. **Skills and docs** (done): `using-xedit-cli` version 2, `adding-a-command`, README, ledger, map.
- **Threading model** (step 8, audit of every static, lock and lazily built table that loading and dumping touch; the code is `crates/xedit-core/src/threads.rs`):
  - *Immutable after setup:* the settings of `globals.rs` (atomics and `RwLock`s), the definitions and every table the definition units fill (`Global<T>` in `xedit-defs`, `RECORD_DEFS` and its index, the group order, the named indices, the record header and flag definitions, the FormID formaters, the contained-in definitions created once), the known subrecord signatures, the language encodings, the resource containers and the Starfield Wwise index. They are written while a game is set up and loaded, before any parallel step.
  - *Per thread, as upstream's `threadvar`s:* the internal edit count and block (`_InternalEditCount`, `_BlockInternalEdit`), the resolve guard of an element (`esResolving`), and `PrecombinedCache`. The progress callback stays process-wide, so messages of worker threads reach stderr too, in no fixed order; stdout never carries them.
  - *Shared, with a lock:* the init of a container (`InitOnce`): another thread waits for a build in progress instead of seeing half of it, a build that needs a build of the waiting thread gives up and is counted (`init_cycles`; the dump then writes the affected batches again on one thread, with a warning; none seen on the corpus). A main record is reset under the lock of its element list and read through `read_built`, so a reader that meets a reset builds the record again, and the released elements are dropped only once every line of the dump that began before the reset is done (`retire`, `ReadGuard`), so an element a line still holds keeps its links to its record. The list of built records (`INITIALIZED_RECORDS`, trimmed with `try_lock`) and the pins of the records a worker writes. The string tables (loaded once under a lock). The FaceGen caches of the Fallout 4, Fallout 76 and Skyrim callbacks (built outside the lock, first one stored wins; the key is the race editor ID as upstream's, so two races with the same editor ID would make the cache depend on order, as it depends on the record order upstream).
  - *Parallel:* the scan of a plugin (`implementation/scan.rs`: groups from 128 KB on are read on the workers, the registration with the file and its masters is replayed on the calling thread in file order) and the dump (`ParallelDump`: the main records in batches of 512 on the workers, at most 4 batches at a time, written in file order).
  - *Serial only:* the load of a save (`TwbFileSource.Scan`) and the state the save definitions keep while one is read (the counters of `fo4saves`, the tables of `save_interface`, the flags of `fnvsaves`), `xedit saves dump`, and every session command (the edit paths read and change element lists directly and use the process-wide `wbCopyIsRunning`). A later parallel step must keep them on one thread or move that state into a context.
- **Status:** Phase 3 is done (2026-10-07, draft pull request #12, branch `phase-3-write-path`, steps 1 to 9). Gate: load then save is byte-identical to the oracle's own save for every corpus plugin the oracle saves. `cargo xtask parity oracle-save` over the 249 plugins of 11 games: 239 `equal`, 7 `equal-error` (the same refusal in the oracle and the port), 3 `oracle-unsupported` (the Morrowind masters, which the 4.1.5q GUI can not save), 0 `different`; `cargo xtask parity roundtrip` with those saves cached: 239 `equal`, 10 `refused`, 0 in every other class. Scripted edit sequences (`cargo xtask parity oracle-edit`): 3 of 4 give the oracle's bytes; `fo4-dlcworkshop01-masters` differs (the sort of unchanged sorted arrays after a master update, owed below). The registry has 19 commands: `system.version`, `session.info`, `files.list`, `records.list|find|get|copy|delete`, `elements.get|set|add|remove`, `masters.add|sort|clean`, `formids.change|renumber`, `files.flags` and `files.save`, each with a CLI subcommand (or `xedit call`), `--dry-run` for the mutating ones and the `--edit` gate; `xedit batch`, `xedit serve` and `xedit mcp` run the same registry; loading and `xedit dump` use all CPUs (`--threads`). Skills: `using-xedit-cli` version 2 and `adding-a-command`; the coverage ledger and `upstream-map.toml` match what landed (`cargo xtask check`). The results of each step follow, then the list of what is owed. Steps 1 and 2 landed on `phase-3-write-path` (Fable 5.1 in place of Opus 5.5; the user continued the project on the running model). Step 1, `cargo xtask parity roundtrip` over the 249 plugins of the 11 games, 2026-10-07: 132 `equal`, 38 `ofst-dropped`, 2 `refused` (the two Starfield blueprint masters, which upstream refuses with the same message), 77 `unsupported` (the header edits upstream makes on save), 0 `different`, 0 failed. Step 2, the same run, 2026-10-07: 125 `equal`, 46 `ofst-dropped`, 24 `header-edited`, 15 `structure-edited`, 5 `refused`, 0 `unsupported`, 34 `different`, 0 failed; `cargo xtask parity dump` unchanged (10 of 10 spot-checked files equal). The 3 new `refused` are the Morrowind masters: the port's `PrepareSave` stops at `must have a FormID` because the identity FormID of a Morrowind record is not ported yet (owed below; step 6 found that the 4.1.5q GUI saves no Morrowind plugin at all, so there is no upstream save to match). The 34 `different` are, by the subrecord diff of each saved file, the three changes upstream makes to records on save that the input cannot show: 25 files whose worldspaces grew by the members `wbWorldAfterLoad` adds (upstream initialises every worldspace on load, `TwbFile.Scan`), 9 files with a FormID beyond their masters clamped by `PrepareSave` (`Oblivion.esm` 10, `Skyrim.esm` 1, the Fallout 3 and New Vegas DLC), and the Skyrim masters, whose `PrepareSave` marks the child groups of the worldspaces with dropped offsets modified (359 `CELL` records rebuilt from their elements) and sets the persistent flag of 4 `ONAM` overrides of `Dawnguard.esm`. Step 6 confirms all of them against the oracle's own save. The 7 `equal` files that became `ofst-dropped` or `header-edited` are the ones whose required members or `CLSZ` are now handled as upstream does. Step 7 (daemon) landed on the same branch: `xedit serve` and `xedit mcp` read their method and tool lists from the registry at run time, so the commands of steps 3 to 5 need no change in them; `crates/xedit-cli/tests/daemon.rs` drives both as processes (stdio, and the named pipe with a second client after the first), and unit tests assert that every registered command is a `serve` method and an MCP tool and that the `--edit` gate and `dry_run` hold in both. `Registry::run_batch` moved from the CLI into `xedit-session` so `xedit batch` and `rpc.batch` share one path. `cargo xtask parity roundtrip` after step 7 is unchanged from step 2 (125 `equal`, 46 `ofst-dropped`, 24 `header-edited`, 15 `structure-edited`, 5 `refused`, 0 `unsupported`, 34 `different`, 0 failed), as the step does not touch the save path. Step 4 (masters) landed on the same branch: `implementation/masters.rs` ports `AddMasters`, `AddMastersIfMissing` and `AddMasterIfMissing` (with `aSortMasters` and `aSilent`), `SortMasters`, `CleanMasters`, `HasMaster`, `GetIsEditable` and the `MastersUpdated` and `FindUsedMasters` chains through the file, its groups (the labels of child groups), main records (the record FormID), subrecords, unions and values down to `TwbIntegerDef`, `TwbFormIDDefFormater`, `TwbIntegerDefFormaterUnion` and `TwbStringMgefCodeDef`; `FixupFormID`, `TwbSlotCounts.Create` and `TwbUsedMasters` are in `interface/form_id.rs`, and `FileImpl::sort_records` (`SortRecords`) can run again after a change. `masters.add`, `masters.sort` and `masters.clean` (`xedit masters add|sort|clean`) run them with `--dry-run` and the `--edit` gate. `crates/xedit-session/tests/masters.rs` checks the saved bytes on synthetic plugins: adding a master and cleaning it again gives the input back byte for byte, and sorting and cleaning give the bytes of the plugin written with the target master list. On `HearthFires.esm`, adding `Dawnguard.esm` and cleaning it again leaves every FormID as loaded; the only differences to a plain save are the ones an initialised record shows (sorted subrecords, the required members and `AfterLoad` fixes of 6 records, the 76 empty groups a modified group drops), because `MastersUpdated` initialises every record, as upstream does. `cargo xtask parity roundtrip` after step 4 is unchanged from step 2 (125 `equal`, 46 `ofst-dropped`, 24 `header-edited`, 15 `structure-edited`, 5 `refused`, 0 `unsupported`, 34 `different`, 0 failed); `cargo xtask parity dump` 3 of 3 spot-checked files equal. Step 5 (FormIDs and flags) landed on the same branch: `implementation/form_ids.rs` ports `SetLoadOrderFormID` (the quiet change of a new record and the full one: `RemoveMainRecord`, `RemoveOverride` or `YouAreTheMaster` for the overrides, the new header FormID with the fixed FormID cleared, `AddMainRecord` with the override and injection registration and the light, medium and update checks, `YouGotAMaster` for an injected record), `UpdateInteriorCellGroup` (an interior cell moves to the block and sub-block of its object ID, made when missing, and an emptied one goes), the whole `SetGroupLabel` (the FileID of a new record, the cell children groups, the contained-in elements), `GetIsInjected` and the named index updates; the main record's `SetEditValue` and `SetNativeValue` now change its FormID. `implementation/file_flags.rs` ports `GetIsNotPlugin` and the `GetIs`/`SetIs` pairs of the ESM, light, medium, update, blueprint and localized flags of `TwbFile`, and `TwbMainRecordStructFlags` clears the light, medium and update flags of each other as upstream. `implementation/refs.rs` ports `BuildRef` (with `AddReferencedFromID` and `CanContainFormIDs`) and `CompareExchangeFormID` of every element, of `TwbIntegerDef`, `TwbFormIDDefFormater` (with its `dfUseLoadOrder` quirk) and `TwbIntegerDefFormaterUnion`. Decision on the references: upstream updates the records in `ReferencedBy` of the master, which `BuildRef` of all loaded files fills; until the reference index of phase 4 exists, `ReferenceScan` finds the same records on demand: in each file that can see the target's FormID it builds the references of every record whose data holds the FormID's four bytes, and keeps the records with a collected FormID that resolves (as `DoBuildRef` resolves it) to the target or one of its overrides. Phase 4 replaces the scan behind `ReferenceScan::referenced_by` with the index. The commands `formids.change` (`mniNavChangeFormIDClick`: a given FormID, the next free one of the file or of a master with `target_file`, the later overrides with `overrides`, a missing master added as `AddRequiredMaster` does), `formids.renumber` (`mniNavRenumberFormIDsFromClick` with `start`, `compact` and `inject_into` with its two questions as parameters, the next object ID moved after) and `files.flags` (with the light, medium and update compatibility of the new records) run with `--dry-run` and the `--edit` gate (a dry run reports as with `--edit`). `crates/xedit-session/tests/formids.rs` covers a change with its references in two files, a master record with its overrides, a taken FormID, a change that adds a master, a renumbering, an interior cell moving block and the ESL flag. On `ccBGSSSE001-Fish.esm` a renumbering from `100000` changed 5336 records and updated 7698 references in 2.3 s; the dump of the saved file has no FormID of the old range left, the same number of references to the plugin, and is the dump of the input apart from the FormIDs and the changes of a plain save (the worldspace `CNAM`, the empty cell children group, the `ONAM` order). The clamp of `PrepareSave` now finds the child group by the old FormID before it changes the label. `cargo xtask parity roundtrip` after step 5 is unchanged from step 2, outcome by outcome including the details (125 `equal`, 46 `ofst-dropped`, 24 `header-edited`, 15 `structure-edited`, 5 `refused`, 0 `unsupported`, 34 `different`, 0 failed). Step 3 (add, remove and copy) landed on the same branch: `Assign` and `CanAssign` of every element class and definition type (`TwbDef.Assign` with the overrides of the integer, FormID, float, flags and formater union definitions; `CanAssign` of every value, formater and record member definition) in `implementation/assign.rs`, the subrecord, value and main record files, `interface/def.rs` and the definition files; `Add` of the file, the groups and the main records with the child groups of a `CELL`, `DIAL`, `WRLD` and `QUST` record (the step 2 owed item), the new record and group constructors, `SetEditorID`, `SetGridCell`, `SetPosition`, `Remove` and `IsRemovable` with `BeforeActualRemove`, `TwbMainRecord.Remove` with its overrides (`YouAreTheMaster`) in `implementation/add.rs`; `CopyInto`, `wbCopyElementToFile`, `wbCopyElementToRecord`, `AddIfMissing` of every container class, `ReportRequiredMasters` and `AddRequiredMasters` (which adds the masters through step 4's `AddMastersIfMissing`) in `implementation/copy.rs`; `NewFormID` with `GetNextObjectID`, `SetNextObjectID` and `GetHighObjectID` in `implementation/new_form_id.rs`. `elements.add`, `elements.remove`, `records.copy` and `records.delete` (`xedit elements add|remove`, `xedit records copy|delete`) run them with `--dry-run` and the `--edit` gate. A path set by `elements.set` now resolves a signature and `[n]` like `ResolveElementName`. `crates/xedit-session/tests/structure.rs` checks the saved bytes on synthetic Skyrim SE plugins (a record copied as an override and as a new record, a copy that adds its master, a member added and removed, a record deleted with its group dropped on save, a reference added to an interior cell and the cell copied deep into a plugin) and, with `XEDIT_SSE_DATA`, a copy of an NPC from `Skyrim.esm` into `Update.esm` that keeps every value. Compared element by element, 308 records of `Skyrim.esm` (3 per signature) copied into `Update.esm` and 364 of `Fallout4.esm` copied into `DLCRobot.esm` keep every value except their localised strings (below); the other differences follow the upstream code: an empty array subrecord with a count prefix gets the prefix (`TwbArray.AssignInternal` requests `PrefixSize` bytes, `NAM5` of `BPTD`), a trailing optional member the target was made without is not copied (the `Unknown` bytes of `PHWT`, a `wbEmpty` member that cannot take a value), a union subrecord keeps the member its defaults decide (`UNAM` of `WTHR`), and the entries of a copied array show `#n` names until the file is loaded again. `cargo xtask parity roundtrip` after step 3 is unchanged from step 2 (125 `equal`, 46 `ofst-dropped`, 24 `header-edited`, 15 `structure-edited`, 5 `refused`, 0 `unsupported`, 34 `different`, 0 failed); `cargo xtask parity dump` 2 of 2 spot-checked files equal. A union decided again during an assign takes storage of its default size at once instead of building its members over no data (an `UPSTREAM-QUIRK` note in `value.rs`); step 6 checks the saved bytes of the copies against the oracle. Step 6 (oracle save) landed on the same branch. `cargo xtask parity oracle-save` has the GUI build of xEdit save every corpus plugin: `crates/xtask/src/parity/gui.rs` starts `xTESEdit.exe`, `xFOEdit.exe` or `xSFEdit.exe` in its script mode (`-script:`, which loads the plugins, runs the script once they are loaded and stays open; upstream honours `-autoload` and `-autoexit` in the edit mode only), in a private folder per run (a copy of the executable, copies of the plugin and its masters as `-D:`, a plugin list as `-P:`, an empty game ini as `-I:` with `-M:` and `-G:`, and `-T:`, `-C:`, `-B:`, `-S:`), presses OK on the module selection, fails on any other dialog, on a `Fatal:` line or an aborted script in the message log of the main form, on a timeout or when the process stops using CPU, ends the process through its job object and removes the folder; nothing is written to a game install or the user's profile. `crates/xtask/oracle/save.pas` writes the plugin with `FileWriteToStream` (the `WriteToStream` with `PrepareSave` that `SaveChanged` runs) or records the exception. The oracle's saves are cached by the contents of the plugin, its masters and the script in `<cache>/<tag>/<MODE>-oracle-save`, the port's saves go to `XEDIT_PARITY_SCRATCH`, and `parity roundtrip` now counts a save that equals a cached oracle save as `equal` (a refusal with the oracle's message as `refused`) and one that does not as `different`, whatever the save changed in the input. `cargo xtask parity oracle-edit` runs the scripted edit sequences of `crates/xtask/oracle/edits` on both: the port as an `xedit batch`, the oracle as a Pascal script generated from the same commands around `crates/xtask/oracle/edit.pas`. Results, 2026-10-07: the first `oracle-save` run over the 249 plugins gave 232 `equal`, 2 `equal-error`, 12 `different`, 3 `oracle-failed`; after the fixes below, 239 `equal` (the run of 2026-10-07 with `SeventySix.esm` run again after the last fix), 7 `equal-error` (the two Starfield blueprint masters, `Saving blueprint modules is not currently supported.`, and `ShatteredSpace.esm`, `SFBGS003.esm`, `SFBGS006.esm`, `SFBGS00D.esm`, `SFBGS050.esm`, `[TES4:00000000] can not be edited`), 3 `oracle-unsupported` (the Morrowind masters: the 4.1.5q GUI runs Morrowind in its view mode only, `ToolModes := [tmView]` in `xeInit.pas`, so the oracle saves no Morrowind plugin at all; the step 2 note that upstream saves them was wrong) and 0 `different`. `cargo xtask parity roundtrip` with the oracle's saves cached: 239 `equal`, 10 `refused` (7 confirmed by the oracle's refusal, the 3 Morrowind masters unconfirmed), 0 `ofst-dropped`, 0 `header-edited`, 0 `structure-edited`, 0 `unsupported`, 0 `different`, 0 failed: every file of the step 2 classes `ofst-dropped`, `header-edited`, `structure-edited` and `different` (the worldspace members, the FormID clamp, the 359 rebuilt Skyrim cells, the `ONAM` persistence) equals the oracle's save. `cargo xtask parity dump` 4 of 4 spot-checked files equal. The port fixes the oracle found: the sort of a sorted subrecord array when its record is rebuilt (`arcSorted` and `arcSortInvalid` of `TwbSubRecordArray`, `CompareSortKeys`) with the sort keys of `TwbContainer`, `TwbSubRecord` and `TwbSubRecordStruct` (`Anchorage.esm`, `IMPS` of its worldspaces); `GetGroupLabel` and the search of `GetChildGroup`, with the quest children group (`Fallout4.esm` has an empty quest children group labelled with FileID 01, which the save gives the quest's FormID); `ChildrenOf` of an exterior block, and `SetIsPersistent` and `SetIsVisibleWhenDistant` with `CheckChildOfCell` and `UpdateCellChildGroup` (the 4 references of `Dawnguard.esm` that the `ONAM` rebuild sets persistent move to the persistent children of their cell, in Skyrim, Skyrim SE and Skyrim VR); `fsIsOfficial` of the official modules, which Starfield does not let be edited, so `PrepareSave` refuses them where it has to edit the header, and an edit that fails in `PrepareSave` is a refusal (`save_refused`) with upstream's message, the record named as the GUI names it (`wbDisplayShorterNames` during `files.save`); and a record skipped on load for a duplicate FormID (`flSetContainsFixedFormID`, anywhere in the file) is left out of the `HEDR` record count (36 `DFOB` of `SeventySix.esm`). Edit sequences (`parity oracle-edit`): `fnv-caravanpack` (copy as override and as new record, set, add and remove elements, delete a record, a FormID change with a referencing record, add, sort and clean a master, renumber, the ESM flag) equal; `fnv-gmst` (a copied game setting whose editor ID changes type, so the `AfterSet` rebuilds its `DATA`) equal; `fo4-dlcworkshop01` (copy as override and as new record, set, add, remove an array entry by index, a reference to a new record, on a localised master) equal; `fo4-dlcworkshop01-masters` (add `DLCRobot.esm` to `DLCworkshop01.esm`, sort and clean it again) different: 30 `MSWP` and 1 `RACE` whose sorted arrays (`Material Substitutions`, `Movement Type Names`) did not change keep their loaded order in the oracle's save, where the port sorts them; the oracle sorts the same arrays after a plain edit of the record (an editor ID set on `MSWP:01000AA9`), so its `MastersUpdated` and `CollapseStorage` path leaves an unchanged sorted array unsorted, which is not found yet. The phase 3 gate: load then save equals the oracle's own save for every corpus file the oracle can save, and three of the four scripted edit sequences give the oracle's bytes; the master update of a plugin with unchanged sorted arrays is the remaining difference. Step 8 (parallel loading) landed 2026-10-07: the scan of a plugin reads its large groups on worker threads and replays the registration with the file in file order; `xedit dump` builds and writes the main records on worker threads in batches and writes them in file order; `--threads N` (default `RAYON_NUM_THREADS`, else one per CPU) sets the count, `--threads 1` is the serial path. The threading model is above. Measured on the development machine (16 threads, release build) against `b2f7a8f`: `Skyrim.esm` dump 187 s to 34 s, `Fallout4.esm` dump 342 s to 87 s, with the same bytes (md5) as one thread and as before; the load of `Skyrim.esm` (scan and indices) 0.48 s to 0.32 s, `Fallout4.esm` 0.90 s to 0.57 s (the registration of the records, which stays serial, is most of what is left); peak memory of the dump 1.6 to 2.5 GiB on `DLCCoast.esm`, 1.5 to 1.8 GiB on `Skyrim.esm`. `cargo xtask parity roundtrip` after step 8: 239 `equal`, 10 `refused`, 0 in every other class; `parity oracle-save`: 239 `equal`, 7 `equal-error`, 3 `oracle-unsupported`, 0 `different` (both as after step 6); `parity dump` spot check of 12 files of 8 games (`Skyrim.esm` SSE and TES5, `Fallout4.esm`, `DLCCoast.esm`, `NW.esm` and others): all equal; `crates/xedit-cli/tests/threads.rs` checks that 1 and 8 threads give the same dump and saved bytes on three corpus plugins. Owed from phase 3 (deduplicated across the steps; items a later step fixed are dropped): (1) *Sorting.* After `MastersUpdated` the oracle leaves a sorted array whose entries did not change in file order, where the port sorts it (30 `MSWP` and 1 `RACE` of `DLCworkshop01.esm`; the oracle sorts the same arrays after a plain edit, so the path through `MastersUpdated` and `CollapseStorage` that skips the sort is not found yet); the sort of a sorted array value inside a subrecord (`srsSorted`, `arrSorted`: sorting the elements on init made `KWDA` arrays differ from the oracle's after a master update, so they are not sorted); the re-sort of a sorted array after `CompareExchangeFormID` changes an entry. (2) *Storage and elements.* `CollapseStorage`; the flags of a flags value inside the element list of its container (the view and the command layer build `flags_as_array_of` on demand under `wbFlagsAsArray`, so a flags value has them as children in `records get`/`elements get` and the parity probe, but `ElementCount`/`ElementByIndex` of a `TwbValue` are still 0, where the release adds the `TwbFlag`s to it); the Starfield templates; the `IsCS` names (the `wbCS` prefixes); the `GroupToSkip` and `RecordToSkip` lists; a record skipped on load as a duplicate stays in the port's tree (upstream drops it and writes its bytes only through an unmodified group). (3) *Strings.* Writing the string tables of a localized file: `LString` can not be set from text or copied (`Can not assign to a localized string`), so the copies of an empty prefixed array (`BPTD`) and of `PHWT` are not covered by an edit sequence. (4) *Copy.* Closed by the parity batch #22 (branch `parity-22`, PR #45): `aAllowOverwrite` with a deep copy (`wbCanOverwrite`, the "with overwriting" menu items, as `records.copy`'s `overwrite` and `remove_deleted`), `MakePartialForm` (`--allow-make-partial`), `IwbMultipleElements`, template elements as sources and the aligned arrays (`GetAlignable`) through the commands `elements.assign`, `elements.templates` and `elements.move`, with the script adapter's `AssignTemplate*`, `TemplateAssign`, `ElementAssign`, `MoveUp`/`MoveDown` and `InsertElement` on the same operations and `files.new` module templates; the phases of it and the oracle counts are in the Parity backlog section below. Still owed: the editor ID index of a file does not learn the records an edit adds. (5) *References.* The reference index (`mrReferencedBy`, `mrReferences`, `BuildOrLoadRef` and its cache, `ReferencedBy` of the scripts, `BuildRef` of the records an edit makes) is phase 4; until then `ReferenceScan` finds the referencing records on demand, and `FindUsedMasters` always walks the elements. (6) *Masters and games.* The two views of the masters while they update (`fsMastersUpdating` with `flOldMasters`, `eMastersGeneration`, read by the Starfield definitions with `dfAfterSetOnIDUpdate`; without them the perk activity JSON keeps the text it had when a masters update rewrites a FormID of its Stream, where upstream writes the new text through `wbVirtualPerkStreamIDAfterSet`); the Starfield complex FileIDs, ported in the master functions but ignored by the FormID lookups, `SetLoadOrderFormID` and the renumbering; the identity FormID of a Morrowind record (`TES3` saves), which also has no oracle save. (7) *Harness.* An edit sequence with a FormID change needs the oracle's reference information (`build_refs`), which initialises every loaded record, so it runs only on a plugin whose records have no load fix-ups. (8) *Daemon.* A session is fixed at startup (no `session.load` command to switch plugins in a running daemon), the MCP server has no resources, prompts or progress notifications, and one named pipe client is served at a time because there is one session. (9) *Performance.* The registration of the scanned records (`AddMainRecord`, the light and update checks) is serial and the slowest part of a load; a session that loads several plugins loads them one after the other (each with its groups in parallel); the per-record caches that a build seeing another thread's partial build could fill (the precombined mesh, the FaceGen caches) are not reset when the dump writes a batch again after an init cycle (none was seen); the dump's speed-up is bounded by its slowest record per window of four batches (a Fallout 4 `RACE` takes 6 s). Step 9 (skills and docs) landed 2026-10-07: `using-xedit-cli` rewritten as version 2 (the mutation rules first, every command of the registry, the known gaps), the new `adding-a-command` skill, the README and this plan brought level with the write path, `coverage/ledger.toml` and `upstream-map.toml` checked against the registry. The oracle-save cache key now hashes the oracle scripts with LF line endings (a CRLF checkout under `core.autocrlf` missed every cached oracle save), and `.gitattributes` keeps `crates/xtask/oracle/*.pas` LF.

### Phase 4: Analysis and tool modes

- **Model:** Opus 5.5. Many separate features, each with its own oracle check.
- **Port:** Conflict detection, reference index, filters, comparisons, error checks, ITM and UDR cleaning, quick auto clean, mod groups, merged patch, localization tools, and all 17 tool modes with their command-line switches.
- **CLI:** `xedit conflicts`, `xedit refs`, `xedit filter`, `xedit compare`, `xedit check`, `xedit clean`, `xedit modgroups`, `xedit patch merged`, `xedit localization`. The legacy switches (`-quickautoclean`, `-IKnowWhatImDoing` and the rest) stay accepted for compatibility with existing mod manager setups.
- **Skills:** Agent workflow skills on the native CLI: conflict audit, plugin cleaning, patch building. These replace the `xEdit-llm` daemon route.
- **Performance:** Build the reference index and run conflict detection across files in parallel.
- **Gate:** Conflict status, reference index, cleaning result and error check output equal the oracle on the corpus.
- **Steps** (one pull request, `phase-4-analysis`; each step lands with its own check; steps 1, 2, 3 and 7 can start at once, the others wait for the steps named):
  1. **Reference index** (done): `mrReferencedBy`, `BuildRef` of every element, `BuildOrLoadRef` and its cache file, the referenced-by lists in place of the phase 3 `ReferenceScan`, built across files in parallel; `xedit refs`.
  2. **Conflict detection** (done): `ConflictLevelForMainRecord`, `ConflictLevelForContainer`, `ConflictLevelForNodeDatas` and the rest of the conflict code of `xeMainForm`, moved into the core; the injected and sibling cases; run across records in parallel; `xedit conflicts`, `xedit compare`.
  3. **Error checks** (done): `CheckForErrors` and `CheckForErrorsLinear` with every `Check` callback of the definitions; `xedit check`.
  4. **Filters** (done; after 1 and 2): the filter options of `xeFilterOptionsForm` and `ApplyFilter`, filter for cleaning, filter for conflicts, the referenced-by and view filters; `xedit filter`.
  5. **Cleaning** (done; after 2): ITM removal, UDR (undelete and disable references), quick auto clean, cleanup of injected records, `mniNavCleaningObsolete`; `xedit clean`.
  6. **Mod groups** (done): `wbModGroups`, mod group files and CRCs, their effect on conflict status; `xedit modgroups`.
  7. **Localization** (done): adding and writing strings, the string tables of a localized file on save, localize and delocalize a plugin, the translate mode; `xedit localization`.
  8. **Merged patch** (done; after 2 and 6): `mniNavCreateMergedPatchClick` and the merge of leveled lists and containers, the ordered form lists included; `xedit patch merged`.
  9. **Tool modes** (done; after 3, 5 and 7): `wbCommandLine`, `xeInit`, all 17 tool modes with their switches (`-quickautoclean`, `-IKnowWhatImDoing` and the rest), `ESMify`, `ESPify`, ONAM update, master update and restore, sort and clean masters, SEQ generation, export; `tmLODgen` is wired in phase 5.
  10. **Skills and docs** (done). The three agent workflow skills on the native CLI, the replacements for the `xEdit-llm` daemon's comparison, cleaning and patch routes: `auditing-conflicts` (`conflicts`, `compare`, the mod groups that hide records and the reference index), `cleaning-plugins` (`clean --itm --udr --quick`, `records cleanup-injected`, the oracle results and the known differences) and `building-patches` (`patch merged`, `files new` and `records copy`, the mod-group interaction), each describing what the code now does; the README with the phase's results and the gate bullet, and `coverage/ledger.toml` and `upstream-map.toml` checked against the registry (`cargo xtask check`, and `cargo xtask sync` against the upstream checkout adds nothing). The final checks found that the debug build of the CLI overflowed the 1 MiB main thread stack Windows gives a process inside clap's argument parsing (the daemon tests failed, `has overflowed its stack`; release builds stay under it, which is why the harness never saw it, and step 9's commit parses fine), so `crates/xedit-cli/build.rs` now links the binary with a 16 MiB stack on Windows.
- **Status:** Gate met 2026-10-09 (draft pull request #13, branch `phase-4-analysis`): conflict status, reference index, cleaning result and error check output equal the oracle on the corpus. `cargo xtask parity conflicts` 10 of 10 games equal (23,383,514 records classified); `cargo xtask parity refs` 10 of 10 games equal (7,013,961 referenced records with 61,219,249 entries, built and loaded from the cache, 79 of 79 cache files equal after decompression; Starfield's 18 entries of 17 records that the perk activity JSON gives are in since parity #33, which ported it; Morrowind has no reference information in upstream); `cargo xtask parity clean` 231 of 238 plugins equal (the saved bytes, every pass's counts and every cleaned record line), 4 `different` (records the clean did not touch: the `GREETING` response text of three Fallout 3 DLCs and the `DOBJ` of `Fallout4_VR.esm`), 1 `oracle-failed` (`NW.esm`) and 2 `oracle-unsupported` (the Morrowind masters); `cargo xtask parity check` 245 of 249 plugins equal (1 `equal-log-loss`, 3 `oracle-unsupported`, 0 `different`) and `cargo xtask parity check-dump` 246 of 246 files. The rest of the phase is in as well: `parity filter` 37 of 37 (`sse-filters`, 26,849,591 nodes walked) and 8 of 8 (`fo4-filters`) configurations, and the reachable-information scenario (`sse-reachable`, the five Skyrim SE masters with "Build Reachable Info" and 4 configurations, whose result or owed re-run is in step 4's record below); `parity modgroups` 9 of 9 scenarios; `parity merged` 12 of 12; the localization sequences (`parity oracle-edit`) 26 of 27 saved plugins and tables in the ten step-7 sequences and 62 of 63 saved files over all 34 sequences at parity #33 (the one difference the known `sf1-strings-delocalize` emulation limit); `parity tool-modes` 24 of 31 cases (4 `known-difference` in the export, 3 `oracle-unsupported`). Step 10 (skills and docs) landed 2026-10-09: the skills `auditing-conflicts`, `cleaning-plugins` and `building-patches` on the native CLI, the replacements for the `xEdit-llm` daemon's comparison, cleaning and patch routes; the README with the same results; `coverage/ledger.toml` and `upstream-map.toml` checked against the registry; `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo test --workspace` and `cargo xtask check` clean. The results of each step follow. Step 2 (conflict detection) landed 2026-10-08. The conflict code of `xeMainForm.pas` is in `crates/xedit-analysis/src/conflict.rs`, out of the GUI: `ConflictLevelForMainRecord` (with the GMST and DFOB records grouped by editor ID, the NAVI and file header cases, `OnlyShowMasterAndLeafs`, `xeQuickShowConflicts`, the injected case and `Fix`), `ConflictLevelForContainer`, `ConflictLevelForNodeDatas`, `ConflictLevelForChildNodeDatas` (with the translate mode cases), `InitChildren` (sorted containers merged by their sort keys, unsorted arrays aligned with `TDiff`, ported from `External/Diff/Diff.pas` in `diff.rs` with its terms, see `NOTICE`, because which entries it pairs decides the status), `InitNodes`, `InitConflictStatus` over the tree of the view tab (visibility, `HideNoConflict`, the names and cells of `vstViewGetText`), `NodeDatasForMainRecord` and `NodeDatasForContainer`. The result is kept in a `ConflictContext`, not on the records, and the view keeps its own sort orders, so a comparison changes nothing in the tree. `conflict_statuses` classifies the FormID groups on the worker threads in batches (pinned, reset afterwards, again on one thread after an init cycle) and the GMST, DFOB, NAVI and TES4 records on one thread in file order; the output does not depend on the thread count (`thread_count_does_not_change_the_result`, and Skyrim SE and Fallout 76 compared on 1 and 16 threads). The hook for step 6 is `ModGroupFilter` in `ConflictOptions`; steps 4, 5 and 8 build on `ConflictContext`, `conflict_statuses` and `view_for_main_record`. The core gained what the view reads (`implementation/sortable.rs`: `Sorted` and `Alignable` of the sortable containers, `DisplaySortKey`, `ConflictPriorityCanChange`, `ContentIsAllZero`, `esOptionalAndMissing`, the elements in the order `DoInit(True)` leaves them; `implementation/flag.rs`: `TwbFlag` and the flags of `wbFlagsAsArray`, built on demand). Commands: `conflicts.list` (`xedit conflicts`: the status of every record with filters by file, signature, `ConflictAll` and `ConflictThis`, and per file the counts and the highest status) and `records.compare` (`xedit compare`: the records of a FormID side by side, row by row). The gate found and fixed: a session now builds its definitions with the GUI's settings (`wbSimpleRecords`, `wbHideUnused`, `wbCreateContainedIn`; `setup_game_for_edit`) and sets `wbFlagsAsArray` and `wbSortINFO` as the GUI does, where it had xDump's; the sort of the responses of a topic (`TwbGroupRecord.Sort` of a group of type 7 with `ProcessDIAL`, `DoInsertRecord` and the `INOM` and `INOA` lists a topic gets on init, `implementation/info_sort.rs`, a phase 3 owed item); the `wbComplexFileFileID` branches of the FormID lookups of Starfield (`FileFileIDtoLoadOrderFileID`, `IsNewRecord`, `GetMasterRecordByFormID` and the rest; phase 3 owed item 6 in part); and the memory order `AddElement` gives an element, which the rank slot arrays of a Starfield `LGDI` read while they are sized (the oracle's xDump crashes before them, so the dump gate never saw it). Oracle: `cargo xtask parity conflicts` (`crates/xtask/src/parity/conflicts.rs`, `crates/xtask/oracle/conflicts.pas`) has the GUI write `ConflictAllForMainRecord` and `ConflictThisForMainRecord` of every record and compares; `--record` probes single records element by element (`conflicts-probe.pas`). `cargo xtask parity conflicts`, 2026-10-08, the vanilla plugins of each game loaded together: 10 of 10 games equal, 23,383,514 records compared (422,552 that are not the only record of their FormID listed record by record, the rest counted per file): Fallout 4 181 files 2,248,005 records, Skyrim SE 11 files 1,188,933, Oblivion 12 files 1,190,878, Fallout 3 7 files 932,219, New Vegas 11 files 629,839, Skyrim LE 6 files 1,161,503, Skyrim VR 7 files 1,177,152, Fallout 4 VR 3 files 1,548,595, Fallout 76 3 files 5,816,095, Starfield 15 files 7,490,295; Morrowind `oracle-unsupported` (the GUI runs it in view mode, without scripts). The oracle takes about 6 minutes for Fallout 4, the port's `conflicts.list` seconds (Skyrim SE 3.8 s on 16 threads, 12.6 s on one; Fallout 76 23 s and 48 s; the output is byte for byte the same). After the step: `cargo xtask parity roundtrip` 239 `equal`, 10 `refused`, 0 in every other class; `parity oracle-edit` 3 of 4 as before (the `fo4-dlcworkshop01-masters` difference of phase 3); `parity dump` spot check of 9 files (Skyrim SE, Skyrim LE and VR, New Vegas, Fallout 4, Starfield) equal. Owed from step 2: mod groups (step 6, through `ModGroupFilter`); records the user hid (`IsHidden`) and the compare-to load (`IsCompareToSame`) do not exist in the port; the raw data compare (`wbCompareRawData`, `GetRawDataAsString`); `CompareSelected` (`ComparingSiblings`, ported in the algorithm) and the script functions `ConflictAllForElements`, `ConflictAllForNode` and `ConflictThisForNode` have no command; the view does not leave `SortOrder` and `csSortedBySortOrder` on the aligned entries for a later drag and drop; the `PNAM` of `wbFillPNAM` (the quick clean mode, step 5) and the postponed sort of a topic group; the element-level view has no oracle of its own (the oracle compares the record statuses, which the same row code decides); `conflicts --include-single` on a big load order spends most of its time reading editor IDs. Step 1 (reference index) landed 2026-10-08. `implementation/refs.rs` ports the reference index of `wbImplementation.pas`: `TwbMainRecord.BuildRef`, `DoBuildRef` with `ProcessRef` (with the complex FileIDs, and the file a compare load compares to as its last master, as upstream's `AddMaster(flCompareTo)`), `UpdateRefs` from `ElementChanged` and `SetParentModified`, `AddReferencedBy`, `RemoveReferencedBy` with `FindReferencedBy`, `SortReferencedBy` with `CompareReferencedBy`, `ReferencedBy` and `ReferencedByCount` (on the master), `mrReferences` with its `MastersUpdated` fix-up (a record whose references are built visits its elements only when one of them changes, as upstream), the generation check (`cntRefsBuildAt`) as a flag that `SetModified` clears, the lists handed over by `YouAreTheMaster`, `YouGotAMaster`, `SetLoadOrderFormID` and `Remove` (`DoBuildRef(True)`), the `BuildRef` of a record that `TwbGroupRecord.Add` or a copy makes, `TwbFile.BuildRef` and `BuildOrLoadRef`; `TwbChar4.BuildRef` and `TwbStringMgefCodeDef.BuildRef` (the Oblivion magic effect codes, which collect the load order FormID of the effect, an `UPSTREAM-QUIRK`). `implementation/refcache.rs` ports the reference cache file: `SaveRefsToStream` and `LoadRefsFromStream` (the references, editor ID, full name, base record, grid cell state and, for the game master, the GUI's name and short name of every record), the file name with `wbCRC32App`, the CRC32 of the plugin, the code pages and the language, the thresholds (more than 500 records or 2 seconds) and the LZ4 frame; the grid cell state is kept by `TwbMainRecord.Init` as upstream keeps it (`mrsGridCellChecked`). The references of the files to build are collected on the worker threads record by record and added to the lists on the calling thread in file order (upstream builds one file per thread); a list is sorted when it is read, so it does not depend on the thread count, and a batch whose builds needed each other is built again on one thread. The `inherited BuildRef` of a file also builds the main records outside `flRecords` (the file header, whose Fallout 4 transient types refer to records, and records with the null FormID). The phase 3 `ReferenceScan` is gone: `formids.change` and `formids.renumber` read `ReferencedBy` of the master, building the index first (a renumbering reads it per record before the change, as upstream). Commands `refs.get` (`xedit refs get`: the referenced-by list of the master with paging, and the references of the record with the records they resolve to) and `refs.build` (`xedit refs build`: `BuildOrLoadRef` of every loaded file, or `TwbFile.BuildRef` of one), `xedit refs dump` (every list as text, for the check), and the global options `--cache-path`, `--dont-cache`, `--dont-cache-load` and `--dont-cache-save` (xEdit's `-C:` and `-DontCache...`; the cache folder defaults to xEdit's `<AppName>Edit Cache` in the data folder). The CLI builds the index when a command first needs it, not on load; Morrowind has none (`wbBuildRefs`). Fixes the check found in earlier code: `SetDataSize` inside the init of a subrecord or value (an `AfterLoad` that resizes it, the Fallout 3 conditions of 20 bytes with "Run On Target") runs the init again instead of leaving the element empty; a name of four characters in a path finds a subrecord struct or array by the signature of its first member (`GetElementBySignature` in `ResolveElementName`), which the Oblivion `wbREFRAfterLoad` needs to drop `XPCI`. Check: `cargo xtask parity refs` loads the vanilla plugins of each game into the GUI oracle in its script mode, which builds the reference information on load, has `crates/xtask/oracle/refs.pas` write `ReferencedByCount` and `ReferencedByIndex` of every master record, and keeps the cache files the oracle writes (`-C:`); the port loads the same plugins (links to them in a folder of their own, so neither side has archives or strings) in the oracle's load order with `--edit` (the GUI edits, which lets the load fix-ups run where `wbAllowInternalEdit` is off) and writes the lists with `xedit refs dump`, once building and saving its cache files and once loading them; the lists are compared record by record and entry by entry, and the cache files after decompression. Results with the final build, 2026-10-08: equal for 9 of the 10 games that have reference information: Oblivion (11 plugins, 55,846 referenced records with 1,478,763 entries, 8 cache files), Fallout 3 (6 plugins, 72,692 records, 1,139,730 entries, 6 cache files), New Vegas (10, 88,881, 927,215, 6), Skyrim (5, 178,741, 1,836,687, 5), Skyrim SE (10, 189,336, 1,940,071, 8), Skyrim VR (6, 185,033, 1,898,156, 6), Fallout 4 (180, 1,676,153, 5,700,743, 28), Fallout 4 VR (2, 1,162,010, 3,685,277, 1) and Fallout 76 (2, 2,220,438, 14,622,187, 2): 5,829,130 referenced records with 33,228,829 entries equal entry by entry, built and loaded from the cache, and 70 of 70 cache files equal after decompression. Parity #33 (2026-10-16) adds Starfield: 14 plugins, 1,184,831 records with 27,990,420 entries, equal in both passes as well — the `ATAV` JSON of a perk activity becomes the virtual `NULL` tree on load (`wbPerkActivityAfterLoad`), which the 18 entries of 17 records and the 2 cache files that lacked them (`Starfield.esm` and `ShatteredSpace.esm`; 9 of 9 now equal) come from; Morrowind has no reference information in upstream (`wbBuildRefs` is off). The cache files hold the same bytes after decompression; the compressed files differ, as upstream compresses with LZ4 HC level 12 and the port with `lz4_flex`, and each reads the other's (the port's files load in the check's second run). Performance (16 threads, release build, the machine shared with other runs): the Skyrim SE masters (5 plugins) load in 2.1 s and build their references in 23 s on 16 threads against 60 s on one, and load them from the cache in under a second; the Fallout 3 masters build in 4.6 s against 14 s on one thread. `cargo xtask parity roundtrip` after step 1: 239 `equal`, 10 `refused`, 0 in every other class (unchanged from phase 3). The Starfield perk activity JSON (`wbPerkActivityAfterLoad`, `wbVirtualPerkActivityUpdateJSON`, the virtual `NULL` elements and `SerializeElementToJson`) was the only difference of the check and is ported by parity #33 (2026-10-16). Owed from step 1 besides: `BuildReachable` and `Reached` ("Build Reachable Info", which a filter by reachability needs, step 4); `SetReferencesInjected` and the cached `GetReferencesInjected` (the error checks of step 3 read `ReferencesInjected`); `TwbGroupRecord.BuildRef` (`SetChildGroup`, which the port's tree has without it); a record whose references come from the cache takes its editor ID and full name from it, not its base record, grid cell or GUI names (read from the record when needed); `CleanupRefCache` of the GUI (phase 7); `UpdateRefs` while a record's own init changes it is skipped (upstream walks the elements built so far, an `UPSTREAM-QUIRK` not reproduced); the hardcoded file is not given the game master as a master (only the reference resolution and the editor ID lookups treat it as one); the cache names are written with the GUI's display settings (`wbDisplayShorterNames`), set for the save and restored. Downstream: filters (step 4) and cleaning (step 5) use `MainRecordImpl::referenced_by`, `referenced_by_count` and `references` after `Session::ensure_refs` (or `refs::build_or_load_refs`); the lists are of masters (call them on any version, as upstream's `ReferencedBy` of an override reads its master's), may hold a record twice, and stay right after edits. Step 5 (cleaning) landed 2026-10-08. `crates/xedit-analysis/src/filter.rs` is the navigation tree of the main form (`ReInitTree`, `vstNavInitNode` and `vstNavInitChildren` built at once, a child group under its record and its own node hidden, `vstNavCompareNodes` for the default FormID column with the top groups by the names of their records, `InheritStateFromChildren`) with `mniNavFilterApplyClick` for the options cleaning needs (the conflict sets and "conflict status inherited by parent"; `FilterOptions::for_cleaning` is `mniNavFilterForCleaningClick`; step 4 adds the other options to `FilterOptions` and `check_filter_node`, `apply_filter` refuses what it can not apply yet). `crates/xedit-analysis/src/cleaning.rs` ports `mniNavRemoveIdenticalToMasterClick` (the walk from the last node, groups left empty removed and counted, `ctConflictBenign` navigation meshes, injected records kept), `mniNavUndeleteAndDisableReferencesClick` (with the `UDRSet*` defaults per game), `mniNavCleanupInjectedClick`, the quick clean pass of `tmrGeneratorTimer` and the message of `mniNavCleaningObsoleteClick`. Core: `SetIsDeleted(False)` with the move of a reference to the cell of its master (`TwbContainedInElement.DoAfterSet`), `SetIsInitiallyDisabled`, `GetPosition`, `TwbGroupRecord.Remove`, `GetInjectionSourceFiles`, `GetReferencesInjected` and `RemoveInjected`, the `wbFillPNAM` fill of `ProcessDIAL` and `TwbMainRecord.Init`. Commands: `files.clean` (`xedit clean --itm --udr --quick --dry-run --output --no-backup`; the quick mode is `-quickautoclean`: UDR, ITM, save, again while a pass changed the plugin, the third pass not saved; `clean_file` and `quick_auto_clean` are the API for step 9) and `records.cleanup_injected` (`xedit records cleanup-injected`). `xedit clean --quick` loads with the settings `xeInit.pas` gives the quick clean modes (`commands::set_quick_clean_on_load`: `wbSimpleRecords` off, `wbFillPNAM` where the game sorts responses, `wbFillINOM` and `wbFillINOA` off, `wbAllowInternalEdit`, `wbRemoveOffsetData`); the global `--fill-pnam` is `-FillPNAM`. Oracle: `cargo xtask parity clean` (`crates/xtask/src/parity/clean.rs`) runs the GUI's own quick auto clean mode (`-quickautoclean -autoexit -autoload <plugin>` on private copies, `GuiRun::run_quick_clean`, a settings file that skips the 64-bit question, the developer message and the tip) on every corpus plugin that has masters, caches the saved plugin and the message log, and compares the saved bytes and, per pass, the filter's node counts, the UDR and ITM counts and the record lines of the log (`Removing:`, `Undeleting:`, `Skipping:`). Results, 2026-10-08: `cargo xtask parity clean` over the 238 corpus plugins with masters of 11 games: 231 `equal` (saved bytes, every pass's counts and every cleaned record line), 4 `different`, 1 `oracle-failed`, 2 `oracle-unsupported` (the Morrowind masters, which the 4.1.5q GUI does not edit). By game: Fallout 4 179 of 179 equal (31 saved; 554 references undeleted, 843 records and groups removed, 13 navigation meshes reported), Skyrim SE 9 of 9 (198 UDR, 1,749 ITM), Skyrim LE 4 of 4 (104, 1,296), Skyrim VR 5 of 5 (192, 1,453), Oblivion 10 of 10 (574, 330), New Vegas 9 of 9 (67, 991), Starfield 13 of 13 (nothing cleaned: the official modules are not editable), Fallout 3 2 of 5, Fallout 4 VR 0 of 1, Fallout 76 0 of 1. In all 235 compared plugins the counts of every pass and the records the log names are the oracle's; the 4 `different` differ only in the bytes of records the clean did not touch: `Anchorage.esm`, `BrokenSteel.esm` and `ThePitt.esm` (2 to 3 responses of the `GREETING` topic whose response text the port trims, `wbDialogueTextAfterLoad`, where the oracle writes them as loaded) and `Fallout4_VR.esm` (the oracle rebuilds its `DOBJ`, dropping the unused entries, where the port writes it as loaded; what builds it in the oracle is not found). `NW.esm` (Fallout 76): the GUI cleans and saves it, but its log file holds only the closing line, so the counts can not be read. The gate found and fixed in the core: a record marked modified whose storage is still valid writes its loaded data (`dcfStorageInvalid` of a main record; `MarkModifiedRecursive` builds before it marks), compressed again when compressed; `TwbSubRecord` (`srsSorted`, `srsSortInvalid`) and `TwbArray` (`arrSorted`, `arrSortInvalid`) sort a sorted array at `DoInit(True)` (an element read by index, a save, a rebuild of the data); `ReverseElements` neither modifies nor invalidates (`wbRPLDAfterLoad`); a build for the names alone (`mrsQuickInit`) stops before the record's fixes and the offsets of a worldspace; a master update (`MastersUpdated`) sorts no array and ends with the data of the record merged from its elements in their order (an approximation of `CollapseStorage`). These also settle most of phase 3 owed item (1): `parity oracle-edit` 3 of 4 as before, but `fo4-dlcworkshop01-masters` now differs only in the order of three responses of one topic group (the `INFO` sort of a type 7 group after its FormIDs change), where the 30 `MSWP` and 1 `RACE` with unchanged sorted arrays differed before. After the step: `cargo xtask parity roundtrip` 239 `equal`, 10 `refused`, 0 in every other class; `parity conflicts` Skyrim SE (`HearthFires.esm` with its masters) and New Vegas (11 files) equal; `parity dump` `Update.esm` and `Dawnguard.esm` (Skyrim SE) equal. Owed from step 5: the response text of the `GREETING` responses in three Fallout 3 DLCs and the `DOBJ` of `Fallout4_VR.esm` (above); the counts of `NW.esm` (the log of a Fallout 76 session); `wbAllowMakePartial` (`MakePartialForm`, partial forms of cells and worldspaces with children); the UDR options of the Options dialog as parameters (the defaults are used); the LOOT dirty information list (`LOOTPluginInfos`, `mniNavLOManagersDirtyInfoClick`); the other filter options (step 4); the move of a cell between worldspaces in `TwbContainedInElement.DoAfterSet`; `TwbMainRecord.Delete`; the `INFO` sort of a topic group after a master update; the tool modes and switches (`-quickautoclean`, `-qac`, `-quickclean`, `-checkforitm`, `-checkfordr`, step 9, through `clean::clean_file` and `clean::quick_auto_clean`). The GUI oracle runs hidden (`hidden.rs`), the quick clean run included. Step 6 (mod groups) landed 2026-10-08. `Core/wbModGroups.pas` is ported in the new crate `xedit-loadorder` (`mod_groups.rs`: the items with their flags and CRC32 lists as `mgiLoad` reads them, `mgCheckValid` with the load order and block checks and every message, the files of the modules and the program's own `<AppName><ToolName>.modgroups`, `wbModGroupsByName`, `Activate`, `ShowValidationMessages`, the CRC32 flags and `mgUpdateCRC` with `mgSaveToFile`), with the part of `wbLoadOrder.pas` it reads (`load_order.rs`: the modules of the data folder and of the loaded files, `wbModuleByName`, `GetCRC32`) and the Delphi `TMemIniFile`, `TStringList` file and `CommaText` behaviour the files are read and written with (`ini_files.rs`). The GUI's handling is session commands in `crates/xedit-session/src/modgroups.rs`: `modgroups.list`, `modgroups.show`, `modgroups.select` (`mniViewModGroupsReloadClick` with `LoadModGroupsSelection` and `SaveModGroupsSelection`: the `[ModGroups] Selection` of xEdit's settings file, `xeSettingsFileName`), `modgroups.create`, `modgroups.edit` (with the CRC32 question of the edit dialog), `modgroups.delete` and `modgroups.update_crcs` (`mniNavUpdateCRCModGroupsClick` with its two questions), the mutating ones with `--dry-run`, the `--edit` gate and the reload that ends them in the GUI; `conflicts.list` and `records.compare` take `mod_groups`, `all_mod_groups` and `saved_mod_groups` (`xedit conflicts --modgroups NAME --all-modgroups --saved-modgroups`) and fill the step 2 `ModGroupFilter` hook (the mod groups of `NodeDatasForMainRecord`); the global options `--modgroups-file` and `--settings` name the program's mod group file and the settings file. Oracle: `cargo xtask parity modgroups` (`crates/xtask/src/parity/modgroups.rs`, `crates/xtask/oracle/modgroups.pas`, scenarios in `crates/xtask/oracle/modgroups/*.json`). The GUI's script mode never activates mod groups (`tmScript` is one of `wbAutoModes`), so the script clicks the main form's menu items (`frmMain.FindComponent(...).Click`), and the harness answers the dialogs each scenario lists (`gui.rs` gained files placed in the run folder, the answers with key presses posted to a dialog's focused control and button clicks, a system dialog's buttons, and the main form's log, which the 4.1.5q memo returns in the ANSI code page: the `Fatal:` and aborted-script checks of the harness never matched it before); the port runs the scenario's commands as a batch and `conflicts.list` with the saved selection; the conflict status of every record, every mod group file and the settings file after the run (byte for byte) and the validation messages of the reload are compared. Results 2026-10-08: 9 of 9 scenarios equal, on Skyrim SE (the five masters, 1,178,665 records, 19,331 listed record by record, in 8 scenarios) and Fallout 4 (five masters, 1,910,960 records, 2,812 listed): activation with chained sources and targets, `@` `#` `-`, optional items of missing modules, blocks, the program's own file (454 records hidden), CRC32 conditions matching, stale, optional, forbidden and unparseable (218 hidden, 16 message lines), load order errors, `}` targets that load after their source, duplicate section names and odd lines (236 hidden, 11 lines), edit with the CRC32 question (51 hidden), delete of one group in a UTF-8 file and of every group across files, and the CRC32 update with both questions Yes and with the first No (265 hidden each); 29 files and 34 message lines equal. The check found and the port follows: Delphi's `Split` keeps an empty part after the last separator (`File.esp:` is a CRC32 list that matches nothing), `TMemIniFile` drops a section whose name it had before (in any case) with its lines, MessageDlg is a system dialog (`#32770`). After the step: `parity conflicts --game sse` equal (no mod groups by default). Parity #36 (branch `parity-36`, draft PR #58) closed the step's owed items: the view's create, the rename and the item edit of the edit dialog have oracle scenarios now (the record whose loaded override chain the view's "Create ModGroup" takes is shown with `JumpTo`, the module selections answer Enter, the item edit deletes the first item with the Delete key and its question), the selection presets of the selection dialog are `modgroups.presets`, `modgroups.preset_load`, `modgroups.preset_save` and `modgroups.preset_delete` (the section `frmModGroupSelect_ActiveModGroups_Presets`, `<Last>`, the questions as `confirm` parameters, `preset_differs`), and the modules that are not loaded order as upstream's `_ModulesLoadOrderCompare` does. Owed from step 6 still: the Creation Club order (the game's `.ccc` file is not read; #37 owns the plugin list) and the element hidden state of the comparison (parity #34). For steps 4 and 5: upstream's "Filter for cleaning", "Filter for only one", "Remove Identical To Master" and "Undelete and Disable References" disable mod groups or refuse to run with them (`ModGroupsEnabled`); the port's comparisons have none unless a request asks for them. Step 8 can read the active groups with `crate::modgroups::activate`. Step 7 (localization) landed 2026-10-08. `crates/xedit-core/src/localization.rs` ports the rest of `wbLocalization.pas`: `TwbLocalizationFile` keeps its strings in table order (`Find`, `Get`, `Put`, `AddString`, `IndexToID`, `IDExists`, `NextID`, `WriteToStream`, `ExportToFile`, the `.cpoverride` code page) and `TwbLocalizationHandler` its tables sorted by name (`Clear` with `Generation`, `AddLocalization`, `LoadForFile`, `AvailableLanguages` and `AvailableLocalizationFiles` through `ContainerResourceList`, `GetValue`, `SetValue`, `AddValue` with `ReuseDup`, `GetStringsFromFile`, `NoTranslate`), behind a read lock so the parallel dump's lookups are not serialized. `TwbLStringDef.FromStringNative` writes through `SetValue` (or stores the text under `NoTranslate`), an element keeps its `Localized` state (`esLocalized`, `esNotLocalized`), and a record reads its cached names again after a `Clear` (`mrLGeneration`, `mrInvalidateNameCache`); so a localized string can now be set and copied (phase 3 owed item 3). `files.save` writes the modified tables of the plugin it saves (`SaveChanged`) to the `Strings` folder next to the plugin. `crates/xedit-session/src/localization.rs` has the commands: `localization.files`, `localization.strings`, `localization.get`, `localization.set` (the localization editor, with the memo's text as `editor_text`), `localization.export`, `localization.languages`, `localization.language` (refused with `unsaved_strings` while a table is unsaved, unless `discard`; upstream switches silently), `localization.localize` (with the translation of `TfrmLocalizePlugin`) and `localization.delocalize` (`mniNavLocalizationSwitchClick` with `GatherLStrings`), as `xedit localization ...`; the global `--language` is `-l:` and `--translate` the translate mode (`wbTranslationMode` with the hidden fields; the structure commands fail with `translate_mode`, `elements.set` edits only the elements the mode shows). Oracle: `parity oracle-edit` takes sequences with `"strings": true` (the tables of the loaded localized plugins, from the game's loose `Strings` folder or its archives, put next to the plugins on both sides; the GUI is closed after the script so that `SaveChanged` saves its modified tables, which its log names, and every table either side saves is compared), `"rename"` (a plugin loaded under another name, so an official Starfield module can be edited) and `"inputs_from"` (plugins taken from another sequence's oracle saves); the script mode has no `AddValue` or `NoTranslate`, so `crates/xtask/src/parity/strings.rs` does the localization and delocalization through `SetEditValue` (`STRINGID:00000000`, the header flag, then the texts in `GatherLStrings` order: the same `SetValue`, `AddValue` and plain writes). Results, 2026-10-08, 10 new sequences: Skyrim SE (`sse-strings-roundtrip`: a string of each table of `Dawnguard.esm` set and set back, so its three tables are written as loaded; `sse-strings-edit`, `sse-strings-delocalize`, `sse-strings-localize` on `ccBGSSSE025-AdvDSGS.esm`), Fallout 4 (`fo4-strings-edit`, `-delocalize`, `-localize` on `DLCworkshop01.esm`) and Starfield (`sf1-strings-*` on `SFBGS004.esm` loaded as `OracleLoc.esm`): 26 of 27 saved files equal, 9 of 10 plugins and 17 of 17 string tables (sets in place, new strings with the next ID, strings of copied records, the three new tables of a localization with 394, 73 and 10 strings for the Skyrim plugin, 556, 56 and 2 for Fallout 4, 103, 3 and 512 for Starfield). The round trip writes `Dawnguard.esm`'s tables in 119,960, 121,100 and 303,019 bytes where the game's are 100,391, 116,999 and 297,716 (the game's tables share the text of equal strings, upstream writes it once per ID): the oracle writes the same bytes. The one difference was `sf1-strings-delocalize`: one response text (`INFO FE0001CB`) keeps a trailing space in the port and loses it in the oracle's emulation, because the script clears the localized flag before it sets the texts and `wbDialogueTextAfterSet` trims a response of a file that is not localized; the GUI's handler sets the texts while the file is still localized, as the port does, so the port follows the handler and the emulation is the limit. Fixes the check found in earlier code: a subrecord with `dfDontSave` (the `INOM` and `INOA` a topic gets in memory) writes nothing (`dcfDontSave` of `TwbSubRecord.WriteToStreamInternal`), and a modified topic group of a game that does not sort responses (Fallout 4 and later) sorts by `CompareGroupContents`, which also makes `fo4-dlcworkshop01-masters` equal (the INFO order of phase 3 owed item 1): `parity oracle-edit` 30 of 31 over all 14 sequences. After the step: `parity roundtrip` 239 `equal`, 10 `refused`, 0 in every other class; `parity dump` `Update.esm`, `Dawnguard.esm`, `DLCworkshop01.esm` equal and `DLCRobot.esm` equal-prefix; `parity conflicts` Skyrim SE (`HearthFires.esm` with its masters) equal.
