Owed from step 7: the Starfield emulation difference above; the Fallout 4 DLC tables in `<plugin> - Main.ba2` (closed by issue #32: every session loads the archives of the game inis and of `HasBSAs` as `TLoaderThread` does, `crates/xedit-session/src/resources.rs`; `parity oracle-edit` `fo4-strings-archive-edit`, `-reload`, `-unloaded` and `-dlcrobot`, with the tables only in the archives, 13 of 13 saved plugins and tables equal); `AddValue` makes empty tables for a plugin whose tables are not loaded, as upstream (a save then replaces the plugin's tables; the GUI has them loaded once it shows a name, and changing an existing string reads it first; issue #32 confirmed the replacement through the oracle's own load sequence: `fo4-strings-archive-unloaded` saves a `.DLSTRINGS` of 1 string and empty `.STRINGS` and `.ILSTRINGS` on both sides), which `copies_a_skyrim_npc_as_an_override` now avoids by loading them first (the `#n` names of the sorted array entries it reads were a port gap of the copy path, fixed in step 8); no oracle for the translation of a localization, the export, the language switch, `.cpoverride` and the translate mode's refusals (unit tests in `crates/xedit-session/tests/localization.rs`); the archive's files are listed in archive order (issue #32: `parity resources` compares the listings of the containers with the oracle's); the `-translate` switch and the executable name of the translate mode are step 9 (`commands::set_translate_on_load`). Step 8 (merged patch) landed 2026-10-09 (a checkpoint of 2026-10-08 on `p4-step8` finished; the commits are on `phase-4-analysis`). `mniNavCreateMergedPatchClick` is in `crates/xedit-analysis/src/merged_patch.rs`: `CheckGroup`, `BuildList`, `UpdateTargetList`, `UpdateOrderedTargetList` and `ListsEqual`, with a `TStringList` emulation whose sort and `Find` compare with the locale (`AnsiCompareText` through `crates/xedit-io/src/collate.rs`), because they decide which entries a patch gets. For each record with two overrides or more the handler's lists are merged: the leveled lists, containers, faction relations, the hairs, eyes and effects of a race, the form lists (a list whose editor ID ends in `OrderedList` only takes appends, `UpdateOrderedTargetList`, and a faulty one is reported and the record left out), the `Added Quests` of a Fallout New Vegas `DIAL` and the items, factions, effects, perks and keywords of an actor and the keywords of the items and effects of the newer games. `patch.merged` (`crates/xedit-session/src/patch.rs`, CLI `xedit patch merged <name> [--dry-run] [--no-save] [--output] [--no-backup]`) makes the patch as the handler does — `AddNewFile` through the new `files.new` command (core `wb_new_file` and `TwbFile.CreateNew` in `implementation/new_file.rs`), then every loaded file as a master, the merge, and `CleanMasters` — and reports what it merged. `cargo xtask parity merged` (`crates/xtask/src/parity/merged.rs`, `oracle/merged.pas`, scenarios in `crates/xtask/oracle/merged/*.json`) makes the plugins of a scenario with the port first, has the GUI create its merged patch with the same files — clicking the menu items that activate mod groups and answering the handler's warning and file-name dialogs (`Answer.text` fills the `TInputQueryForm`) — and compares the patches byte for byte and the lines the handler logs for faulty ordered lists. Results 2026-10-09: 12 of 12 scenarios equal: Fallout New Vegas vanilla (71 records merged) and with two plugins the port makes (74), Skyrim SE with those plugins (3) and with a mod group active (the same patch, 933 bytes), the ordered form lists (`sse-orderedlist`: the list merged with 4 entries where the winning override has 3, and the faulty one reported and left out), Fallout 4 (3), Oblivion (2), and the vanilla loads of the games with a header-only patch (the mod groups of step 6 change nothing, as upstream: the handler reads `Overrides`). The gate found and the port follows: `LoadOrderFileIDtoFileFileID` takes the last of two masters with one slot (the merged patch lists the game master twice, an `UPSTREAM-QUIRK`), `TwbSubRecord.GetDisplayName` no longer initialises the subrecord (it sorted the VMAD properties of the source record), `wbGameName2` set in `apply_edit_settings`, and the handler's messages carry the GUI's short record names (`wbDisplayShorterNames`, which the main form sets at its creation, is set around the merge: the faulty-list message named the record in the long form where the GUI writes `DraugrWeaponsOrderedList [FLST:05000801]`). The step also finishes the name suffixes of array entries (the item step 7 left owed): the port numbered the members of a sub-record array eagerly at load and after every assignment, where upstream numbers them lazily in `TwbSubRecordArray.UpdateNameSuffixes` (`TwbElement.GetNameSuffix` asks the container first) and not at all while the array is sorted, so the editor shows no `#n` on the entries of a sorted array; the port does the same now (`ElementBase::display_name`, `SubRecordArrayImpl::update_name_suffixes` with `arcSorted` and `arcNameGen`, which keeps a dump from numbering a large array twice), and `copies_a_skyrim_npc_as_an_override` passes. The handover's "a copy as new needs `deep`" is not a copy gap: `records.copy` copies a record's contents without `deep` (`deep` takes the child group of a `CELL`, `DIAL`, `WRLD` or `QUST` record along); the empty copy of the ordered-list scenario is `wbFLSTEDIDAfterSet`, which drops the `FormIDs` of an FLST whose editor ID starts or stops ending in `OrderedList` — in the oracle as well, which the new edit sequence `sse-flst-orderedlist` shows: the entries dropped by the copy with the suffix, a list built again afterwards, a copy without the suffix that keeps its entries, and an editor ID change that drops them again, saved byte-identically by both. Regression after the step: `parity dump` spot check of 14 files of 5 games (`Skyrim.esm` SSE, `Update.esm`, `Dawnguard.esm`, `Dragonborn.esm`, `HearthFires.esm`, `Fallout4.esm`, `DLCRobot.esm`, `DLCCoast.esm`, `DLCworkshop01.esm`, `Knights.esp`, `DLCShiveringIsles.esp`, `Anchorage.esm`, `DeadMoney.esm`, `CaravanPack.esm`) all equal (2 equal-prefix, the Fallout 4 `INFO` alias crash of the oracle); `parity roundtrip` 239 `equal`, 10 `refused`, 0 in every other class; `parity oracle-edit` 31 of 32 saved files over 15 sequences (the one difference the known `sf1-strings-delocalize` trim); `cargo test --workspace` and clippy pass. Owed from step 8: the copy features the handler shares with the GUI's copy commands (`wbCanOverwrite` with a deep copy, `MakePartialForm`, `IwbMultipleElements` and template elements as sources, the aligned arrays; phase 3 owed item 4); a merge scenario on Fallout 3, Fallout 76 or Starfield (the same code; Fallout New Vegas, Skyrim SE, Fallout 4 and Oblivion are covered); the tool mode and switches of the merged patch (step 9). Step 3 (error checks) landed 2026-10-09. `crates/xedit-analysis/src/check.rs` ports `CheckForErrors`, `CheckForErrorsLinear` and the `mniNavCheckForErrorsClick` walk with every `Check` callback of the definitions (`Element::get_check` of the main record, subrecord, subrecord array and struct and value, in `crates/xedit-core/src/implementation/check.rs` and the element files): the main records are checked on worker threads in batches of 512, `LastRecord`, `ErrorsCount` and `CheckedCount` are kept, a topic children group is walked only after its topic was built, each record is built twice as Delphi resets it when `GetCheck` releases its last `IwbContainerElementRef`, and the messages a build logs are captured per record (`misc::capture_progress`). xDump's `-check` is `dump_check` (`xedit dump --check`) and the command `files.check` (`crates/xedit-session/src/check.rs`, request `files`, `records`, `record_file`, `last`) backs `xedit check` (text = the oracle's log lines, `--json` = records and errors); it loads as the `-CheckForErrors` mode does (`commands::set_check_on_load`: the checks run, the new global `wbBuildRefs` off, `wbAllowInternalEdit` off with `wbEditAllowed` on during the load and the check). The harness `cargo xtask parity check` runs the GUI's own `-CheckForErrors <plugin>` mode (`GuiRun::run_check_for_errors`, hidden) on every vanilla plugin of the 11 games and compares the message log lines from `Start:` to `Done:` (the record's line, the `    <path> -> <error>` of each error, `Above errors were found in:` after the elements of a container with errors), the "Processed Records" and "Errors found" counts and the exit code; `equal-log-loss` is an oracle log that lost a run of lines but whose kept lines, in order, and counts and exit code are equal, `oracle-unsupported` is Morrowind (the GUI runs it in view mode only). `cargo xtask parity check-dump` compares `xDump -check` with `xedit dump --check` over the same corpus, an oracle run that ended in an error comparing as its prefix (`equal-prefix`). A game master as the target has the GUI check the hardcoded file (`[00] <game>.exe`), so the harness runs the port with `check --last` and the master's own content is covered by `check-dump`. Core fixes the check found: `TwbMainRecord.GetPath` is the signature; `TwbValue.Init` runs the `AfterLoad` of the resolved definition (plain values never ran theirs; `wbScrollCastAfterLoad`); the sorted array flags (`arcSortInvalid` and the rest) are cleared only after the sort (`edit::sort_if_invalid`: a reader on another thread saw unsorted entries, which made parallel output depend on timing); `wbQUSTEventToStr` reads `ReferencedBy` under `wbBuildRefs`; flags of `wbFlagsAsArray` found by name in paths (`...\ACBS\Flags\Female` of the Starfield face dials); `TwbValueBase.GetName` without the space before the suffix of an unnamed value; `Contained subrecords:` after `Errors were found in:` (the release is built with `DBGSUBREC`); `SetReferencesInjected` and the cached `GetReferencesInjected` (step 1 owed item; `records.cleanup_injected` builds the index first). Results, 2026-10-09, with the final code: `parity check` over the 5,852,286 records of the 249 vanilla plugins of the 11 games: 245 `equal`, 1 `equal-log-loss` (`fo76/NW.esm`, the oracle's log lost 2,365 of its 87,414 lines in one run, its 171,206 records and 12,395 with errors and every line it kept equal), 3 `oracle-unsupported` (the Morrowind masters), 0 `different`, 0 failed (143,951 records with errors, 260,691 error lines). `parity check-dump` over the corpus: 246 of the 246 files the oracle dumps equal (229 `equal` and 17 as prefixes where xDump crashes on the file: `Fallout4.esm` of Fallout 4 and of Fallout 4 VR, `DLCCoast.esm`, `DLCNukaWorld.esm`, `DLCRobot.esm`, `DLCworkshop03.esm` and 5 CC ESLs, `fo76/SeventySix.esm`, and `Starfield.esm`, `ShatteredSpace.esm`, `SFBGS003.esm`, `SFBGS050.esm` and `SFBGS00D.esm`), 0 `different`; the 3 Morrowind masters have no oracle dump at all (xDump fails on them, issue #9); in the whole-corpus run `sf1/Starfield.esm` hit the harness's per-process memory cap while other sessions of the machine ran their own parity jobs, and it is `equal-prefix` when run alone. The Fallout 76 run found that `wbDefinitionsFO76` defines `wbLGDIFiltersToStr` a second time, with the same name and signature as the one of `wbDefinitionsCommon` and an older body, which the FO76 definitions call; the transpiler keeps one routine per name and signature, so the port answered Fallout 76's `LGDI` filter checks with the common copy, the only difference of the oracle's whole `SeventySix.esm` prefix (`LGDI:005A3641`: the oracle wrote `00529A14`, the port the common copy's warning); `wbLGDIFiltersToStr` answers the Fallout 76 case from the game mode now, with a port of the FO76 body, and `parity check-dump --game fo76` is 2 of 2 equal. Regressions with the final code: `parity roundtrip` 239 `equal`, 10 `refused`, 0 in every other class of the 249 plugins (the worldspace, header and FormID fixes of phase 3 hold); `parity oracle-edit` 30 of 31 saved files equal over the 14 sequences (the one difference is step 7's `sf1-strings-delocalize` emulation limit, not this step's; the 4 sequences of phase 3 are equal, `fo4-dlcworkshop01-masters` included); `parity conflicts` Skyrim SE (11 files, 1,188,933 records), Fallout 4 (181 files, 2,248,005) and New Vegas (11 files, 629,839) 3 of 3 games equal; `parity clean` Skyrim SE and New Vegas 18 of 18 plugins equal (every pass's counts and every cleaned record line); `parity dump` `Dawnguard.esm` equal. `cargo fmt`, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo test --workspace` (294 tests of 32 binaries, 0 failed) and `cargo xtask check` clean. Owed from step 3, closed by issue #40: the transpiler keeps routines of one name and signature in different units apart (a call or a callback is resolved by the unit it is written in, the unit's own copy first and then the copy of the unit it uses last), so Fallout 76's own `wbLGDIFiltersToStr`, Fallout 3's and New Vegas's own `wbSOUNAfterLoad` and Starfield's own `wbOBND` are routed to their own ports (`wb_lgdi_filters_to_str_fo76`, `wb_soun_after_load_fo3`, `wb_soun_after_load_fnv`, `wb_obnd_sf1`) instead of the `wbDefinitionsCommon` copies of the names, checked by the `probes/soun-after-load.pas` fixtures of both games against the released oracle; the Fallout 76 check-dump of `SeventySix.esm` compares only the prefix the oracle writes before it crashes (xDump's INFO alias conditions). Step 9 (tool modes) landed 2026-10-09. `crates/xedit-core/src/command_line.rs` ports `wbCommandLine.pas` (the four overloads of `wbFindCmdLineParam` with Delphi's 1-based `ParamStr` indexes, `SwitchChars`, `FindCmdLineSwitch`), `crates/xedit-session/src/tool_modes.rs` the mode selection of `xeInit.pas` (`DetectAppMode` with the executable names and the switches, `isMode`, `CheckForcedMode` with `wbForcedModes` and the `.FNVpas` script extensions, `wbAlwaysMode`, `wbAutoModes` and `wbPluginModes` with the games' own mode lists, the settings each mode loads with in `apply_on_load`) and the modes that act (`SetAllToMaster`, `RestorePluginsFromMaster`, `UpdateAllOnam`, the sort and clean masters loop, `GenerateSEQFileForFile` and the check modes with the `Counting`/`Counted` lines of their log and their exit code), and `crates/xedit-session/src/export.rs` xDump's `-export` (the sorted profile list of AddProfile/FindProfile/MarkProfile/LockProfile and the five passes of the definition walk, with the definitions built as xDump builds them). The commands are `tool.modes` and `tool.run` (`xedit tool modes`, `xedit tool run <mode> [<plugin>...] [--dry-run] [--output] [--format RAW|UESPWIKI] [--seq-path]`); the modes that save act over the loaded files, save every plugin they changed, each to its own path, and skip the files that are not editable, as the GUI's `SaveChanged` at the end of an auto mode does, and the modes of other steps point at their commands (`checkforerrors` is `files.check --last`, the quick clean modes are `files.clean --quick`, `dump` is `xedit dump`, and `lodgen` and `script` are accepted and run nothing, phases 5 and 6). The command line of xEdit itself is read before the CLI's own arguments: `parse_legacy` accepts the switches a mod manager passes (`-quickautoclean -qac -quickclean -checkforitm -checkfordr -CheckForErrors -translate -setesm -clearesm -masterupdate -masterrestore -onamupdate -sortandclean -generateseq: -export -lodgen -script: -D: -P: -l: -O: -R: -autoexit -autoload -IKnowWhatImDoing` and the rest), the executable names (`SSEEditQuickAutoClean.exe`) and the game switches, loads the plugins of `-P:` or the plugin named as a parameter from `-D:`, runs the mode, prints its message log and exits with the count of the check modes, at most 127 (`xedit -TES4 -D:<data> -checkforitm Knights.esp` exits 73, the records it counted). UPSTREAM-QUIRK: the auto mode dispatch of 4.1.5q has no branch for `tmESMify`, so `-setesm` changes nothing (its load only sets `wbMasterUpdateFilterONAM`); the port follows the release and says so in the result, and `files flags --esm true` (`TwbFile.SetIsESM`) sets the flag. UPSTREAM-QUIRK: the release refuses the switch `-sortandcleanmasters` (its mode selection compares the whole name with `sortandclean`); the port accepts both. Oracle: `cargo xtask parity tool-modes` has the GUI run its own mode on a private copy of the plugin and its masters (with `-autoload`, the executable of Fallout 3 and New Vegas beside them, which the release's plugin list loader opens, and an ESM-flagged `.esp` for `-clearesm`, which the port builds) and compares every plugin the mode saved byte for byte, the lines of the message log the mode writes and the exit code of the check modes; a run ends when the log says the mode is done (`--= All Done =--`) even if the GUI then waits, and a save the release queued to its shutdown is read from its `<name>.save.<timestamp>`. Results, 2026-10-09, `cargo xtask parity tool-modes --game tes4 --game fnv --game tes5 --game fo4` (one plugin per game by default: the largest vanilla plugin that has masters and is at most 16 MiB, the game master excluded): 24 of the 31 cases `equal`, 4 `known-difference` (the export of each game), 3 `oracle-unsupported` (`-clearesm` where the plugin of the game is an `.esm`), 0 `different` and 0 failed. Every mode the release offers for a game is equal: Oblivion `Knights.esp` (setesm, clearesm on an ESM-flagged `.esp` of the plugin that the port builds, generateseq, checkforitm, checkfordr, sortandcleanmasters; masterupdate, masterrestore and onamupdate are not in the release's ToolModes of Oblivion and are skipped), Fallout New Vegas `OldWorldBlues.esm` (setesm, masterupdate, masterrestore, generateseq, checkforitm, checkfordr, sortandcleanmasters), Skyrim LE `HearthFires.esm` (setesm, onamupdate, whose own save of `HearthFires.esm` is compared byte for byte, generateseq, whose sequence file is compared, checkforitm, checkfordr, sortandcleanmasters) and Fallout 4 `DLCRobot.esm` (setesm, generateseq, checkforitm, checkfordr, sortandcleanmasters). The check found and the port follows: the sequence file of `-generateseq` holds the FormIDs in the order the QUST group of the file holds its records, which is the order of the file and not the FormID order of `records()` (`HearthFires.esm` keeps its quests in descending FormID order); a run of the GUI that the release ends by itself is a finished mode, whatever the harness waited for. The harness runs the GUI with `-autoload` (its module selection answers itself), copies the executable of Fallout 3 and New Vegas into the private data folder (the release's plugin list loader opens `<Data>\<game>.exe` there), and ends a run at the mode's own `--= All Done =--` line, reading a save the release queued for renaming from its `<name>.save.<timestamp>`. Regressions with the final code: `parity roundtrip` 239 `equal`, 10 `refused`, 0 in every other class of the 249 plugins; `parity dump` 8 of 8 spot-checked files equal (`Update.esm` of Skyrim SE, Skyrim VR and Skyrim LE, `Dawnguard.esm`, `DLCworkshop01.esm`, `DeadMoney.esm`). `cargo fmt`, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo test --workspace` and `cargo xtask check` clean. Owed from step 9: the export's definition profile differed from the oracle's in the type name of one definition (`Group=` in the oracle, `Group=TwbEnumDef` in the port): batch #37 fixed it (the bare `inherited` of `TwbEnumDef.GetDefTypeName` leaves an empty result for a name list without names), together with the CRLF the release's `WriteLn` writes and the profile `CheckPass` clears before `ExportElement`; the Fallout 4 export text now has the oracle's exact byte size and the same lines in another order, which the check still reports as `known-difference`; the harness's run of the modes needs the mode's own `--= All Done =--` line because the release can wait after its saves on the hidden desktop for a rename it queued (a `different` that follows from an oracle run that never reached its saves would be read as a port difference); the legacy switches the port reads and does not act on are named on stderr with a reason each (the switches the release itself does nothing with, the GUI-only ones, and the unported `-moreunknown`, `-devmode`, `-speed`/`-memory` and `-moprofile`); and `-setesm` changes nothing, as the release does. Step 4 (filters) landed 2026-10-09; finished 2026-10-10 with the fix of the reachable walk's collector drain (below). `crates/xedit-analysis/src/filter.rs` holds the whole filter of the navigation tree on top of the tree of step 5: `FilterOptions` with every field of the filter dialog (`TfrmFilterOptions`) and `apply_filter` as `mniNavFilterApplyClick` with `FilterPreset` — the prelude (the sets that hold every status are turned off, `Signatures` and `BaseSignatures` as sorted comma texts, a base editor ID of 8 or 9 characters read as the FormID of a base record instead, `FilterScaledActors` and `FilterByHasVWDMesh` narrowing the base signatures, the reference signatures derived from them (`recBuildReferences`), `TopLevelGroups` with the `CELL`, `WRLD`, `DIAL` and `QUST` additions and `PotentiallyUnfilteredRefs`, `FilterRequiresBaseRecord`, `FilterRequiresReference`, `FilterRequiresMainRecord` and `FilterByStatus`) and `CheckFilterNode` with `CheckContainerForElementValue` (the values of the record and of every element below it, depth first) and `CheckValueRegex` (a case-insensitive multi-line regular expression, which `regex` runs where upstream uses `TPerlRegEx`); the conflict sets and `FilterConflictOnly` with `ctIdenticalToMasterWinsConflict, ctConflictWins, ctConflictLoses` as the "Filter Conflicts" preset, `FilterOnlyOne`, the inject, not-reachable (`ReachableBuild`) and references-injected states, the editor ID, name and element value texts, the base editor ID and base name, the scaled actors (`XSCL` missing or 1), the signatures and base signatures, the persistence options (`FilterPersistent`, `IsUnnecessaryPersistent` with the `LCTN` loop that upstream leaves dead, `IsMasterTemporary`, `IsPositionChanged` with the `DATA` sort keys of the record and its master), the deleted and visible-when-distant flags, the visible-when-distant mesh (`GetHasVisibleWhenDistantMesh`, the `_far.nif` or the `TREE` billboard in a loaded container) and the precombined mesh (`GetPrecombinedMesh`), the pruning of the top level groups, the flattening of the blocks and cell children (`MoveTo(Node, Node, amInsertBefore, True)` then `DeleteNode`, the children take the place of the group) and `AssignPersWrldChild` (the persistent references of a worldspace move into the cell they stand in, first child first, and the walk goes on at the first moved node as upstream's `NextNode := Node2` does); the second pass walks what is left, `CheckFilterNode(True)` deletes the childless nodes it filters out and `FileFiltered[i] := RecordCount - MainRecordCount` is reported per file (a file with no record left reports nothing, as upstream). `crates/xedit-session/src/filter.rs` ports the presets of `xeFilterOptionsForm`: `FilterListPresets` (the `[Filter <name>]` sections of xEdit's settings file, `xeSettingsFileName`), `FilterLoadPreset`, `FilterSavePreset` with `FilterHasPresetChanged` (the question the dialog asks on close, which the response's `changed` answers) and `btnFilterDelClick`'s erase; `TMemIniFile.ReadBool` is ported in `xedit-loadorder/src/ini_files.rs`. `crates/xedit-core/src/implementation/reachable.rs` ports "Build Reachable Info" (`mniNavBuildReachableClick`): `TwbFile.BuildReachable` with the file header record, the hardcoded records, the groups a game always loads (ADDN to SKIL) and the per-game cases of CLAS, DIAL, EYES, HDPT, PERK, NPC_, QUST and RACE, `TwbElement`, `TwbContainer`, `TwbMainRecord` (with its collector as a thread local and the SMBN, FURN, NPC_ and QUST reach-back cases) and `TwbGroupRecord`'s `Reached`, `ResetReachable` of all of them, the `esReachable` and `esNotReachable` element states (the `SetContainer` inheritance among them), and `GetIsReachable` and `GetIsNotReachable` of a main record over its master and its overrides; `ReachableBuild` is the session flag `filter.apply` reads. Commands: `filter.apply` (`xedit filter apply`, the options of the dialog as parameters or a `[Filter <name>]` preset, the files of the tree, and the mod groups `ModGroupsEnabled` activates — the report holds the two passes, the nodes left, the records the filter took out of each file with the line xEdit logs for a partial count, and optionally the names of the records the tree keeps), `filter.remove` (`xedit filter remove`, `mniNavFilterRemoveClick`), `filter.presets`, `filter.preset.save|delete` (`xedit filter presets|save|delete`) and `refs.build_reachable` (`xedit refs build-reachable`, `mniNavBuildReachableClick`, which builds the references of the files that have none as `BuildAllRef` does); the flags the dialog has no checkbox for are `conflict_only`, `only_one` and `no_game_master`. The view filter of the view tab (`ApplyViewFilter` and `IsViewNodeFiltered`, whose column loops start at the second column, so a view of one record matches neither the name nor a value) is the `view_filter_name`, `view_filter_value`, `view_filter_or` and `keep_children`, `keep_siblings` and `keep_parents_siblings` parameters of `records.compare` (`xedit compare`), and the referenced-by filter (`ApplyReferencedByFilter`) is the `filter_name`, `filter_signature`, `filter_file` and `filter_or` parameters of `refs.get` (`xedit refs get`). Oracle: `cargo xtask parity filter` (`crates/xtask/src/parity/filter.rs`, `crates/xtask/oracle/filter.pas`, scenarios in `crates/xtask/oracle/filters/*.json`). The GUI's script mode has the filter variables of the main form and the `ApplyFilter` function of the script host (what the shipped "Apply filter for cleaning.pas" uses; `FilterConflictOnly`, `FilterOnlyOne` and `FilterNoGameMaster` are not exposed there, so the three flags have no oracle), so the harness generates one block per configuration of a scenario that sets every variable (the ones a configuration leaves out at the dialog's defaults, because the variables keep their values between two filters) and applies the filter, and reads the counts the GUI writes to its message log: the two passes and the nodes left from the closing `Done: Applying Filter, ...` line and the `[file] Filtered n of m records` lines of the partly filtered files. The port runs the same configurations as one `xedit batch` from a folder of hard links (the GUI's private data folder: no archives, no strings files, so a localized string shows `<Error: No strings file for lstring ID ...>` on both sides), and a scenario that filters by the reachable status clicks `mniNavBuildReachable` in the GUI first, which the port's `refs.build_reachable` mirrors. Results, 2026-10-09 (the reachable scenario re-run 2026-10-10): `--file sse-filters` (Skyrim.esm and Update.esm, 37 configurations, 26,849,591 nodes walked in the first passes) 37 of 37 equal; `--file fo4-filters` (Fallout4.esm and DLCRobot.esm, 8 configurations) 8 of 8 equal; `--file sse-reachable` (the five Skyrim SE masters, "Build Reachable Info" and 4 configurations) 4 of 4 equal (after the drain fix below; the port's batch of the walk and the four filters takes 41.5 s where the GUI needs 116.1 s). The check found and the port follows: the walk of `AssignPersWrldChild` continues at the first moved node (without it the port deleted 18,889 nodes fewer and kept a different tree); `PotentiallyUnfilteredRefs` (not the top level groups) decides whether a base record filter clears the tree, so `--base-editor-id` and `--base-name` filter instead of emptying the tree; `IsUnnecessaryPersistent` reads the reference information, which the filter builds first (`BuildAllRef`), and its `LCTN` loop never takes an entry off the count (a main record's `LinksTo` is nil); the port loads the plugins from a folder of links as the GUI's private data folder does, so the localized strings and the archives of the game do not leak into a filter (the first run of `element-value-skyrim` kept 325,006 nodes against the oracle's 10,140). a child group of a record is the group `GroupRecord.GroupLabel` names, the fixed-up label: in Fallout 4 the label of the child groups of a record carries the file's own FileID while the record holds the FormID of the masters-updated space, so the raw label did not match and the port did not nest the child group of such a record (the one node the oracle hides in its first pass and the port did not: the empty quest children group of `QUST 0000089C` in Fallout4.esm, the difference of six configurations). The reachable walk drains the collector only in the call that owns it: a call whose `_Collector` is assigned queues into the owner's list and its own `Collector` (a local record) stays empty, so its own drain loop runs over nothing, where the port walked the shared queue again from every record it drained — the stack of the 1 GiB worker died in that recursion (the check found the record walk past depth 3,000 with the same material and impact records of Skyrim at every level), the bodies ran in another order (5,833 records of the not-reachable configurations stayed unmarked) and the walk of the five Skyrim SE masters took 7.4 hours against the 41.5 s it needs with the drain in the owning call. After the step: `cargo xtask parity roundtrip` 239 `equal`, 10 `refused`, 0 in every other class (unchanged from step 7); `parity dump` `Update.esm` and `Dawnguard.esm` (Skyrim SE) equal; `parity conflicts` `--game sse` equal (11 files, 1,188,933 records, 20,782 listed record by record); `parity clean` `DLCRobot.esm` and `DLCworkshop01.esm` equal (the counts of every pass and the saved bytes). `cargo fmt --all -- --check`, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets` (no warning) and the workspace tests are clean, and `cargo xtask check` finds the ledger and the map consistent. Owed from step 4: `FilterScripted` (the `Filter` function of a script, phase 6); the records the user hid (`IsHidden`) and the compare-to load (`IsCompareToSame`) have no state in the port; the names of placed records and cells are not the special ones xEdit builds (`wbRefrDisplayName` and its neighbours), so a filter by name and a view comparison can differ from xEdit's wherever such a name contains the text searched for (the check's texts avoid it); the file name filter of the navigation tree (`edFileNameFilter` with `cbRegExFilter`) has no command; the "* Selected" menu items and "Build Reference Info" of one file use the file selection dialogs of the GUI, which are the `files` parameter and `refs.build file` (the dialogs themselves are phase 7); the three flags `conflict_only`, `only_one` and `no_game_master` have no oracle scenario (the script host does not expose the variables); the view filter and the referenced-by filter have no oracle either (the script host exposes neither the view tab nor the referenced-by tab), so `crates/xedit-session/tests/filter.rs` and `the_view_filter_keeps_the_rows` cover them on synthetic plugins; the `filter.apply` request has no `no_game_master` oracle and no test with a game master file (the fixtures have none); a `by_persistent` filter that needs the reference index builds it even when the files had none (as `BuildAllRef` does) and reports nothing about it; the reachable walk builds the elements of every record it reaches and keeps them (upstream releases a record's elements when the last reference to it goes), so `refs.build_reachable` needs about 7 GB for the five Skyrim SE masters; after the drain fix the walk and the four filters of the check take 41.5 s.
- **Status:** Gate met 2026-10-09 (draft pull request #14, branch `phase-5-assets`): the archives `pack` writes are byte-identical to `BSArch.exe -mt:no` (`cargo xtask parity bsarch`: 1553 checks, 1553 equal, 0 different, 0 failed, 0 deferred, over the archives of the 11 games and the synthetic folders; step 1's 1400 checks with the 250 deferred texture ones became these when the textures landed), and the outputs of the asset operations equal the oracle: `cargo xtask parity nif` 792961 files, 542505 equal (dump and save), 250543 equal-error, 0 different; `cargo xtask parity sniff` 0 files written differently in every case of steps 4 and 5 that ran on the full corpus and in the sample runs of every case (the `equal-error` and `oracle-failed` files are the oracle's own refusals and crashes, below; the full-corpus per-case totals: step 4's tangents 123853, tangents-add 130384, bounds 320297, replace-assets 325003, json 339899, tweaker 60797, fixer 281561, apply-transform 34428 and then 8580, the 12 differences the RTL power-of-ten port fixed, adjust-transform 328692; step 5's wei-explosion 5, headtracking 4444, headtracking-clamp 2907, facial 8586, facial-remove 8586, jam-anim 10125, jam-anim-translation 10125, copy-controlled 8577, copy-geometry-transform 23549, group-shapes 825, copy-geometry 4926, group-shapes-split 714 and merge-shapes 21 with `--threads 1`), while the cases listed as owed below have only their samples; `cargo xtask parity lodgen` 20 cases, 20 equal, 0 different. Step results follow. Step 1 (archives) landed on `phase-5-assets` (Sonnet 5.5, 2026-10-08). It ports the rest of `wbBSArchive` (`TwbAsset`; `TwbBSArchive` reading and writing every BSA version, Morrowind and general BA2 version, with the DX10 file table; `TwbSplitPacker` and `TwbMultiSourcePacker`), LZ4 and LZ4F compression and zlib above 8 MiB (`wbCompression`, from the C libraries upstream links: lz4 1.10 and zlib give the oracle's bytes), the BSA, BA2 and xxHash hashes of `wbHash`, the resource listings and hash tables of `wbBSA` (`container_handler.rs`), the console tool of `BSArch.dpr` as the `bsarch` binary with the upstream arguments and text, and the commands `archive.list`, `archive.extract` and `archive.pack` (`xedit archive list|extract|pack`, with `--dry-run` and the `--edit` gate). The code is `crates/xedit-io/src/archive/` (`mod.rs` reading and listing, `write.rs` the creation, `packer/` the packers and the unpacking, `asset.rs`, `texture.rs`), `crates/bsarch`, `crates/xedit-session/src/archive.rs`. *Determinism.* Upstream packs on a thread pool and the data of an archive lands in the order the threads finish; the oracle is `BSArch.exe -mt:no`, which is one thread. The port reproduces that single thread's steps on any thread count: the reading, hashing and compressing of the files runs ahead on the pool (a window of files and 512 MiB), and the decisions that depend on the order (the files that share data, the split into archives from the compressed sizes, the order the data is written in, `fProcessTick mod 3` of the loading and writing steps) are made in upstream's order as the results arrive. Two quirks make `-mt:yes` and `-mt:no` differ upstream: the Morrowind list is sorted by name only without threads (the port always sorts), and the data order is a race (the port takes the single thread's). *Texture archives.* Everything that looks inside a DDS file or builds one is the extension point `archive/texture.rs` (`chunk_dds`, `pack_dds_chunks`, `pack_dds`, `unpack_dds`, each failing with `Texture (DX10) archives are not supported yet`); `create_archive` and `save` already reserve and write the DX10 file table, `pack_chunk` takes any chunk, and the file table, `-dump` and the format names of the DX10 archives read and print like the oracle's. The gate evidence is `cargo xtask parity bsarch`: for each archive of the game folders (the 363 archives of 11 games up to `--max-size` MB, 40 by default, or the ones named with `--archive`) it compares the `-dump` text of the oracle and the port, the files `unpack` writes, and the archives `pack` writes from the unpacked folder (the oracle with `-mt:no`; the port with all threads and with `-mt:no`; every compression of the source's format, and the text of the run), plus `--synthetic` for what the game archives do not cover (two source folders and an archive merged, filters, flags, `-split`, files without data, names that sort several ways, identical files, textures only, a name with accents). Result on 2026-10-08: 1400 checks, 1150 equal, 0 different, 0 failed, 250 `deferred`. The 250 are the `unpack` and `pack` of the 125 texture archives (step 2); their `-dump` is compared and equal. By format (list, unpack, pack): BSA 103 (Oblivion) 6, 6, 12 of 12; BSA 104 (Fallout 3, New Vegas, Skyrim LE) 18, 18, 32; BSA 105 (Skyrim SE) 4, 4, 8; BA2 v1 general 17, 17, 34; v2 general (Starfield) 20, 20, 60 (zlib, zlib with `-z`, LZ4); v8 general 173, 173, 346; texture archives v1, v2, v7, v8 listed only (125 of 125 equal); Morrowind has no archive small enough, so `Tribunal.bsa` (63 MB) was checked by hand and the synthetic folders cover it (4 of 4 equal); 57 synthetic packs equal. Two archives (`Fallout - Misc.bsa` of Fallout 3 and New Vegas) cannot be unpacked by the oracle (`menus\s.txt`: a file flagged compressed that is not, `LibDeflate error: Bad data`); the port stops with the same message. Spot checks outside the harness: a synthetic 1.5 GB folder packed with `-split:1` for `-sse`, `-fnv -z` and `-fo4` gives two archives each that equal the oracle's bytes; `Skyrim - Meshes1.bsa` unpacked and packed with `-sse -z` (548 MB, 14242 files, LZ4F) gives the oracle's 377 735 969 bytes in 33 s where `BSArch.exe -mt:no` needs 3 min 4 s; the raw text of a pack (progress included) equals the oracle's `-mt:no`; `cargo xtask parity dump` on `Update.esm` (Skyrim SE), `DLCRobot.esm` and `SFBGS004.esm` (which read strings from archives) is unchanged (equal, equal-prefix, equal). `BSArch.exe` hung at its start in 9 runs of the batch (no CPU time at all, `-dump` and `pack` alike); the harness stops a run that uses no CPU for two minutes and runs it again. The oracle fails on paths over 260 characters, which the port writes (the harness uses short folder names). Owed from step 1: (1) the DDS code (step 2); (2) the stream-to-stream overloads of `wbCompression` have no caller (ported by issue #32 as `compress_to`, `compress_stream` and `decompress_stream`, checked by unit tests against the buffer routes; still no caller in 4.1.5q, so no oracle); (3) `ResolveFileHash` and `ResolveFolderHash` are wired into `wbFileHashCallback` and `wbFolderHashCallback` behind `wbLoaderDone`, which only a GUI load sets (xDump never does), so no dump output changed (closed by issue #32: every session sets it after its load and builds the cache as `TLoaderThread` does; `BuildCache` walks its `TDictionary` in bucket order, which `crates/xedit-core/src/delphi_dictionary.rs` reproduces with the 2^21 buckets and the FNV-1a string hash measured on the oracle; `parity resources fnv-hashes`, ten pairs of TES4 hash collisions, equal); (4) `TList.Sort` of the BSA hash order is a quicksort upstream and a stable sort in the port, which differ only for two files with the same folder and name hash (closed by issue #32: the RTL's quick sort, `delphi_list_sort` in `archive/write.rs`; `parity bsarch --synthetic` `edge`); (5) names that hash differently in a code page other than 1252 were not checked (the port converts like `AnsiString` with the system ANSI code page; issue #32 checked names outside ASCII in and out of 1252 against the oracle, which found that the archive code lowers ASCII letters only, `LowerCase`, where the port lowered every letter, and that `BSArch.exe` prints in the ANSI code page and trims the period of an OS message, all fixed; another system code page stays unverifiable on this machine); (6) `BSArchPro` (the GUI of `BSArch/`) is the GUI phase's. Step 2 (textures) landed on `phase-5-assets` (Sonnet 5.5, 2026-10-08). `crates/xedit-io/src/dds.rs` ports `wbDDS` (the header records `DdsHeader`, `DdsHeaderDx10` and `DdsHeaderXbox` read from and written to byte slices, the `TDXGI` and `TD3DFORMAT` numbers as `Dxgi` and `D3dFormat`, the format sets, `IsDDS`, `GetDXGI`, `GetD3DFMT`, `SetUpHeader`, `GetBitsPerPixel`, `GetMipSize`, `ConvertR8G8B8toB8G8R8X8` and the rest of `TwbDDS`); `archive/texture.rs` fills the extension points of step 1: `chunk_dds` (`TwbSplitPacker.LoadFile` for a DDS: the uncompressed header chunk and the compressed mipmap chunks, the size and hash of the converted file), `pack_dds_chunks` (`TwbBSArchive.Pack` with a packer), `prepare_dds` and `store_dds` (`Pack` without one, cut and compressed on the pool, stored in file order), `unpack_dds` (the header `SetUpHeader` makes and the chunks appended). `bsarch -fo4dds` and `-sf1dds`, `xedit archive pack --format fo4dds|sf1dds` and `archive extract` of texture archives work; the texture helper of `wbBSA` (the `.ddx` name `BuildCache` adds) was ported with the unit. *Vector code.* Upstream has none; the plan's SIMD items are `xedit_io::simd` (24 to 32 bit pixel conversion on SSSE3 or AVX2, 9.0 GB/s scalar, 19 GB/s vector in a release build) and `xedit_core::half_float` (`half_to_float_slice` and `single_to_half_slice` on F16C, 1.3 and 1.4 G values/s scalar, 8.7 and 9.0 vector, with `FloatToHalf`'s truncation and its infinity for what overflows kept, and blocks that hold a NaN or an overflow left to the scalar routine). The scalar version is the definition: the unit tests compare every level on every input shape, the half-float conversion was checked on all 2^32 singles and all 2^16 halves (`cargo test -p xedit-core --release --lib -- --ignored every_float`), and `XEDIT_SIMD=off` takes the scalar route everywhere; no output depends on the CPU or the thread count (`crates/xedit-session/tests/archive.rs` packs textures with 1, 2 and 7 threads, with and without a split, to the same bytes). The half-float routines have no caller yet (the vertex arrays of the data formats go through the element tree one value at a time). *Oracle.* `cargo xtask parity bsarch` now runs the `unpack` and `pack` of the texture archives (`--textures` takes only those; `pack` uses `-fo4dds` or `-sf1dds`, each compression of the format) and `--synthetic` adds generated DDS folders (BC1 to BC7, uncompressed, half-float and 8 bit formats, odd sizes, a cube map, a header with mipmap count 0, 24 bit RGB, identical textures, data that ends early, and the files both sides refuse: noise, a short header, A8, an unknown DX10 format, an Xbox texture), each as `-z`, uncompressed, `-share:no`, split, `-split:0`, a filter, `-split:1` and `-z:lz4`. Result on 2026-10-08, the whole selection (every archive up to 40 MB of the 11 games, with `--synthetic`): 1553 checks, 1553 equal, 0 different, 0 failed, 0 deferred (1400 before, 250 of them deferred, which the 125 `unpack` and 254 `pack` checks of the texture archives replace). The 125 texture archives: v1 2, v2 4 (Starfield), v7 1, v8 118; `list`, `unpack` and `pack` (2 packs for Fallout 4, 3 for Starfield) all equal (125 `unpack` and 254 `pack` checks); 24 synthetic texture packs equal, 5 of them refused identically. Spot check of a 208 MB archive (`DLCworkshop03 - Textures.ba2`): unpack and pack equal, and packing it with `-z` takes 3.4 s where `BSArch.exe -mt:no` needs 27.4 s. *Quirks kept.* `GetD3DFMT` tests `DDPF_ALPHA`, not `DDPF_ALPHAPIXELS`, and asks `X1R5G5B5` for an alpha mask; the header `SetUpHeader` writes for `A8_UNORM` has only the alpha flag, which `GetDXGI` does not read as a format, so such a file is `Unsupported DDS format`; the enumeration is one byte, so a DX10 format number is cut to a byte. *Deviation.* A texture whose data ends before its last mipmap is cut where the data ends in both routes (upstream's route with a packer, `Copy` of a dynamic array, does that; the route without one reads past its buffer, which cannot be compared). *API for step 5 (Sniff's "Find textures" and the texture checks of "Check for errors").* The DDS file functions are free functions of `xedit_io::dds` on the bytes of the file: `is_dds`, `dxgi`, `dxgi_format_name`, `bits_per_pixel`, `bits_per_pixel_of`, `is_compressed`, `has_alpha`, `is_cube_map`, `is_xbox`, `d3dfmt`, `d3dfmt_format_name`, `D3D_NODXGI`, `DdsHeader::read` (`width`, `height`, `mip_map_count`, `pixel_format`) and `MAX_HEADER_SIZE` for reading the head of a loose file; for a file of a texture archive `ProcFileObject.file_entry` has the `dds` record (`width`, `height`, `num_mips`, `dxgi_format`, `tex_chunks[].chunk.size`), `is_cube_map()` and `dxgi_format_name()`, and `ProcFileObject.input.archive` tells the archive type (`archive_type().is_dds()`). The two processors stay `pending` in `sniff/procs.rs` and `proc_check_for_errors.rs` with messages that name step 5. Owed from step 2: (1) the Xbox 360 conversion of `BSArchPro` (`xtexconv.exe`, the GUI tool) and the Xbox archive target of the console tool, which `BSArch.dpr` does not have; (2) the half-float routines have no caller; (3) NEON versions for ARM (the kernels fall back to the scalar code there); (4) `wbHelpers.wbDDSDataToBitmap` and the other bitmap conversions belong to the GUI phase. Step 3 (NIF and materials) landed on `phase-5-assets` (Opus 5.5, 2026-10-08). The new crate `crates/xedit-assets` ports `wbDataFormat` (`data_format.rs`: the definitions `TdfDef` and their subclasses, and the element tree, which is an arena `Tree` of elements `El` whose methods are the upstream methods of `TdfElement` dispatched on the upstream class; `json.rs` builds and prints JSON as `JsonDataObjects` does, `variant.rs` the `Variant` conversions and comparisons the callbacks rely on), `wbDataFormatNifTypes` (`data_format_nif_types.rs`), `wbDataFormatNif` (`data_format_nif.rs`: the NiObject registry, `TwbNifFile` with its header maintenance and `TwbNifBlock`; `data_format_nif/geometry.rs`: vertices, normals, UVs, triangles, strips, transforms, bounds, `ApplyTransform`, `UpdateBounds`, `UpdateNormals`, `UpdateTangents`, `RemoveBranch`, `ConvertBlock`, `GetAssets`, `GetLinkArrays`, `DetectBSXFlags` and the `Spell*` that need no mesh optimizer), `wbDataFormatMaterial`, `wbDataFormatMisc`, `wbNifMath` and `wbNifScanner`; `asset.rs` puts the kinds of file behind one type. The block and type definitions are generated: `cargo xtask port-defs emit-df` (`crates/xtask/src/portdefs/df.rs`, on the Pascal reader of the record transpiler) writes every builder overload as a function of its own (upstream's `dfBool` with events drops them, which only per-overload code keeps), the 322 `wbDefine*` procedures (383 NiObject definitions), and the callbacks that stay in a small typed subset (version checks, `NativeValues` lookups and comparisons, `with nif(e) do`, assignments to the `var` parameter); it translated 832 routines of the two units, and the 28 callbacks outside the subset are ported by hand (`callbacks.rs` of each). There is no Wwise data format unit at the baseline tag: the Wwise GUID index of Starfield is part of `wbDefinitionsSF1` and was ported in phase 2. The commands are `assets.dump` (`ToText`, or `ToJSON` as Sniff's JSON converter writes it, with the decimals and the rotation form as options), `assets.blocks`, `assets.types`, `assets.scan` (`NifBlockList`, `NifTextureList`, `NifTextureListUVRange`), `assets.save`, `assets.set` and `assets.from-json`, all reading a loose file or a file in an archive (`xedit assets ...`), the writing ones with `--dry-run` and the `--edit` gate. *API for steps 4 and 6.* Sniff's processors and LODGen work on `NifFile` (`tree`, `root`) and the free functions of `data_format_nif` named after the upstream methods (`block_add_child`, `block_get_vertices`, `block_apply_transform`, `copy_block`, `convert_block`, `block_by_path`, `get_assets_list`, ...), with element access through `Tree::elements`, `native_values`, `edit_values` and their setters; a `TdfElement` method is a `Tree` method of the same name. *Oracle.* `cargo xtask parity nif` reads every archive of the corpus with the port's archive reader and checks each NIF, KF, BGSM and BGEM against `Sniff.exe` in its automation mode, which reads the archive itself: the JSON converter gives the dump, and the universal tweaker setting `NiHeader\Num Blocks`, which the save recomputes, gives a plain load and save (a material has no such element, and the tweaker writes it anyway: `GetEditValues` leaves its string result unassigned for a missing element, so the caller's variable comes back); `--text` runs the GUI's script adapter of the data format units (`crates/xtask/oracle/dataformat.pas`) for the `ToText` dumps and saves of the materials, LOD, FUZ and DDS files and of a sample of NIFs, and compares `dfCalcHash`; `--from-json` builds NIFs from JSON dumps with Sniff and with the port. Results on 2026-10-08: `parity nif` over every archive of the eleven games (Morrowind, Oblivion, Fallout 3, New Vegas, Skyrim, Skyrim SE, Skyrim VR, Fallout 4, Fallout 4 VR, Fallout 76, Starfield): 792961 files, 542505 `equal` (dump and save), 250543 `equal-error`, 0 in every other class; the `equal-error` files are the 87 Oblivion meshes of NIF versions 10.0.1.0 and 10.0.1.2 and every Fallout 76 and Starfield mesh (20.2.0.7 with user version 2 of 155, 173 or 175), which upstream refuses with `Unknown NIF version`, and the Fallout 76 materials (`Unexpected end of stream`). Files per game: 7482, 12069, 20744, 25760, 21625, 22806, 21907, 243351, 166845, 129916 and 120543. `--text`: 68892 files through the script adapter, each `equal` or refused with the same message (bgem 1141 and 4101, bgsm 19608 and 25888, nif 1911 and 287; btt 1272, dds 1460, dlodsettings 53, fuz 360, lod 12768 and lst 43 all equal), and `dfCalcHash` equal for 1596 of 1596 names. `--from-json` (the first 20 NIFs of each archive of the nine games Sniff reads): 1911 dumps, 1674 `equal`, 237 `equal-error`, 0 different; 3156 generated dumps with 9468 rotations (special angles and axes, random ones, every rotation of the Skyrim dragon) give Sniff's bytes. Fixes the oracle found: the hash by which upstream finds an element by name is `HashName` of the ANSI string with each character in lower case xored in and the sum rotated left by five (the first guess, an upper-cased hash, matched none of the 1596 names of the definitions); Sniff writes its JSON through a `TStringStream` in the system's ANSI code page, so names with bytes that are not UTF-8 show as `?` (24 Fallout 4 and 6 Skyrim meshes); Sniff's threads share state and rarely fail a file with an access violation that does not repeat when the file runs alone, which the harness now does for every such crash; text that is not valid UTF-8 is decoded as `UTF8ToString` decodes it (`MultiByteToWideChar`, one Skyrim mesh); `FromJSON` reads values with the `JsonDataObjects` semantics (`Cannot cast Array into String` for the 237 Fallout 4 dumps with a `BSConnectPoint::Children` block, whose array `Name` hides the `Name` of `NiExtraData`: neither xEdit nor the port can build those NIFs from their own dumps); Delphi's `Sin` and `Cos` on Win64 are the RTL's own, an ulp apart from the C runtime's and from the correctly rounded value at times, which flipped the sign or the last bit of matrix elements near zero in 71 NIFs built from JSON with the C runtime's (90 degree rotations among them) and in 6 with correctly rounded ones: `nif_math::sin` and `cos` are ported from the machine code of `Sniff.exe` and give the bits the GUI's script adapter returns for 20097 arguments; the harness builds the port's NIF from the ANSI text Sniff reads; `assets.from-json` reads its file as `TStringList.LoadFromFile` does (byte order mark, else ANSI). Owed from step 3: (1) `SpellOptimize`, `SpellStripify`, `SpellTriangulate` and `StripifyTriangles` need `wbMeshOptimize` (step 6, which landed it with the Sniff `Optimize mesh` and `Analyze mesh` processors); (2) `dfCalcHash` of names with characters outside ASCII differs from the oracle's (no definition has such a name; one probe of `parity nif --text`); (3) upstream's undefined memory is not reproduced: `AllocateValue` keeps stale bytes where the port has zeros, `WriteChars` of a fixed size copies a longer string past the value, `CalculateFaceNormals` writes past its array for a triangle index out of range (the port fails with the message of the tangent routines), and a function result left unassigned returns the caller's string upstream and an empty one in the port; (4) closed for the numbers by parity #39 (PR #53): `StrToFloat` is the RTL's own parse, fitted at full precision (`xedit_core::delphi::str_to_float`: the integer part accumulates in an `Int64` while it fits and the fraction digits in double precision, the scale is the paired-table `power10`, so the text of `MaxDouble` gives the double one ulp below it and the 21 digits of Pi give `0x1.921fb54442d17p+1` where Rust's correctly rounded parse gives `...d18`; `Inf`/`-Inf`/`+Inf`/`NaN` parse where `Infinity` refuses and `1e-400` is 0), the script `FloatToStrF` prints `FloatToDecimal`'s digits with the RTL's exponent padding, and `ArcTan2`/`ArcCos`/`ArcSin`/`Sin`/`Cos` (the large-argument reduction included) were compared at full precision by `cargo xtask parity script --script probes/numerics`; `EulerToM33` with the Euler rotation form (`iRotation=1`) keeps its unit tests and its angles are probed, but the NIF rotation union's Euler branch itself still has no oracle run; (5) the script adapter's object API of the data format units (`xejviScriptAdapterDF`, 107 ledger entries) is phase 6's. Step 4 (Sniff: processor pattern and the first processors) landed on `phase-5-assets` (Opus 5.5, 2026-10-08). `crates/xedit-assets/src/sniff/` ports `SniffProcessor.pas` (`processor.rs`: `TProcBase` as the trait `Proc`, the settings storage, `TMemIniFile`, the string list and `TStrings` text helpers, `TProcManager.Process`), the automation mode of `frmMain.pas` (`main_form.rs`: the command line switches, the file list of a folder or an archive, processing on all threads with the results committed in file order, which is Sniff's `-threads:1` order, the summary, and the abort on the first error without `-skip:yes`), `TPerlRegEx` (`perl_regex.rs`, on the `regex` crate) and 36 of the 50 operations of `Sniff/Proc` (`procs.rs` lists all 50 in the form's order; the others are `pending!` with their reason). The `sniff` binary takes the arguments of `Sniff.exe` (`-S: -OP: -I: -O: -LOG: -P: -skip: -subdir: -all: -threads:`); the commands are `sniff.list` and `sniff.run` (`xedit sniff list|run`, mutating, `--dry-run`, the `--edit` gate). The guide for the rest is "Porting a Sniff processor" in the `porting-pascal-unit` skill. *Oracle.* `cargo xtask parity sniff` (`crates/xtask/src/parity/sniff.rs`, 56 cases: the defaults of each operation and the settings that take other branches) runs `Sniff.exe` in its automation mode and the port in process on every archive of the games each operation supports, and compares the output of each file (FNV-1a), the error of each skipped file, the log lines as a multiset, the summary counts and the log file a processor writes. Sniff's threads share state, so a crash and any file that differs (up to 40 per archive) are run again alone (`-P:<file> -threads:1`) before they count; `--sample <n>` unpacks the first `n` files of each archive (of the case's extensions) for quick runs. Sniff and the xEdit GUI oracle now start hidden on a desktop of the harness's own (`parity/hidden.rs`: `SW_HIDE` and `CreateDesktopW`; the GUI watcher reads that desktop), and the work folder of an archive is removed once it compares equal (`--keep` keeps it; a full JSON run of `Fallout4 - Meshes.ba2` still peaks near 26 GB while Sniff writes it). Results on 2026-10-08 with the first 40 files of every archive (`--sample 40`), all 56 cases: 41808 `equal`, 56 `equal-error`, 88917 `unchanged`, 3 `oracle-failed`, nothing else; the KF cases on their own KF samples (`fix-kf`, `copy-priorities`, `quadratic-to-linear`, `remove-controlled`, `remove-controlled-others`, `optimize-kf`): 1099 `equal`, 3 `equal-error`, 5016 `unchanged`, logs equal. Full corpus (every file of every archive), as equal, `equal-error` (Fallout 76 and Starfield meshes and the Oblivion 10.0.1.x meshes, refused with the same message), `unchanged`: `tangents` 123853, 87, 197521; `tangents-add` 130384, 91, 190986; `bounds` 320297, 87, 8397; `replace-assets` 325003, 87, 17542; `json` 339899, 87, 0 (one Nuka World mesh that Sniff failed among its threads is equal alone); `tweaker` 60797, 87, 292953; `fixer` 281561, 87, 47133 (one Dead Money log difference was a file that failed among the threads, equal alone with `--refresh-oracle`); `apply-transform` 34428, 91, 294250 and 12 differences that the RTL's `Power10` below fixed (the Skyrim LE and SE mesh archives that held them then compared 8580 `equal`, 3 `equal-error`, 30680 `unchanged`); `adjust-transform` 328692, 87, 11207. The 3 `oracle-failed` are `Remove nodes` on three Fallout 4 Creation Club meshes (`midfloor01foundation_carpet.nif`, `midfloortoroof01_carpet.nif`, `midfloortoroof01_tile.nif`): `Sniff.exe` reads freed memory after `RemoveBranch` of several shapes (an access violation in `TdfElement.Root` that repeats alone and depends on the heap); the port writes the file. Fixes the oracle found: Delphi's `ArcTan2` (with `ArcTan`, `ArcCos`, `ArcSin`) and `RoundTo` from the machine code (`nif_math`; the `Bitangent` bytes of Oblivion tangents); `SameValue` of doubles at `1E-12` (`nif_math::same_value`); `Round` of NaN to the integer indefinite in the byte float setter; the RTL's `Power10` with paired-double tables in `xedit_core::delphi` (`FloatToStrF` of vertices rounded to six decimals; `parity dump` of `Update.esm` (SSE) and `DLCRobot.esm` unchanged); an inline `var` initialised from a `Single` is a `Double` (`Update Havok inertia`); unassigned string and Variant results kept by their hidden temporaries (`Search for Havok material`, `Havok information`, and `not NativeValues[...]` negating its temporary in place in `Check for errors`); the typed constant `fColor4White` holding the integer `$3F800000` as a float; the `Stack overflow` of `GetTarget` on skinned meshes. *Step 5 work list (Sonnet 5.5).* (a) Ported and checked on the sample (and on the full corpus where listed above): Update tangents and binormals, Update bounds, Search and replace assets, Convert to and from JSON, Universal tweaker, Universal fixer, Apply transformation, Adjust transformation, Attach parent NiNode, Remove nodes, Remove unused nodes, Convert block type, Unskin mesh, Set missing names, Fix 3DS exported KF, Merge properties, Add NiLODNode, Add bounding box, Add RootCollisionNode, Copy anim priorities, Remove controlled blocks, Quadratic to linear anim, Optimize Animations, Update Havok settings, Update Havok inertia, Update ragdoll constraint, Search for Havok material, Update shader flags, Real Time Reflections - NVSE, Check for errors (mesh checks), Transform information, Havok information, Find unwelded vertices, Find excessive draw calls, Find UVs, Vanilla Plus Particles - NVSE. (b) Owed checks and known differences: the full-corpus run of the other 28 default cases and of the 19 cases with other settings than `tangents-add` (`cargo xtask parity sniff` without `--sample`; the oracle results of the cases above are cached); `Remove nodes` on the 3 files above (upstream reads freed memory, not reproduced); the texture checks of `Check for errors` (which failed each `.dds` file then; done in step 5); Sniff's threads now and then write a file differently or fail it (`Unskin mesh`, `Remove nodes`, `Universal fixer`, `Convert to and from JSON`), which the harness runs alone. (c) Not ported: Copy geometry blocks (`ProcCopyGeometryBlocks`), Vertex color painting (`ProcVertexPaint`), Group shapes (`ProcGroupShapes`), Merge shapes (`ProcMergeShapes`), Copy anim controlled blocks (`ProcCopyControlledBlocks`), Add headtracking anim (`ProcAddHeadtrackingAnim`), Add facial anim (`ProcAddFacialAnim`), Add NiTransformData (`ProcJamAnim`), Weijiesen's blow up thing (`ProcWeiExplosion`) and Add blocks from skeleton (`ProcAnimSkeletonDeath`) for step 5; Optimize mesh (`ProcOptimize`) and Analyze mesh (`ProcAnalyzeMesh`), which need `wbMeshOptimize` (step 6); Find textures (`ProcFindTextures`), which needs `wbDDS` (step 2); Update MOPP code (`ProcMoppUpdate`), which calls `NifMopp.dll` (not ported); `ProcCollapseLinksArrays` is not registered in the 4.1.5q form. Owed from step 4 besides: (1) closed by parity #39 (PR #53): `xedit_core::delphi::same_value` is the RTL's `DoubleResolution` (`1E-12`) and `delphi::round`/`delphi::trunc` give the x64 integer indefinite for a NaN or an out-of-range value, which the record code (`wbFloatDef`, the `wbDefinitions*` callbacks, the universal tweaker), the script `Round`/`Trunc`/`Ceil`/`Floor` and the integer formatters all reach; the probes' SameValue boundary pairs and NaN/out-of-range cases equal the oracle; (2) `Search and replace assets` and the tweaker ran on the `regex` crate in place of PCRE (closed by the parity backlog's #29, which replaced it with the port's own PCRE engine: `crates/xedit-core/src/pcre.rs`); (3) `TStringList.Sort` and `AnsiSameText` compare with the user's locale through `CompareStringW` (`xedit_io::collate::ansi_compare_text`), which is the API the RTL calls; parity #39 probed the comparisons, the sorts and `LowerCase`/`UpperCase`/`AnsiLowerCase`/`AnsiUpperCase` under the machine's locale (English) and code page (1252), and other user locales cannot be driven without changing the machine's locale, so they are still owed; (4) the morph model comparison of `Check for errors` never runs upstream (its file name is empty) and was not ported; (5) closed by parity #39 (PR #53) as in (1); the script's `Round`/`Trunc`/`Ceil`/`Floor` of NaN and of out-of-range values and the `Math` unit's constants (`MinSingle`, `MinDouble`, `NaN`) were compared with the oracle at the byte level. This also settles step 3's owed `ArcTan2`. Step 5 (Sniff processors) landed on `phase-5-assets` (Sonnet 5.5, 2026-10-09). It ports the ten remaining units of `Sniff/Proc` (Copy geometry blocks, Vertex color painting, Group shapes, Merge shapes, Copy anim controlled blocks, Add headtracking anim, Add facial anim, Add NiTransformData, Weijiesen's blow up thing, Add blocks from skeleton) as `crates/xedit-assets/src/sniff/proc_*.rs` on the pattern of step 4 (the controls of the frame are the fields of the processor with the `.dfm` defaults, `on_show` reads them, `on_start` checks them, `process_file` takes `&self`), `Find textures` (the DDS record of a texture archive entry, the header of a loose or archived file read to `MAX_HEADER_SIZE`, the filters of the value list editor, the report line and the `TwbDDSFile` header dump of `data_format_misc`), and the texture checks of `Check for errors` (`CheckDDS` and `CheckSSEDdsFormat` over `xedit_io::dds`; a `.dds` file is read while one of them is on, and one that is not a DDS is reported `Not a valid DDS file`). `Update MOPP code` (`NifMopp.dll`) and `ProcCollapseLinksArrays` (not registered in the 4.1.5q form) stay unported; 49 of the 50 operations of the form are ported and their settings are listed by `sniff list`, and the ledger, the map, the README and the `using-xedit-cli` skill are updated. *Harness.* A case that names `{source}` gets that folder made by the port itself over the unpacked files (the controlled block priorities of `Copy anim priorities`, the renamed controlled blocks of `Copy anim controlled blocks`, the scaled transforms, the renamed texture sets and the baked transforms of `Copy geometry blocks`), and `Add blocks from skeleton` gets an input folder with a `death.kf` of the archive and the `skeleton.nif` of its folder. 27 cases were added (the check has 91 now): copy-geometry 3, vertex-paint 5, group-shapes 2, merge-shapes 2, find-textures 4, copy-controlled, headtracking 2, facial 2, jam-anim 2, wei-explosion, skeleton-death 2 and check-errors-dds. *Results* (2026-10-09; equal / unchanged / oracle-failed, `--sample 20` over every game of the operation unless a game is named): vertex-paint 395/792, vertex-paint-adjust 675/1090, vertex-paint-adjust-add 675/1090, vertex-paint-remove 2/1763, vertex-paint-replace 358/1407; merge-shapes and merge-shapes-exact 0/1725/40; copy-geometry 2/8 (fo4), copy-geometry-transform 10 (tes4), copy-geometry-shader 104/30 (fnv, tes5), copy-controlled 130 (fo3, fnv); headtracking and headtracking-clamp 130 each, facial and facial-remove 130 each (fo3, fnv), jam-anim and jam-anim-translation 124/26 each (tes4, fo3, fnv); `find-textures` over `Fallout4 - Textures1.ba2` is 3551 unchanged with the report of every file equal but for the `XBOX` marker below. `group-shapes-split` on Oblivion is 714 equal, 7231 unchanged and 87 equal-error with `--threads 1`: at the default thread count Sniff's threads lost the output of the 714 files, which the harness reports as `port-only-output` because there are more of them than the 40 it runs again alone (they compare equal alone). The step 6 ports this step verifies on the sample: optimize 171/191 (fo4, tes4), optimize-vertex-cache 117/201, optimize-overdraw 110/208, optimize-fetch 119/199, optimize-stripify 117/201, optimize-triangulate 318 unchanged, analyze-mesh and analyze-mesh-shapes 318 unchanged (fo4), all equal. Compared by hand outside the harness: the texture checks on five DDS files built for them (not a power of two, R5G6B5, X1R5G5B5, X4R4G4B4 and noise) report the same messages as `Sniff.exe`, the header dump of the Fallout 4 loose textures is the same text, `Add blocks from skeleton` writes the same `death.kf` for four archives (the Oblivion and Fallout meshes archives and Shivering Isles) and the outputs of `Copy geometry blocks` of the Skyrim and New Vegas samples are byte for byte the oracle's. *Fixes the oracle found.* `Vertex color painting` read `iAdjustMod` but kept its own default, so the add mode of the HSL adjustment was never taken (the 675 files of `vertex-paint-adjust-add` differed, the multiply case passed); Delphi's `Format` has no zero flag, so the `%04d` of the `Find textures` report line pads with spaces where the port wrote zeros; `Copy geometry blocks` raised an access violation for the 24 Skyrim and New Vegas meshes whose shader has no `Texture Set` element or one that links to nothing, which upstream checks; the harness matched only the exact `{source}` placeholder, so a case whose setting names a longer placeholder ran with an empty source directory (Sniff then waits on a dialog), and the first file of a prepared folder could be its `.complete` marker. *Known differences.* `Merge shapes`: `Sniff.exe` fails with an access violation (offset 604EFD, Read of address 0, the same when the file runs alone) on the files it merges, 40 of the sample (`outfit_go`, `outfitf` and `outfitm` of the FNV preorder packs and Fallout 3 armour and clutter meshes among them), where the port writes the file; one file of the Fallout 3 sample merged on both sides with equal bytes. `Add blocks from skeleton`: the harness case hangs `Sniff.exe` on the desktop the harness makes (the same command on the user's desktop takes a second), so the operation was compared by hand (above) and the case stays owed. `Find textures` on a texture archive: upstream's `prop` record is a local Delphi does not zero and its archive branch never assigns `XBOX`, so the report prints `  XBOX` on every line of `Fallout4 - Textures1.ba2` (228 lines) where the port prints none; the two line sets are equal once the marker is stripped, and the port reads the flag as false (`UPSTREAM-QUIRK` in `proc_find_textures.rs`). The single file mode of `Copy geometry blocks` (`bMatchingFiles=0`) hangs `Sniff.exe` as soon as a block of the source file matches one of the file (a dialog; one thread does it too), so it has no case and a unit test of the processor stands in (`the_single_file_mode_copies_the_transform`). *Owed.* (1) The full-corpus run of the cases not named above (vertex-paint and its four settings cases, group-shapes and group-shapes-split over the other games, copy-geometry on Fallout 4, copy-geometry-shader, merge-shapes on the remaining games, find-textures and its three settings cases over the other games, check-errors-dds over the other games; the sample runs listed above are cached); (2) the `Add blocks from skeleton` case run (see above); (3) the step 4 owed list (b), the other 28 default and 19 alt-settings cases without `--sample`, and step 6's owed full-corpus run of `Optimize mesh` and `Analyze mesh`; (4) `ProcCollapseLinksArrays` has no operation in the 4.1.5q form, so it stays unported by design. Step 7 (skills and docs) landed on `phase-5-assets` (2026-10-09, run on deepseek-flash in place of the suggested Sonnet 5.5): the skills `handling-archives` and `nif-batch-operations`, the README with the phase 5 status and the gate, the gate statement above, `coverage/ledger.toml` in the canonical key order of `cargo xtask sync` (the step 5 agent had left the field order unsynced) with `coverage/ledger.toml` and `upstream-map.toml` checked against what the steps ported, and the map notes that now name `lodgen.generate` for `xeLODGenForm.pas`, `xeInit.pas` and `xeMainForm.pas`. Final checks at the tip: `cargo fmt --check` and clippy `--workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` are clean, `cargo xtask check` passes, and `cargo test --workspace` passes but for the one pre-existing failure `copies_a_skyrim_npc_as_an_override` (a phase-4 issue: the order of a copied container array, not this phase's); no harness run was repeated for this step, the numbers above are the recorded runs of steps 1 to 6. The Fable review of the phase found two things, both fixed in this pull request: the stale `Core/wbHelpers.pas` map entry (four of its functions are ported since step 6, now named in the note) and `parity sniff` and `parity nif` returning success on a difference (they now exit with an error on a fatal outcome, with the oracle's own crashes and the outputs its threads lose non-fatal; the rule is in the `checking-parity` skill).



### Phase 5: Archives, assets and LOD

- **Model:** Opus 5.5 for NIF, Sniff and LODGen. Sonnet 5.5 for BSA, BA2 and DDS, which are well-specified container formats.
- **Port:** BSA and BA2 read and write, DDS, BSArch, NIF and material formats, Wwise, Sniff operations, LODGen.
- **CLI:** `bsarch` with the upstream arguments, `xedit archive list|extract|pack`, `sniff <operation>`, `xedit lodgen`.
- **Skills:** Asset skills: `handling-archives` (archive handling: `xedit archive list|extract|pack` and the `bsarch` binary) and `nif-batch-operations` (the Sniff batch operations and `xedit lodgen`).
- **Performance:** Parallel pack and unpack. SIMD for half-float conversion, hashing and texture block processing.
- **Gate:** Packed archives are byte-identical to BSArch output. Sniff and LODGen outputs equal the oracle. Met 2026-10-09: the packed archives are byte-identical to `BSArch.exe -mt:no` and the Sniff and LODGen outputs equal the oracle on the corpus (the numbers are in Status).
- **Steps** (one pull request, `phase-5-assets`; each step lands with its own check; steps 1 and 3 can start at once, the others wait for the steps named):
  1. **Archives** (Sonnet 5.5, done): the rest of `wbBSArchive` (Morrowind archives, writing every BSA and general BA2 version), LZ4 compression, the BSA hashes and xxHash of `wbHash`, the resource listings of `wbBSA`, parallel pack and unpack; `bsarch` with the upstream arguments, `xedit archive list|extract|pack`.
  2. **Textures** (Sonnet 5.5, done): `wbDDS`, texture BA2 (DX10) read and write, the texture helpers of `wbBSA`, SIMD for half-float conversion and texture block processing with a scalar fallback.
  3. **NIF and materials** (Opus 5.5, done): `wbDataFormat`, `wbDataFormatNif`, `wbDataFormatNifTypes`, `wbDataFormatMaterial`, `wbDataFormatMisc`, `wbNifMath`, `wbNifScanner`, Wwise; load then save byte-identical for the meshes and materials of the corpus archives.
  4. **Sniff** (Opus 5.5 for the processor pattern and the first processors, after 3, done): `SniffProcessor` and the processors of `Sniff/Proc`; `sniff <operation>`.
  5. **Sniff processors** (Sonnet 5.5, after 4, done): the remaining processors on the pattern of step 4 (the guide is "Porting a Sniff processor" in the `porting-pascal-unit` skill): `ProcCopyGeometryBlocks` (Copy geometry blocks), `ProcVertexPaint` (Vertex color painting), `ProcGroupShapes` (Group shapes), `ProcMergeShapes` (Merge shapes), `ProcCopyControlledBlocks` (Copy anim controlled blocks), `ProcAddHeadtrackingAnim` (Add headtracking anim), `ProcAddFacialAnim` (Add facial anim), `ProcJamAnim` (Add NiTransformData), `ProcWeiExplosion` (Weijiesen's blow up thing) and `ProcAnimSkeletonDeath` (Add blocks from skeleton), each a `pending!` line in `sniff/procs.rs`. Left for other steps: `ProcOptimize` (Optimize mesh) and `ProcAnalyzeMesh` (Analyze mesh) need `wbMeshOptimize` (step 6); `ProcFindTextures` (Find textures) and the texture checks of `ProcCheckForErrors` need `wbDDS` (step 2).
  6. **LODGen** (Opus 5.5, after 2 and 3, done): `wbLOD`, `wbMeshOptimize`, the `tmLODgen` tool mode; `xedit lodgen`.
     - *Notes (branch `p5-step6`, step done 2026-10-09).* Done: `wbMeshOptimize` (`crates/xedit-assets/src/mesh_optimize.rs`), `SpellOptimize`, `SpellStripify`, `SpellTriangulate`, Sniff `Optimize mesh` and `Analyze mesh` (parity sniff cases equal with `--sample 20`; full-corpus run owed); the Vampyre imaging subset (`crates/xedit-assets/src/imaging/`); all of `wbLOD` (`crates/xedit-assets/src/lod/`, `wbSplitTreeLOD` included); `wbHelpers` process and archive helpers (`crates/xedit-core/src/helpers.rs`); `lodgen.generate` (`crates/xedit-session/src/lodgen.rs`, CLI `xedit lodgen` with `--set`, `--seed`, `--split-trees`, `--dry-run`); `cargo xtask parity lodgen` (`crates/xtask/src/parity/lodgen.rs`). Results (all equal): fo4-sanctuary, fo4-vr, fo4-nukaworld, fo4-farharbor (log only: UPSTREAM-QUIRK, the LOD model path `MNAM\LOD #n (Level n)\Mesh` of `wbGetLODMeshName` is not in the 4.1.5q definitions, so FO4 and SSE objects LOD always end with `no valid references found`), sse-sovngarde, sse-soulcairn, sse-japhetsfolly, fnv-strip (10 output files), fnv-gamorrah, fo3-dcworld05, tes4-all (455 `.lod`/`.cmp` files), fnv-strip-dxt5-bc5, -8888-565, -888-bc4, -dxt1-chunk, fnv-strip-trees, sse-sovngarde-trees, sse-sovngarde-trees3d (2026-10-08), sse-split (100 output files, 54 log lines: Tamriel, Sovngarde and DLC2SolstheimWorld split; Blackreach writes `Worldspace doesn't have a Trees LOD list file.`) and fnv-split (no output file, 2 log lines: New Vegas has no trees LOD, both its worldspaces only write a message) (2026-10-09). The harness holds Shift for the oracle from a helper thread on the harness desktop (`gui::hold_shift`: `AttachThreadInput` needs `SetThreadDesktop` across desktops) so that the hidden `Split LOD Atlas` button shows and is pressed; `finished()` counts one end message per worldspace, and the message log of the GUI (read from its memo) ends in a run of NUL characters right after its last line, which hid the fourth end (3 of 4 counted): `split_end` trims them like `log_lines` does. Traps: write Rust with backslashes through files, not heredocs; LODGenx64 (.NET) fails on a verbatim `\\?\` working folder; the FNV/FO3/TES4 work goes to `E:\projects\xedit-parity-scratch-p5-step6` (hard links need the game's drive).
  7. **Skills and docs** (Sonnet 5.5, after all, done): archive handling and NIF batch operation skills, README, ledger, map, the gate evidence.
- **Status:** Gate met 2026-10-09 (draft pull request #14, branch `phase-5-assets`): the archives `pack` writes are byte-identical to `BSArch.exe -mt:no` (`cargo xtask parity bsarch`: 1553 checks, 1553 equal, 0 different, 0 failed, 0 deferred, over the archives of the 11 games and the synthetic folders; step 1's 1400 checks with the 250 deferred texture ones became these when the textures landed), and the outputs of the asset operations equal the oracle: `cargo xtask parity nif` 792961 files, 542505 equal (dump and save), 250543 equal-error, 0 different; `cargo xtask parity sniff` 0 files written differently in every case of steps 4 and 5 that ran on the full corpus and in the sample runs of every case (the `equal-error` and `oracle-failed` files are the oracle's own refusals and crashes, below; the full-corpus per-case totals: step 4's tangents 123853, tangents-add 130384, bounds 320297, replace-assets 325003, json 339899, tweaker 60797, fixer 281561, apply-transform 34428 and then 8580, the 12 differences the RTL power-of-ten port fixed, adjust-transform 328692; step 5's wei-explosion 5, headtracking 4444, headtracking-clamp 2907, facial 8586, facial-remove 8586, jam-anim 10125, jam-anim-translation 10125, copy-controlled 8577, copy-geometry-transform 23549, group-shapes 825, copy-geometry 4926, group-shapes-split 714 and merge-shapes 21 with `--threads 1`), while the cases listed as owed below have only their samples; `cargo xtask parity lodgen` 20 cases, 20 equal, 0 different. Step results follow. Step 1 (archives) landed on `phase-5-assets` (Sonnet 5.5, 2026-10-08). It ports the rest of `wbBSArchive` (`TwbAsset`; `TwbBSArchive` reading and writing every BSA version, Morrowind and general BA2 version, with the DX10 file table; `TwbSplitPacker` and `TwbMultiSourcePacker`), LZ4 and LZ4F compression and zlib above 8 MiB (`wbCompression`, from the C libraries upstream links: lz4 1.10 and zlib give the oracle's bytes), the BSA, BA2 and xxHash hashes of `wbHash`, the resource listings and hash tables of `wbBSA` (`container_handler.rs`), the console tool of `BSArch.dpr` as the `bsarch` binary with the upstream arguments and text, and the commands `archive.list`, `archive.extract` and `archive.pack` (`xedit archive list|extract|pack`, with `--dry-run` and the `--edit` gate). The code is `crates/xedit-io/src/archive/` (`mod.rs` reading and listing, `write.rs` the creation, `packer/` the packers and the unpacking, `asset.rs`, `texture.rs`), `crates/bsarch`, `crates/xedit-session/src/archive.rs`. *Determinism.* Upstream packs on a thread pool and the data of an archive lands in the order the threads finish; the oracle is `BSArch.exe -mt:no`, which is one thread. The port reproduces that single thread's steps on any thread count: the reading, hashing and compressing of the files runs ahead on the pool (a window of files and 512 MiB), and the decisions that depend on the order (the files that share data, the split into archives from the compressed sizes, the order the data is written in, `fProcessTick mod 3` of the loading and writing steps) are made in upstream's order as the results arrive. Two quirks make `-mt:yes` and `-mt:no` differ upstream: the Morrowind list is sorted by name only without threads (the port always sorts), and the data order is a race (the port takes the single thread's). *Texture archives.* Everything that looks inside a DDS file or builds one is the extension point `archive/texture.rs` (`chunk_dds`, `pack_dds_chunks`, `pack_dds`, `unpack_dds`, each failing with `Texture (DX10) archives are not supported yet`); `create_archive` and `save` already reserve and write the DX10 file table, `pack_chunk` takes any chunk, and the file table, `-dump` and the format names of the DX10 archives read and print like the oracle's.
