The gate evidence is `cargo xtask parity bsarch`: for each archive of the game folders (the 363 archives of 11 games up to `--max-size` MB, 40 by default, or the ones named with `--archive`) it compares the `-dump` text of the oracle and the port, the files `unpack` writes, and the archives `pack` writes from the unpacked folder (the oracle with `-mt:no`; the port with all threads and with `-mt:no`; every compression of the source's format, and the text of the run), plus `--synthetic` for what the game archives do not cover (two source folders and an archive merged, filters, flags, `-split`, files without data, names that sort several ways, identical files, textures only, a name with accents). Result on 2026-10-08: 1400 checks, 1150 equal, 0 different, 0 failed, 250 `deferred`. The 250 are the `unpack` and `pack` of the 125 texture archives (step 2); their `-dump` is compared and equal. By format (list, unpack, pack): BSA 103 (Oblivion) 6, 6, 12 of 12; BSA 104 (Fallout 3, New Vegas, Skyrim LE) 18, 18, 32; BSA 105 (Skyrim SE) 4, 4, 8; BA2 v1 general 17, 17, 34; v2 general (Starfield) 20, 20, 60 (zlib, zlib with `-z`, LZ4); v8 general 173, 173, 346; texture archives v1, v2, v7, v8 listed only (125 of 125 equal); Morrowind has no archive small enough, so `Tribunal.bsa` (63 MB) was checked by hand and the synthetic folders cover it (4 of 4 equal); 57 synthetic packs equal. Two archives (`Fallout - Misc.bsa` of Fallout 3 and New Vegas) cannot be unpacked by the oracle (`menus\s.txt`: a file flagged compressed that is not, `LibDeflate error: Bad data`); the port stops with the same message. Spot checks outside the harness: a synthetic 1.5 GB folder packed with `-split:1` for `-sse`, `-fnv -z` and `-fo4` gives two archives each that equal the oracle's bytes; `Skyrim - Meshes1.bsa` unpacked and packed with `-sse -z` (548 MB, 14242 files, LZ4F) gives the oracle's 377 735 969 bytes in 33 s where `BSArch.exe -mt:no` needs 3 min 4 s; the raw text of a pack (progress included) equals the oracle's `-mt:no`; `cargo xtask parity dump` on `Update.esm` (Skyrim SE), `DLCRobot.esm` and `SFBGS004.esm` (which read strings from archives) is unchanged (equal, equal-prefix, equal). `BSArch.exe` hung at its start in 9 runs of the batch (no CPU time at all, `-dump` and `pack` alike); the harness stops a run that uses no CPU for two minutes and runs it again. The oracle fails on paths over 260 characters, which the port writes (the harness uses short folder names). Owed from step 1: (1) the DDS code (step 2); (2) the stream-to-stream overloads of `wbCompression` have no caller (ported by issue #32 as `compress_to`, `compress_stream` and `decompress_stream`, checked by unit tests against the buffer routes; still no caller in 4.1.5q, so no oracle); (3) `ResolveFileHash` and `ResolveFolderHash` are wired into `wbFileHashCallback` and `wbFolderHashCallback` behind `wbLoaderDone`, which only a GUI load sets (xDump never does), so no dump output changed (closed by issue #32: every session sets it after its load and builds the cache as `TLoaderThread` does; `BuildCache` walks its `TDictionary` in bucket order, which `crates/xedit-core/src/delphi_dictionary.rs` reproduces with the 2^21 buckets and the FNV-1a string hash measured on the oracle; `parity resources fnv-hashes`, ten pairs of TES4 hash collisions, equal); (4) `TList.Sort` of the BSA hash order is a quicksort upstream and a stable sort in the port, which differ only for two files with the same folder and name hash (closed by issue #32: the RTL's quick sort, `delphi_list_sort` in `archive/write.rs`; `parity bsarch --synthetic` `edge`); (5) names that hash differently in a code page other than 1252 were not checked (the port converts like `AnsiString` with the system ANSI code page; issue #32 checked names outside ASCII in and out of 1252 against the oracle, which found that the archive code lowers ASCII letters only, `LowerCase`, where the port lowered every letter, and that `BSArch.exe` prints in the ANSI code page and trims the period of an OS message, all fixed; another system code page stays unverifiable on this machine); (6) `BSArchPro` (the GUI of `BSArch/`) is the GUI phase's. Step 2 (textures) landed on `phase-5-assets` (Sonnet 5.5, 2026-10-08). `crates/xedit-io/src/dds.rs` ports `wbDDS` (the header records `DdsHeader`, `DdsHeaderDx10` and `DdsHeaderXbox` read from and written to byte slices, the `TDXGI` and `TD3DFORMAT` numbers as `Dxgi` and `D3dFormat`, the format sets, `IsDDS`, `GetDXGI`, `GetD3DFMT`, `SetUpHeader`, `GetBitsPerPixel`, `GetMipSize`, `ConvertR8G8B8toB8G8R8X8` and the rest of `TwbDDS`); `archive/texture.rs` fills the extension points of step 1: `chunk_dds` (`TwbSplitPacker.LoadFile` for a DDS: the uncompressed header chunk and the compressed mipmap chunks, the size and hash of the converted file), `pack_dds_chunks` (`TwbBSArchive.Pack` with a packer), `prepare_dds` and `store_dds` (`Pack` without one, cut and compressed on the pool, stored in file order), `unpack_dds` (the header `SetUpHeader` makes and the chunks appended). `bsarch -fo4dds` and `-sf1dds`, `xedit archive pack --format fo4dds|sf1dds` and `archive extract` of texture archives work; the texture helper of `wbBSA` (the `.ddx` name `BuildCache` adds) was ported with the unit. *Vector code.* Upstream has none; the plan's SIMD items are `xedit_io::simd` (24 to 32 bit pixel conversion on SSSE3 or AVX2, 9.0 GB/s scalar, 19 GB/s vector in a release build) and `xedit_core::half_float` (`half_to_float_slice` and `single_to_half_slice` on F16C, 1.3 and 1.4 G values/s scalar, 8.7 and 9.0 vector, with `FloatToHalf`'s truncation and its infinity for what overflows kept, and blocks that hold a NaN or an overflow left to the scalar routine). The scalar version is the definition: the unit tests compare every level on every input shape, the half-float conversion was checked on all 2^32 singles and all 2^16 halves (`cargo test -p xedit-core --release --lib -- --ignored every_float`), and `XEDIT_SIMD=off` takes the scalar route everywhere; no output depends on the CPU or the thread count (`crates/xedit-session/tests/archive.rs` packs textures with 1, 2 and 7 threads, with and without a split, to the same bytes). The half-float routines have no caller yet (the vertex arrays of the data formats go through the element tree one value at a time). *Oracle.* `cargo xtask parity bsarch` now runs the `unpack` and `pack` of the texture archives (`--textures` takes only those; `pack` uses `-fo4dds` or `-sf1dds`, each compression of the format) and `--synthetic` adds generated DDS folders (BC1 to BC7, uncompressed, half-float and 8 bit formats, odd sizes, a cube map, a header with mipmap count 0, 24 bit RGB, identical textures, data that ends early, and the files both sides refuse: noise, a short header, A8, an unknown DX10 format, an Xbox texture), each as `-z`, uncompressed, `-share:no`, split, `-split:0`, a filter, `-split:1` and `-z:lz4`. Result on 2026-10-08, the whole selection (every archive up to 40 MB of the 11 games, with `--synthetic`): 1553 checks, 1553 equal, 0 different, 0 failed, 0 deferred (1400 before, 250 of them deferred, which the 125 `unpack` and 254 `pack` checks of the texture archives replace). The 125 texture archives: v1 2, v2 4 (Starfield), v7 1, v8 118; `list`, `unpack` and `pack` (2 packs for Fallout 4, 3 for Starfield) all equal (125 `unpack` and 254 `pack` checks); 24 synthetic texture packs equal, 5 of them refused identically. Spot check of a 208 MB archive (`DLCworkshop03 - Textures.ba2`): unpack and pack equal, and packing it with `-z` takes 3.4 s where `BSArch.exe -mt:no` needs 27.4 s. *Quirks kept.* `GetD3DFMT` tests `DDPF_ALPHA`, not `DDPF_ALPHAPIXELS`, and asks `X1R5G5B5` for an alpha mask; the header `SetUpHeader` writes for `A8_UNORM` has only the alpha flag, which `GetDXGI` does not read as a format, so such a file is `Unsupported DDS format`; the enumeration is one byte, so a DX10 format number is cut to a byte. *Deviation.* A texture whose data ends before its last mipmap is cut where the data ends in both routes (upstream's route with a packer, `Copy` of a dynamic array, does that; the route without one reads past its buffer, which cannot be compared). *API for step 5 (Sniff's "Find textures" and the texture checks of "Check for errors").* The DDS file functions are free functions of `xedit_io::dds` on the bytes of the file: `is_dds`, `dxgi`, `dxgi_format_name`, `bits_per_pixel`, `bits_per_pixel_of`, `is_compressed`, `has_alpha`, `is_cube_map`, `is_xbox`, `d3dfmt`, `d3dfmt_format_name`, `D3D_NODXGI`, `DdsHeader::read` (`width`, `height`, `mip_map_count`, `pixel_format`) and `MAX_HEADER_SIZE` for reading the head of a loose file; for a file of a texture archive `ProcFileObject.file_entry` has the `dds` record (`width`, `height`, `num_mips`, `dxgi_format`, `tex_chunks[].chunk.size`), `is_cube_map()` and `dxgi_format_name()`, and `ProcFileObject.input.archive` tells the archive type (`archive_type().is_dds()`). The two processors stay `pending` in `sniff/procs.rs` and `proc_check_for_errors.rs` with messages that name step 5. Owed from step 2: (1) the Xbox 360 conversion of `BSArchPro` (`xtexconv.exe`, the GUI tool) and the Xbox archive target of the console tool, which `BSArch.dpr` does not have; (2) the half-float routines have no caller; (3) NEON versions for ARM (the kernels fall back to the scalar code there); (4) `wbHelpers.wbDDSDataToBitmap` and the other bitmap conversions belong to the GUI phase. Step 3 (NIF and materials) landed on `phase-5-assets` (Opus 5.5, 2026-10-08). The new crate `crates/xedit-assets` ports `wbDataFormat` (`data_format.rs`: the definitions `TdfDef` and their subclasses, and the element tree, which is an arena `Tree` of elements `El` whose methods are the upstream methods of `TdfElement` dispatched on the upstream class; `json.rs` builds and prints JSON as `JsonDataObjects` does, `variant.rs` the `Variant` conversions and comparisons the callbacks rely on), `wbDataFormatNifTypes` (`data_format_nif_types.rs`), `wbDataFormatNif` (`data_format_nif.rs`: the NiObject registry, `TwbNifFile` with its header maintenance and `TwbNifBlock`; `data_format_nif/geometry.rs`: vertices, normals, UVs, triangles, strips, transforms, bounds, `ApplyTransform`, `UpdateBounds`, `UpdateNormals`, `UpdateTangents`, `RemoveBranch`, `ConvertBlock`, `GetAssets`, `GetLinkArrays`, `DetectBSXFlags` and the `Spell*` that need no mesh optimizer), `wbDataFormatMaterial`, `wbDataFormatMisc`, `wbNifMath` and `wbNifScanner`; `asset.rs` puts the kinds of file behind one type. The block and type definitions are generated: `cargo xtask port-defs emit-df` (`crates/xtask/src/portdefs/df.rs`, on the Pascal reader of the record transpiler) writes every builder overload as a function of its own (upstream's `dfBool` with events drops them, which only per-overload code keeps), the 322 `wbDefine*` procedures (383 NiObject definitions), and the callbacks that stay in a small typed subset (version checks, `NativeValues` lookups and comparisons, `with nif(e) do`, assignments to the `var` parameter); it translated 832 routines of the two units, and the 28 callbacks outside the subset are ported by hand (`callbacks.rs` of each). There is no Wwise data format unit at the baseline tag: the Wwise GUID index of Starfield is part of `wbDefinitionsSF1` and was ported in phase 2. The commands are `assets.dump` (`ToText`, or `ToJSON` as Sniff's JSON converter writes it, with the decimals and the rotation form as options), `assets.blocks`, `assets.types`, `assets.scan` (`NifBlockList`, `NifTextureList`, `NifTextureListUVRange`), `assets.save`, `assets.set` and `assets.from-json`, all reading a loose file or a file in an archive (`xedit assets ...`), the writing ones with `--dry-run` and the `--edit` gate. *API for steps 4 and 6.* Sniff's processors and LODGen work on `NifFile` (`tree`, `root`) and the free functions of `data_format_nif` named after the upstream methods (`block_add_child`, `block_get_vertices`, `block_apply_transform`, `copy_block`, `convert_block`, `block_by_path`, `get_assets_list`, ...), with element access through `Tree::elements`, `native_values`, `edit_values` and their setters; a `TdfElement` method is a `Tree` method of the same name. *Oracle.* `cargo xtask parity nif` reads every archive of the corpus with the port's archive reader and checks each NIF, KF, BGSM and BGEM against `Sniff.exe` in its automation mode, which reads the archive itself: the JSON converter gives the dump, and the universal tweaker setting `NiHeader\Num Blocks`, which the save recomputes, gives a plain load and save (a material has no such element, and the tweaker writes it anyway: `GetEditValues` leaves its string result unassigned for a missing element, so the caller's variable comes back); `--text` runs the GUI's script adapter of the data format units (`crates/xtask/oracle/dataformat.pas`) for the `ToText` dumps and saves of the materials, LOD, FUZ and DDS files and of a sample of NIFs, and compares `dfCalcHash`; `--from-json` builds NIFs from JSON dumps with Sniff and with the port. Results on 2026-10-08: `parity nif` over every archive of the eleven games (Morrowind, Oblivion, Fallout 3, New Vegas, Skyrim, Skyrim SE, Skyrim VR, Fallout 4, Fallout 4 VR, Fallout 76, Starfield): 792961 files, 542505 `equal` (dump and save), 250543 `equal-error`, 0 in every other class; the `equal-error` files are the 87 Oblivion meshes of NIF versions 10.0.1.0 and 10.0.1.2 and every Fallout 76 and Starfield mesh (20.2.0.7 with user version 2 of 155, 173 or 175), which upstream refuses with `Unknown NIF version`, and the Fallout 76 materials (`Unexpected end of stream`). Files per game: 7482, 12069, 20744, 25760, 21625, 22806, 21907, 243351, 166845, 129916 and 120543. `--text`: 68892 files through the script adapter, each `equal` or refused with the same message (bgem 1141 and 4101, bgsm 19608 and 25888, nif 1911 and 287; btt 1272, dds 1460, dlodsettings 53, fuz 360, lod 12768 and lst 43 all equal), and `dfCalcHash` equal for 1596 of 1596 names. `--from-json` (the first 20 NIFs of each archive of the nine games Sniff reads): 1911 dumps, 1674 `equal`, 237 `equal-error`, 0 different; 3156 generated dumps with 9468 rotations (special angles and axes, random ones, every rotation of the Skyrim dragon) give Sniff's bytes. Fixes the oracle found: the hash by which upstream finds an element by name is `HashName` of the ANSI string with each character in lower case xored in and the sum rotated left by five (the first guess, an upper-cased hash, matched none of the 1596 names of the definitions); Sniff writes its JSON through a `TStringStream` in the system's ANSI code page, so names with bytes that are not UTF-8 show as `?` (24 Fallout 4 and 6 Skyrim meshes); Sniff's threads share state and rarely fail a file with an access violation that does not repeat when the file runs alone, which the harness now does for every such crash; text that is not valid UTF-8 is decoded as `UTF8ToString` decodes it (`MultiByteToWideChar`, one Skyrim mesh); `FromJSON` reads values with the `JsonDataObjects` semantics (`Cannot cast Array into String` for the 237 Fallout 4 dumps with a `BSConnectPoint::Children` block, whose array `Name` hides the `Name` of `NiExtraData`: neither xEdit nor the port can build those NIFs from their own dumps); Delphi's `Sin` and `Cos` on Win64 are the RTL's own, an ulp apart from the C runtime's and from the correctly rounded value at times, which flipped the sign or the last bit of matrix elements near zero in 71 NIFs built from JSON with the C runtime's (90 degree rotations among them) and in 6 with correctly rounded ones: `nif_math::sin` and `cos` are ported from the machine code of `Sniff.exe` and give the bits the GUI's script adapter returns for 20097 arguments; the harness builds the port's NIF from the ANSI text Sniff reads; `assets.from-json` reads its file as `TStringList.LoadFromFile` does (byte order mark, else ANSI). Owed from step 3: (1) `SpellOptimize`, `SpellStripify`, `SpellTriangulate` and `StripifyTriangles` need `wbMeshOptimize` (step 6, which landed it with the Sniff `Optimize mesh` and `Analyze mesh` processors); (2) `dfCalcHash` of names with characters outside ASCII differs from the oracle's (no definition has such a name; one probe of `parity nif --text`); (3) upstream's undefined memory is not reproduced: `AllocateValue` keeps stale bytes where the port has zeros, `WriteChars` of a fixed size copies a longer string past the value, `CalculateFaceNormals` writes past its array for a triangle index out of range (the port fails with the message of the tangent routines), and a function result left unassigned returns the caller's string upstream and an empty one in the port; (4) closed for the numbers by parity #39 (PR #53): `StrToFloat` is the RTL's own parse, fitted at full precision (`xedit_core::delphi::str_to_float`: the integer part accumulates in an `Int64` while it fits and the fraction digits in double precision, the scale is the paired-table `power10`, so the text of `MaxDouble` gives the double one ulp below it and the 21 digits of Pi give `0x1.921fb54442d17p+1` where Rust's correctly rounded parse gives `...d18`; `Inf`/`-Inf`/`+Inf`/`NaN` parse where `Infinity` refuses and `1e-400` is 0), the script `FloatToStrF` prints `FloatToDecimal`'s digits with the RTL's exponent padding, and `ArcTan2`/`ArcCos`/`ArcSin`/`Sin`/`Cos` (the large-argument reduction included) were compared at full precision by `cargo xtask parity script --script probes/numerics`; `EulerToM33` with the Euler rotation form (`iRotation=1`) keeps its unit tests and its angles are probed, but the NIF rotation union's Euler branch itself still has no oracle run; (5) the script adapter's object API of the data format units (`xejviScriptAdapterDF`, 107 ledger entries) is phase 6's. Step 4 (Sniff: processor pattern and the first processors) landed on `phase-5-assets` (Opus 5.5, 2026-10-08). `crates/xedit-assets/src/sniff/` ports `SniffProcessor.pas` (`processor.rs`: `TProcBase` as the trait `Proc`, the settings storage, `TMemIniFile`, the string list and `TStrings` text helpers, `TProcManager.Process`), the automation mode of `frmMain.pas` (`main_form.rs`: the command line switches, the file list of a folder or an archive, processing on all threads with the results committed in file order, which is Sniff's `-threads:1` order, the summary, and the abort on the first error without `-skip:yes`), `TPerlRegEx` (`perl_regex.rs`, on the `regex` crate) and 36 of the 50 operations of `Sniff/Proc` (`procs.rs` lists all 50 in the form's order; the others are `pending!` with their reason). The `sniff` binary takes the arguments of `Sniff.exe` (`-S: -OP: -I: -O: -LOG: -P: -skip: -subdir: -all: -threads:`); the commands are `sniff.list` and `sniff.run` (`xedit sniff list|run`, mutating, `--dry-run`, the `--edit` gate). The guide for the rest is "Porting a Sniff processor" in the `porting-pascal-unit` skill. *Oracle.* `cargo xtask parity sniff` (`crates/xtask/src/parity/sniff.rs`, 56 cases: the defaults of each operation and the settings that take other branches) runs `Sniff.exe` in its automation mode and the port in process on every archive of the games each operation supports, and compares the output of each file (FNV-1a), the error of each skipped file, the log lines as a multiset, the summary counts and the log file a processor writes. Sniff's threads share state, so a crash and any file that differs (up to 40 per archive) are run again alone (`-P:<file> -threads:1`) before they count; `--sample <n>` unpacks the first `n` files of each archive (of the case's extensions) for quick runs. Sniff and the xEdit GUI oracle now start hidden on a desktop of the harness's own (`parity/hidden.rs`: `SW_HIDE` and `CreateDesktopW`; the GUI watcher reads that desktop), and the work folder of an archive is removed once it compares equal (`--keep` keeps it; a full JSON run of `Fallout4 - Meshes.ba2` still peaks near 26 GB while Sniff writes it). Results on 2026-10-08 with the first 40 files of every archive (`--sample 40`), all 56 cases: 41808 `equal`, 56 `equal-error`, 88917 `unchanged`, 3 `oracle-failed`, nothing else; the KF cases on their own KF samples (`fix-kf`, `copy-priorities`, `quadratic-to-linear`, `remove-controlled`, `remove-controlled-others`, `optimize-kf`): 1099 `equal`, 3 `equal-error`, 5016 `unchanged`, logs equal. Full corpus (every file of every archive), as equal, `equal-error` (Fallout 76 and Starfield meshes and the Oblivion 10.0.1.x meshes, refused with the same message), `unchanged`: `tangents` 123853, 87, 197521; `tangents-add` 130384, 91, 190986; `bounds` 320297, 87, 8397; `replace-assets` 325003, 87, 17542; `json` 339899, 87, 0 (one Nuka World mesh that Sniff failed among its threads is equal alone); `tweaker` 60797, 87, 292953; `fixer` 281561, 87, 47133 (one Dead Money log difference was a file that failed among the threads, equal alone with `--refresh-oracle`); `apply-transform` 34428, 91, 294250 and 12 differences that the RTL's `Power10` below fixed (the Skyrim LE and SE mesh archives that held them then compared 8580 `equal`, 3 `equal-error`, 30680 `unchanged`); `adjust-transform` 328692, 87, 11207. The 3 `oracle-failed` are `Remove nodes` on three Fallout 4 Creation Club meshes (`midfloor01foundation_carpet.nif`, `midfloortoroof01_carpet.nif`, `midfloortoroof01_tile.nif`): `Sniff.exe` reads freed memory after `RemoveBranch` of several shapes (an access violation in `TdfElement.Root` that repeats alone and depends on the heap); the port writes the file. Fixes the oracle found: Delphi's `ArcTan2` (with `ArcTan`, `ArcCos`, `ArcSin`) and `RoundTo` from the machine code (`nif_math`; the `Bitangent` bytes of Oblivion tangents); `SameValue` of doubles at `1E-12` (`nif_math::same_value`); `Round` of NaN to the integer indefinite in the byte float setter; the RTL's `Power10` with paired-double tables in `xedit_core::delphi` (`FloatToStrF` of vertices rounded to six decimals; `parity dump` of `Update.esm` (SSE) and `DLCRobot.esm` unchanged); an inline `var` initialised from a `Single` is a `Double` (`Update Havok inertia`); unassigned string and Variant results kept by their hidden temporaries (`Search for Havok material`, `Havok information`, and `not NativeValues[...]` negating its temporary in place in `Check for errors`); the typed constant `fColor4White` holding the integer `$3F800000` as a float; the `Stack overflow` of `GetTarget` on skinned meshes. *Step 5 work list (Sonnet 5.5).* (a) Ported and checked on the sample (and on the full corpus where listed above): Update tangents and binormals, Update bounds, Search and replace assets, Convert to and from JSON, Universal tweaker, Universal fixer, Apply transformation, Adjust transformation, Attach parent NiNode, Remove nodes, Remove unused nodes, Convert block type, Unskin mesh, Set missing names, Fix 3DS exported KF, Merge properties, Add NiLODNode, Add bounding box, Add RootCollisionNode, Copy anim priorities, Remove controlled blocks, Quadratic to linear anim, Optimize Animations, Update Havok settings, Update Havok inertia, Update ragdoll constraint, Search for Havok material, Update shader flags, Real Time Reflections - NVSE, Check for errors (mesh checks), Transform information, Havok information, Find unwelded vertices, Find excessive draw calls, Find UVs, Vanilla Plus Particles - NVSE. (b) Owed checks and known differences: the full-corpus run of the other 28 default cases and of the 19 cases with other settings than `tangents-add` (`cargo xtask parity sniff` without `--sample`; the oracle results of the cases above are cached); `Remove nodes` on the 3 files above (upstream reads freed memory, not reproduced); the texture checks of `Check for errors` (which failed each `.dds` file then; done in step 5); Sniff's threads now and then write a file differently or fail it (`Unskin mesh`, `Remove nodes`, `Universal fixer`, `Convert to and from JSON`), which the harness runs alone. (c) Not ported: Copy geometry blocks (`ProcCopyGeometryBlocks`), Vertex color painting (`ProcVertexPaint`), Group shapes (`ProcGroupShapes`), Merge shapes (`ProcMergeShapes`), Copy anim controlled blocks (`ProcCopyControlledBlocks`), Add headtracking anim (`ProcAddHeadtrackingAnim`), Add facial anim (`ProcAddFacialAnim`), Add NiTransformData (`ProcJamAnim`), Weijiesen's blow up thing (`ProcWeiExplosion`) and Add blocks from skeleton (`ProcAnimSkeletonDeath`) for step 5; Optimize mesh (`ProcOptimize`) and Analyze mesh (`ProcAnalyzeMesh`), which need `wbMeshOptimize` (step 6); Find textures (`ProcFindTextures`), which needs `wbDDS` (step 2); Update MOPP code (`ProcMoppUpdate`), which calls `NifMopp.dll` (not ported); `ProcCollapseLinksArrays` is not registered in the 4.1.5q form. Owed from step 4 besides: (1) closed by parity #39 (PR #53): `xedit_core::delphi::same_value` is the RTL's `DoubleResolution` (`1E-12`) and `delphi::round`/`delphi::trunc` give the x64 integer indefinite for a NaN or an out-of-range value, which the record code (`wbFloatDef`, the `wbDefinitions*` callbacks, the universal tweaker), the script `Round`/`Trunc`/`Ceil`/`Floor` and the integer formatters all reach; the probes' SameValue boundary pairs and NaN/out-of-range cases equal the oracle; (2) `Search and replace assets` and the tweaker ran on the `regex` crate in place of PCRE (closed by the parity backlog's #29, which replaced it with the port's own PCRE engine: `crates/xedit-core/src/pcre.rs`); (3) `TStringList.Sort` and `AnsiSameText` compare with the user's locale through `CompareStringW` (`xedit_io::collate::ansi_compare_text`), which is the API the RTL calls; parity #39 probed the comparisons, the sorts and `LowerCase`/`UpperCase`/`AnsiLowerCase`/`AnsiUpperCase` under the machine's locale (English) and code page (1252), and other user locales cannot be driven without changing the machine's locale, so they are still owed; (4) the morph model comparison of `Check for errors` never runs upstream (its file name is empty) and was not ported; (5) closed by parity #39 (PR #53) as in (1); the script's `Round`/`Trunc`/`Ceil`/`Floor` of NaN and of out-of-range values and the `Math` unit's constants (`MinSingle`, `MinDouble`, `NaN`) were compared with the oracle at the byte level. This also settles step 3's owed `ArcTan2`. Step 6 (LODGen) landed on `phase-5-assets` (Opus 5.5, 2026-10-09): `wbMeshOptimize` with the Sniff `Optimize mesh` and `Analyze mesh` processors, the part of the Vampyre Imaging Library the texture atlases go through, `wbLOD` (`wbSplitTreeLOD` included), the process and archive helpers of `wbHelpers`, the `lodgen.generate` command (`xedit lodgen`) and `cargo xtask parity lodgen`, whose 20 cases (FO4, SSE, FNV, FO3 and Oblivion worldspaces; the atlas formats and sizes; the tree LOD with billboards and with 3D trees; the splits of the trees atlases) all compare equal to the GUI oracle, output files, export files and log. The oracle is driven through its LODGen form (the harness answers it and holds Shift so that the hidden `Split LOD Atlas` button shows), and the tree rotations are recovered from the oracle's block files because upstream seeds `Random` from the clock. Owed: (1) the full-corpus `parity sniff` run for `Optimize mesh` and `Analyze mesh` (only the `--sample 20` cases are equal so far); (2) `wbBuildAtlasFromAtlasMap` and the canvas gamma are reached only from scripts (phase 6, unchecked); (3) the FO4 export path and the `LODGenx64.exe` objects meshes are unreachable on vanilla data (the MNAM quirk of the 4.1.5q definitions); (4) the DX10 header is read as 14 bytes (UPSTREAM-QUIRK kept). Step 5 (Sniff processors) landed on `phase-5-assets` (Sonnet 5.5, 2026-10-09). It ports the ten remaining units of `Sniff/Proc` (Copy geometry blocks, Vertex color painting, Group shapes, Merge shapes, Copy anim controlled blocks, Add headtracking anim, Add facial anim, Add NiTransformData, Weijiesen's blow up thing, Add blocks from skeleton) as `crates/xedit-assets/src/sniff/proc_*.rs` on the pattern of step 4 (the controls of the frame are the fields of the processor with the `.dfm` defaults, `on_show` reads them, `on_start` checks them, `process_file` takes `&self`), `Find textures` (the DDS record of a texture archive entry, the header of a loose or archived file read to `MAX_HEADER_SIZE`, the filters of the value list editor, the report line and the `TwbDDSFile` header dump of `data_format_misc`), and the texture checks of `Check for errors` (`CheckDDS` and `CheckSSEDdsFormat` over `xedit_io::dds`; a `.dds` file is read while one of them is on, and one that is not a DDS is reported `Not a valid DDS file`). `Update MOPP code` (`NifMopp.dll`) and `ProcCollapseLinksArrays` (not registered in the 4.1.5q form) stay unported; 49 of the 50 operations of the form are ported and their settings are listed by `sniff list`, and the ledger, the map, the README and the `using-xedit-cli` skill are updated. *Harness.* A case that names `{source}` gets that folder made by the port itself over the unpacked files (the controlled block priorities of `Copy anim priorities`, the renamed controlled blocks of `Copy anim controlled blocks`, the scaled transforms, the renamed texture sets and the baked transforms of `Copy geometry blocks`), and `Add blocks from skeleton` gets an input folder with a `death.kf` of the archive and the `skeleton.nif` of its folder. 27 cases were added (the check has 91 now): copy-geometry 3, vertex-paint 5, group-shapes 2, merge-shapes 2, find-textures 4, copy-controlled, headtracking 2, facial 2, jam-anim 2, wei-explosion, skeleton-death 2 and check-errors-dds. *Results* (2026-10-09; equal / unchanged / oracle-failed, `--sample 20` over every game of the operation unless a game is named): vertex-paint 395/792, vertex-paint-adjust 675/1090, vertex-paint-adjust-add 675/1090, vertex-paint-remove 2/1763, vertex-paint-replace 358/1407; merge-shapes and merge-shapes-exact 0/1725/40; copy-geometry 2/8 (fo4), copy-geometry-transform 10 (tes4), copy-geometry-shader 104/30 (fnv, tes5), copy-controlled 130 (fo3, fnv); headtracking and headtracking-clamp 130 each, facial and facial-remove 130 each (fo3, fnv), jam-anim and jam-anim-translation 124/26 each (tes4, fo3, fnv); wei-explosion 0/274 (fo3, fnv); find-textures, find-textures-filter and find-textures-header 1881 unchanged each with the reports and the header dumps equal, find-textures-copy 881/1000 (fo4, sse, tes4), check-errors-dds 1939 unchanged with the messages of the checks equal (fo4, sse, tes5, tes4); every one of them with the logs equal. *Full corpus* (every archive of the games of an operation, no `--sample`; equal / unchanged / equal-error, the logs equal everywhere): wei-explosion 5/37876/37, headtracking 4444/4142, headtracking-clamp 2907/5679, facial 8586 and facial-remove 8586 (fo3, fnv), jam-anim 10125/918 and jam-anim-translation 10125/918, copy-controlled 8577/2466 (tes4, fo3, fnv), copy-geometry-transform 23549/3235, group-shapes 825/8700/87 and copy-geometry 4926/21858 (tes4, fo3). `merge-shapes` on Fallout 3 and New Vegas with `--threads 1` is 21 equal, 35865 unchanged and 2032 oracle-failed (the crash below, on every file it merges) and `check-errors-dds` over the Oblivion and Skyrim LE texture archives 51373 unchanged with the messages of the checks equal; `find-textures` over `Fallout4 - Textures1.ba2` is 3551 unchanged with the report of every file equal but for the `XBOX` marker below. `group-shapes-split` on Oblivion is 714 equal, 7231 unchanged and 87 equal-error with `--threads 1`: at the default thread count Sniff's threads lost the output of the 714 files, which the harness reports as `port-only-output` because there are more of them than the 40 it runs again alone (they compare equal alone). The step 6 ports this step verifies on the sample: optimize 171/191 (fo4, tes4), optimize-vertex-cache 117/201, optimize-overdraw 110/208, optimize-fetch 119/199, optimize-stripify 117/201, optimize-triangulate 318 unchanged, analyze-mesh and analyze-mesh-shapes 318 unchanged (fo4), all equal. Compared by hand outside the harness: the texture checks on five DDS files built for them (not a power of two, R5G6B5, X1R5G5B5, X4R4G4B4 and noise) report the same messages as `Sniff.exe`, the header dump of the Fallout 4 loose textures is the same text, `Add blocks from skeleton` writes the same `death.kf` for four archives (the Oblivion and Fallout meshes archives and Shivering Isles) and the outputs of `Copy geometry blocks` of the Skyrim and New Vegas samples are byte for byte the oracle's. *Fixes the oracle found.* `Vertex color painting` read `iAdjustMod` but kept its own default, so the add mode of the HSL adjustment was never taken (the 675 files of `vertex-paint-adjust-add` differed, the multiply case passed); Delphi's `Format` has no zero flag, so the `%04d` of the `Find textures` report line pads with spaces where the port wrote zeros; `Copy geometry blocks` raised an access violation for the 24 Skyrim and New Vegas meshes whose shader has no `Texture Set` element or one that links to nothing, which upstream checks; the harness matched only the exact `{source}` placeholder, so a case whose setting names a longer placeholder ran with an empty source directory (Sniff then waits on a dialog), and the first file of a prepared folder could be its `.complete` marker. *Known differences.* `Merge shapes`: `Sniff.exe` fails with an access violation (offset 604EFD, Read of address 0, the same when the file runs alone) on the files it merges, 40 of the sample (`outfit_go`, `outfitf` and `outfitm` of the FNV preorder packs and Fallout 3 armour and clutter meshes among them), where the port writes the file; one file of the Fallout 3 sample merged on both sides with equal bytes. `Add blocks from skeleton`: the harness case hangs `Sniff.exe` on the desktop the harness makes (the same command on the user's desktop takes a second), so the operation was compared by hand (above) and the case stays owed. `Find textures` on a texture archive: upstream's `prop` record is a local Delphi does not zero and its archive branch never assigns `XBOX`, so the report prints `  XBOX` on every line of `Fallout4 - Textures1.ba2` (228 lines) where the port prints none; the two line sets are equal once the marker is stripped, and the port reads the flag as false (`UPSTREAM-QUIRK` in `proc_find_textures.rs`). The single file mode of `Copy geometry blocks` (`bMatchingFiles=0`) hangs `Sniff.exe` as soon as a block of the source file matches one of the file (a dialog; one thread does it too), so it has no case and a unit test of the processor stands in (`the_single_file_mode_copies_the_transform`). *Owed.* (1) The full-corpus run of the cases not named above (vertex-paint and its four settings cases, group-shapes and group-shapes-split over the other games, copy-geometry on Fallout 4, copy-geometry-shader, merge-shapes on the remaining games, find-textures and its three settings cases over the other games, check-errors-dds over the other games; the sample runs listed above are cached); (2) the `Add blocks from skeleton` case run (see above); (3) the step 4 owed list (b), the other 28 default and 19 alt-settings cases without `--sample`, and step 6's owed full-corpus run of `Optimize mesh` and `Analyze mesh`; (4) `ProcCollapseLinksArrays` has no operation in the 4.1.5q form, so it stays unported by design. Step 7 (skills and docs) landed on `phase-5-assets` (2026-10-09, run on deepseek-flash in place of the suggested Sonnet 5.5): the skills `handling-archives` and `nif-batch-operations`, the README with the phase 5 status and the gate, the gate statement above, `coverage/ledger.toml` in the canonical key order of `cargo xtask sync` (the step 5 agent had left the field order unsynced) with `coverage/ledger.toml` and `upstream-map.toml` checked against what the steps ported, and the map notes that now name `lodgen.generate` for `xeLODGenForm.pas`, `xeInit.pas` and `xeMainForm.pas`. Final checks at the tip: `cargo fmt --check` and clippy `--workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` are clean, `cargo xtask check` passes, and `cargo test --workspace` passes but for the one pre-existing failure `copies_a_skyrim_npc_as_an_override` (a phase-4 issue: the order of a copied container array, not this phase's); no harness run was repeated for this step, the numbers above are the recorded runs of steps 1 to 6. The Fable review of the phase found two things, both fixed in this pull request: the stale `Core/wbHelpers.pas` map entry (four of its functions are ported since step 6, now named in the note) and `parity sniff` and `parity nif` returning success on a difference (they now exit with an error on a fatal outcome, with the oracle's own crashes and the outputs its threads lose non-fatal; the rule is in the `checking-parity` skill).

### Phase 6: Scripting

- **Model:** Fable 5.1 for the interpreter, because script compatibility is the most important goal and JvInterpreter semantics are undocumented. Sonnet 5.5 for the host bindings once the binding pattern exists.
- **Port:** A Pascal interpreter compatible with the JvInterpreter dialect, and all host registrations bound to `xedit-session` commands. Scripts that build VCL forms run against a form shim in the GUI and fail with a clear error when headless.
- **CLI:** `xedit script run|list|check`.
- **Skills:** `writing-xedit-scripts` for agents that need custom batch logic.
- **Gate:** All 150 shipped `.pas` scripts parse (`xEditAPI.pas` included). Every script that uses no form produces the same output and the same plugin bytes as the oracle.

Premises, corrected 2026-10-10 by reconnaissance before the phase started (the numbers in the phase stub above were estimates):

- *The corpus.* The `Edit Scripts` folder of the 4.1.5q oracle holds 172 entries: 150 `.pas`, 17 `.txt`, 4 `.exe` (`LODGen`, `LODGenx64`, `Texconv`, `Texconvx64`), 1 `.nif`, flat, no subdirectories; the 171 of the stub counted every entry. The parse corpus is the 150 `.pas`: 149 runnable scripts (the form's `_newscript_.pas` template among them) plus `xEditAPI.pas`, the declaration-only API reference the scripts `uses` (1,633 lines, 202 routines), which parses but never runs. 32 scripts use `TForm` or `ShowModal` and 11 add `InputQuery` or `InputBox` (the 40 of the first reconnaissance was the union of the two; re-measured by step 1 on 2026-10-10), so 110 are the headless parity corpus. The classification, with the line count of every file, is `crates/xtask/oracle/scripts/corpus.json`.
- *The interpreter source.* It is the `External/jvcl` submodule of the upstream clone (ElminsterAU/jvcl, branch `xEdit-4.1.x`, pinned at `dba248a2`), populated on 2026-10-10. Core units: `JvInterpreter.pas` (8,820 lines at the pin, re-measured by step 1 on 2026-10-10 against the 9,092 of the first reconnaissance; the Add* registration interface of `TJvInterpreterAdapter` is declared at :656-810 with `AddFunction` at :744, the identifier dispatch is `TJvInterpreterAdapter.GetValue` at :3939 and `TJvInterpreterUnit.CallFunction` at :8434), `JvInterpreterParser.pas` (818 lines, a hand-written tokenizer and recursive descent with no generator artifacts), `JvInterpreter_Types.pas` (170), `JvInterpreterConst.pas` (141), `JvInterpreterFm.pas` (755), `JvInterpreter_all.pas` (123), and the 14 registered JVCL units (`_System` 664, `_SysUtils` 2076, `_Classes` 1390, `_Dialogs` 1020, `_Windows` 311, `_Graphics` 1996, `_Controls` 1974, `_Buttons` 392, `_StdCtrls` 771, `_ComCtrls` 2946, `_ExtCtrls` 605, `_Forms` 1506, `_Menus` 653, `_Math` 478).
- *The registration count.* The 604 of the stub is re-derived from source: measured 2026-10-10, the 12 `xejviScriptAdapter*` adapter units (`xEdit/JvI/`) register 337 `AddFunction`, 245 `AddConst`, 231 `AddGet`, 59 `AddClass` (41 of them `TdfDef` classes), 36 `AddIGet`, 31 `AddSet`, 25 `AddISet`, 4 `AddRec`, 4 `AddRecGet`, 3 `AddHandler`, 3 `AddIDGet`, 2 `AddIDSet` and 1 `AddExtUnit`, 981 registrations in all, and the 14 JVCL units register 320 more `AddFunction` (295 of them outside comments). Step 1 re-measured these on 2026-10-10 (the first reconnaissance counted 11 units and read `AddRec`/`AddRecGet` as 7, `AddHandler`/`AddIDGet`/`AddIDSet` as 5 and the JVCL `AddFunction` as 313; a raw grep of the adapter units finds 341 `AddFunction` because 4 of them sit in lines commented out upstream, and the 5 commented-out registrations - those 4 and one `AddGet` - are the entries the ledger records as excluded). `coverage/ledger.toml` carries 986 script entries across the 12 units since step 1 (981 live: 337 function, 245 const, 232 get, 59 class, 36 iget, 31 set, 25 iset, 4 rec, 4 recget, 3 handler, 3 idget, 2 idset, 1 extunit; plus the 5 commented-out, excluded), of which 40 are covered by the phase 3 and 4 commands and 941 pending, which is the actual work list; `cargo xtask sync`'s registration regex missed some kinds (it found 598) and step 1 fixed it.
- *The integration.* `xEdit/xeScriptHost.pas` and `xEdit/JvI/xejviScriptHost.pas` compile a script (`uses` namespace stripping for `system.`/`vcl.`/`winapi.`/`data.`/`web.` prefixes, `OnSetValue`/`OnGetUnitSource`/`OnStatement` hooks, unit sources from `<scripts folder>\<Unit>.pas` or built-in empty stubs, `Compile`), and `xeMainForm.pas` runs it: `Initialize`, then `Process` per selected element through the filter hooks (`FunctionIsEmpty` skips empty ones), then `Finalize`; an exception prints `Aborted: Applying script` and the error location. The script's observable output is the files it writes, the message log, and the plugins saved when the application closes. The gate harness drives the oracle GUI hidden (the `-script:` switch, the desktop of `crates/xtask/src/parity/hidden.rs`) as phase 3 step 6 and phase 4 already do.

Steps:

1. **Corpus and premises** (Sonnet 5.5, first): `xedit script list` lists the scripts of `XEDIT_SCRIPTS` (default: the oracle's `Edit Scripts`), `xedit script check` parses one script (front end from step 2); the form/no-form classification, the `xEditAPI.pas` special case and the measured registration counts are recorded in the ledger and the map; `cargo xtask sync`'s script-registration regex is extended to every `Add*` kind so the 598 pending entries become the exact work list.
   - *Notes (branch `p6-step1`, 2026-10-10).* Landed: `script.list` (`crates/xedit-session/src/script.rs`: the folder of the request, else `XEDIT_SCRIPTS`, else `XEDIT_ORACLE_DIR\Edit Scripts`, else `<program>\Edit Scripts`; the `*.pas` of the top folder only, sorted as the form's list, each with its line count; read-only, no plugins loaded; tests in `crates/xedit-session/tests/script.rs`, the oracle one skipped without `XEDIT_ORACLE_DIR`), the CLI `xedit script list [--scripts DIR]`, the extended `script_registrations` of `cargo xtask sync` (the 13 `Add*` kinds the adapters use, the `'?'` unit and name of `AddClass`/`AddRec`, the record type and method of `AddRecGet`, a separate pattern for `AddExtUnit`, and Pascal comments stripped, so the two commented-out `AddHandler` of MiscRegEx and the five commented-out registrations are not counted), the regenerated `[script.*]` section of `coverage/ledger.toml` (598 entries before, 986 after: 981 live and the 5 commented-out ones marked `excluded` - `xejviScriptAdapterDF:TdfDef.Create:get` and the four `wbMD5*`/`wbSHA1*` of Resource - with 40 covered by the phase 3 and 4 commands and 941 pending; `cargo xtask check`: script 986 entries, 941 pending), the phase-6 notes of the 14 map entries (the 12 adapters, `xeScriptHost.pas` and `JvI/xejviScriptHost.pas`), the corpus file `crates/xtask/oracle/scripts/corpus.json` (150 scripts, 32 form, 11 dialogs, 110 headless, `xEditAPI.pas` marked `api`), and the corrections of the premises above (measured on 2026-10-10: `JvInterpreter.pas` is 8,820 lines at the pin, not 9,092; the adapters are 12 units, not 11; `AddRec`/`AddRecGet` are 4 and 4, `AddHandler`/`AddIDGet`/`AddIDSet` 3, 3 and 2, `AddExtUnit` 1; the JVCL units register 320 `AddFunction`, not 313; the dialog scripts are 11, not 40). Owed: `script.check` (step 2) and `script.run` (step 4), and the ledger's `script` entries stay `pending` until the adapter steps cover them. The 14 JVCL units of step 8 are not in the ledger at all: `cargo xtask sync` scans `xEdit/JvI/` only (the jvcl submodule is outside the tracked unit directories), so step 8 needs the sync extended to `External/jvcl/jvcl/run/JvInterpreter_*.pas` or its own list (the 320 `AddFunction`, 295 of them outside comments).
2. **Interpreter front end** (Fable 5.1): the new crate `xedit-script`; `JvInterpreterParser.pas` ported as-is (tokenizer, expressions, statements, routine headers, `uses`; its own token stream, not the xtask transpiler's); `xedit script check` parses the whole corpus plus every unit the scripts `uses`, and all 150 parse.
  *Notes (branch p6-step2, 2026-10-10)*: landed `crates/xedit-script` (`interpreter_parser.rs` = `JvInterpreterParser.pas` as-is incl. the `PaTokenizeTag` hash tables; `interpreter.rs` + `ast.rs` = the front end of `JvInterpreter.pas` as an AST builder, its lowering interface documented on the AST for step 3; `check.rs` = the `uses` resolution and namespace stripping of `xejviScriptHost.pas`) and `xedit script check` (registry `script.check`, read-only, non-zero exit listing `file:line: message`; units resolve from the scripts folder, `XEDIT_SCRIPTS` else `<XEDIT_ORACLE_DIR>\Edit Scripts`). Gate run 2026-10-10: **150 of 150 `.pas` of the 4.1.5q `Edit Scripts` parse, 0 fail** (`xedit script check` over the folder, exit 0; the corpus test `crates/xedit-script/tests/corpus.rs` asserts the same; 35 unit/integration tests pass). What the run taught, kept for steps 3 and 10: **`check` mirrors `Compile`, which skips routine bodies** (`FindToken(ttBegin); SkipToEnd`), so shipped scripts with statement-level errors inside bodies (e.g. the missing paren in `Assets manager.pas`) still compile — `interpreter` exposes both the compile acceptance (`parse_compile`) and the full statement grammar (`parse`) the evaluator runs; `InterpretStatement` accepts empty `;`/`end`/`else` statements (empty `then` branches are in the corpus); `ReadFunctionHeader` requires an identifier before the result type (`array` results do not parse); `xEditAPI.pas` needs type aliases, enums and header directives/defaulted parameters, which `InterpretType`/`ReadParams` themselves do not accept (documented front-end extensions); `GetLineByPos` counts `#13` only (LF-only files report line 1). Owed for step 3: the evaluator over the AST (values, types, name resolution, `var`/`out` calls, the `for`/`case` 64-bit semantics) and the error-location plumbing.
3. **Interpreter runtime** (Fable 5.1): the `JvInterpreter.pas` semantics: the value model and types (`JvInterpreter_Types.pas`), the adapter registration model in full (`AddFunction`, `AddGet`, `AddSet`, `AddIGet`, `AddISet`, `AddClass`, `AddConst`, `AddHandler`, `AddRec*`), the unit namespaces with the sorted-list dispatch, routine calls with `var`/`out` parameters, `CallFunction`/`CallFunctionEx`, exceptions with the error location (`GetLastErrorLocation`), the statement hook, and the dialect quirks the compatibility decision keeps (the `for`/`case` 64-bit integer behaviour of `whatsnew.md`, the visible `Integer required` message).
  *Notes (branch p6-step3, 2026-10-10)*: landed the runtime in `crates/xedit-script`: `values.rs` (the `Variant` value model of `JvInterpreter.pas` -- the extended variant types, `JvInterpreterVarAsType`/`Var2Type`, the copy and assignment helpers, `V2O` ... `P2R`, `S2V`/`V2S`, `TypeName2VarTyp`, the array record and `Expression`'s variant operators with the `varSet` left-operand union/difference and the raw pointer compares), `adapter.rs` (`TJvInterpreterAdapter` in full -- every `Add*` of the step list, `CheckArgs`/`FindFunDesc`, `VarList` with the typed `SetValue` coercion, `TJvInterpreterFunctionDesc` with the lazy `ExecFunction` body cache, and `GetValue`/`SetValue`/`GetElement`/`SetElement` in upstream order with the `AnsiStrIComp` sorted `Find` including the method-list derived-class-first tie-break), and `eval.rs` (`compile` over `parse_compile` with `ReadUnit`, the unit-source hook and the cycle guard; `exec_function` with the `InFunction` parameter copies and the `LeaveFunction` write-back; the statement walk on the flag model, so `Break`/`Continue`/`Exit` cross a call boundary as upstream; `InterpretRaise`/`try` with the `DoFinallyExcept` swallow rules; `UpdateExceptionPos`/`last_error_location`; the `OnStatement` hook at `DoOnStatement`'s exact points, the statement separators included). `Interpreter::call_function`/`call_function_ex`/`run_main` are the step-4 seam and the adapter is a public field. `interpreter.rs` gained `parse_body` (with `ast::RoutineBody` and `RoutineDecl::body_pos`) so a statement error of a shipped script surfaces at first execution, as upstream, and `error.rs` gained the rest of the `RsEInterpreter*` texts and the `ieErrorPos`/`ieExternal` message builders. Tests: 39 integration tests in `tests/eval.rs` (arithmetic and conversions, `for`/`while`/`repeat`/`case`, `var`/const/defaulted parameter modes and the `out` prefix, recursion, `with` refuted, arrays and records including `AddRec`, exceptions with their positions, an adapter-written `var` parameter, the sorted dispatch, the `for`/`case` 64-bit quirks, the statement hook, `CallFunctionEx`, and three 4.1.5q corpus scripts executed verbatim) plus 9 unit tests in `adapter.rs`/`values.rs`; `cargo test -p xedit-script` gives 17 + 39 + 26 + 1 green and `xedit script check` stays 150/150. Owed: step 4 wires `script run`; the RTTI form/event surface is step 9; `external` DLL calls go through `Adapter::external_call` (host seam) and `AddIntfGet`/OLE stay out (no `varUnknown`; OLE is compiled out upstream).
4. **Host and run loop** (Sonnet 5.5, after 2 and 3): `xeScriptHost.pas` and `xejviScriptHost.pas` ported: the `uses` namespace stripping, unit sources from the scripts folder with the built-in stub set, `Compile`, the `Initialize`/`Process`/`Finalize` loop with `FunctionIsEmpty` and the filter hooks (`ScriptProcessElements`, `ScriptProcessSignatures`, the filter variables), the `Aborted: Applying script` error text; `xedit script run` and the `-script:` legacy switch and `tmScript` tool mode (`tool_modes.rs`'s seam) run it.
   - *Notes (branch `p6-step4`, 2026-10-10).* Landed: `crates/xedit-script/src/host.rs` (`Script` = `TxejviScript`: create with `check::strip_namespaces`, the `OnGetUnitSource` hook resolving `xEditAPI`, `UITypes` and `HOST_UNITS` to the `unit <name>; end.` stub and everything else to `<folder>\<Unit>.pas`, else `ieUnitNotFound`; `function_exists`, `function_is_empty` (the exact source-text scan, incl. the six accepted bodies), `call_function` with `VResult`, `last_error_location`; and the `TxeScriptHost` name registry -- `DEFAULT_SCRIPT_HOST`/`host_supported`/`init`/`initialized_host`/`create_script`/`reset_host` with upstream's three texts; 6 integration tests in `crates/xedit-script/tests/host.rs`, 2 unit tests). `crates/xedit-session/src/script.rs`: the run loop of `xeMainForm.pas` -- `DoRunScript` (the script resolved against the scripts folder, `wbScriptsPath := the script's folder`, every root node selected), `ApplyScript` (`Initialize`/`Process`/`Finalize` guarded by `FunctionExists` and `FunctionIsEmpty`, the `Script terminated itself, Result=<n>` of a non-zero result with the walk stopping, the `Mode: Silent` line, the `Exception in unit <u> line <n>: [<class>] <message>` + `Aborted: Applying script "<name>"` of a failure and no closing line, `Done: ..., Processed Records: n, Elapsed Time: mm:ss`, `You can close this application now.`, and the `SaveChanged` of an auto mode), `ApplyScriptToSelection` (the per-root reverse walk, the `wbProgressLock` around a `Process` call), `ScriptCanProcessElement`/`ScriptProcessSingleElement`, `vstNavInitNode`'s element children (a main record's node container is its `ChildGroup`), the `wbProgressLock`/`wbShowStartTime`/`wbHideStartTime` model with the `[mm:ss]` prefixes, `wbFormatElapsedTime`, and the `ScriptSelection` seam (`files` + `selection`, default `NavigationSelection`) the adapter steps 5-9 bind, with `ScriptElement`/`ScriptRunState::element_value`/`element_of` as the element-as-`IInterface` value convention (a `Value::Object` of class `IwbElement`) and `set_variable` as the whole `OnSetValue` switch (`ScriptProcessElements`, `ScriptProcessSignatures`, `wbOutputPath`, `dfFloatDecimalDigits`, the 36 filter fields) . Bound on top (the map's "step 4 for the session side" of `xejviScriptAdapterHost.pas`): the message log (`AddMessage`, `ClearMessages`), the paths and game names (14 functions over `xedit-core::interface::globals`, which gained `wbScriptsPath`/`wbTempPath`/`wbOutputPath`), the file slice (`FileCount`, `FileByIndex`, `FileByName` and, from `xejviScriptAdapterFile.pas`/`Element.pas`, `GetFileName`, `MasterCount`, `MasterByIndex`, `Name` incl. `TwbFile.GetName`'s `[<id>] <name>`/hardcoded case) and the filter (`ApplyFilter` -> `filter.apply` with the recorded variables, `FilterApplied`, `RemoveFilter`), plus the 33 `et*`/`ca*`/`ct*` constants the hooks name; 60 ledger entries set to `covered` (`script.run`), 16 integration tests in `crates/xedit-session/tests/script.rs` (the loop and the derived selection order incl. the file headers, the `begin Result := 0; end` skip, the self-termination, the planted error with the exact `Aborted` text and the missing closing line, `[etFile]`, the signature filter and its four-character error, the filter hooks, the tool mode + legacy `-script:`/`-S:` parse, the dry run, the compile failure and `Could not open script:`, a `uses` unit beside the script, and the two corpus scripts `List loaded plugins and their masters.pas` + `Apply filter for cleaning.pas` end to end, run when `XEDIT_ORACLE_DIR` is set). CLI: `xedit script run <file> [--scripts DIR] [--no-save] [--dry-run]` (registry `script.run`, mutating; prints the message log, exits 1 when aborted), `tool.run`'s `script` mode with `--script`/`--scripts`, and the legacy `-script:`/`-S:` read into `LegacyRun.script`/`scripts_folder` (two fields; `-S:` no longer ignored) with `run_legacy` passing both. Gates: `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo test --workspace` (all green; `xedit-script` 91, `xedit-session` script.rs 16) and `cargo xtask check` (script 986 entries, 881 pending; units 157, 81 pending). No parity number is claimed here: the oracle comparison of the run loop is step 10's. Confirmed against the oracle's own log of a step-10 run (`xedit-parity-scratch-p6-step10/xedit-4.1.5q/oracle-work/run-Worldspace displacement.pas.<key>/bin/SSEScript_log.txt`): `[mm:ss] Start: Applying script \"<name>\"`, `[mm:ss] Done: Applying script \"<name>\", Processed Records: <n>, Elapsed Time: mm:ss`, `You can close this application now.` (no prefix) and `[mm:ss] Saving: <name>` after it are the same texts and the same order; the elapsed origin differs (the oracle counts from the load's first long action, the port from the run's start) and the port logs the file name where the oracle logs the `.save.<timestamp>` name its queued rename writes. Owed to step 10/next: an oracle log of the abort path (the port's `Exception in unit ...` + `Aborted:` lines are untested against it), the `Processed Records` of a run whose `Process` is missing (the port always prints the count) and the save path itself (a script cannot change a plugin before steps 5-9 bind the element API, so the `Saving:` line is written but not exercised); the `wbCurrentProgress` of a run with no `Process`; a routine body terminated by the unit's `.` (`function P: integer; begin end.`) is refused by the front end (`';' expected but '.' found`), which makes the two `end.` variants of `FunctionIsEmpty` unreachable; `xEditAPI.pas` is stubbed at run time even when the folder holds it, as upstream, but the host reads unit files as bytes (no `TStringList.LoadFromFile` encoding detection); `FilterScripted` (the scripted filter function) is recorded and unused; `wbSettingsFileName`, `wbLoadBSAs`, `wbRecordDefMap`, `wbSelectedFiles`, `JumpTo`, `AddNewFile`/`AddNewFileName`, `RecordByFormID`, the conflict helpers and the form/LOD/atlas functions of `xejviScriptAdapterHost.pas` stay pending (steps 5-9 and the GUI phase), as do the element/record/container/data-format/misc/JVCL surfaces, so a corpus script that uses them still aborts with `Undeclared Identifier`; `-scripthost:` is not read (only `JvI` exists).
5. **Adapters: element, record, file, container, resource** (Sonnet 5.5, after 4): the five `xejviScriptAdapter*` units (2,373 lines, the `wbInterface`/`wbImplementation` API) bound to the session commands and the element tree.
  *Notes (branch p6-step5, 2026-10-10)* -- landed: `crates/xedit-session/src/script_units/` (one module per ported unit -- `element.rs` 54 functions, `record.rs` 49, `file.rs` 36, `container.rs` 22, `resource.rs` 44 live -- plus `mod.rs` with the shared helpers: the argument casts of the Pascal `Supports` calls, the `TwbVector`/`TwbGridCell` record types (`Adapter::add_rec` is now idempotent by name so steps 5, 7 and 9 can register the same record type once), the `TStrings` and `TList` values and their classes, and the native-value conversion). `crates/xedit-script` gained `strings.rs` (the `TStrings`/`TStringList` value: the `Sorted`/`Duplicates` add semantics of Delphi, `Create`/`Add`/`AddObject`/`Count`/`Strings`/`Text`/`Clear`/`Delete`/`IndexOf`, and the `TwbFastStringList` child the resource unit registers) and `lists.rs` (the `TList` `wbGetSiblingRecords`/`wbFindREFRsByBase` fill); the adapter functions take them through `ScriptObject::string_list`/`list`, so a later step that ports the JVCL `Classes` unit extends the same classes (`Adapter::find_class`) instead of registering a second `TStringList`/`TList`. `xedit-core` gained the ports of `TwbMainRecord.GetEditValue`'s FormID text, `SetVersion`, `SetFormVCS1/2` (with `GetFormVCS1/2`), `ChangeFormSignature`, `GetIsInjected`, `GetIsInitiallyDisabled`/setter, `GetPosition`/`GetRotation` (they were missing where `SetPosition` existed), `TwbGroupRecord.GetMainRecordByEditorID` (and `find_child_group` is public now), the `wbHelpers.pas` pair `wbStringToSignatures`/`wbGetSiblingRecords`, `ElementBase::set_state` and `Element::begin_update_value`/`end_update_value`/`get_last_element`/`index_of_id`/`get_index_of`; `xedit_io::archive::asset` already had `TwbAsset`, so `AssetTypeByExtension`/`ByFolder`/`wbNormalizeResourceName` are three lines each. Ledger: 203 of the 207 entries of the five units are `covered` with `command = "script.run"` (the four commented-out `wbMD5*`/`wbSHA1*` stay `excluded`; `cargo xtask check`: script 986 entries, 719 pending, from 881 -- the 162 new plus the 41 already covered), and `upstream-map.toml`'s five entries are `ported`. Tests: `crates/xedit-session/tests/script_adapters.rs` (9, all green; the corpus ones skip without `XEDIT_ORACLE_DIR`): synthetic scripts that print and assert every getter family over a two-plugin SSE load order (element, container, record/override, file and resource functions) and two that assert the *saved* bytes (a renamed editor ID and a value) and three 4.1.5q corpus scripts end to end -- `List null FormID.pas` (asserts the `FullPath` line it prints for a `01000000` record), `Check for invalid alternate textures.pas` (the element walk with `ResourceExists`) and `Skyrim - Add shadow bias to lights.pas` (asserts the `XLIG` subrecord with 23.76 at the shadow depth bias of the saved plugin). Corpus sweep with the CLI over the 109 headless scripts of the 4.1.5q folder and a synthetic pair of SSE plugins: 39 run to completion (`Done:`/`You can close this application now.`), up from ~10 before this step; the rest abort on identifiers of steps 7 to 9 (`SysUtils`'s `IntToStr`/`Format`/`StringOfChar`/`Pos`/`Pred`/`SameText`/`BoolToStr`/`IntToHex`, `Classes`' `TMemIniFile`, the dialogs' `InputQuery`/`mtConfirmation`, `Assigned`, `TwbFastStringList` before this step, `wbSimpleRecords`, `TPerlRegEx`) or on `xEditAPI.pas` names that are not registered upstream either (`GetIsEditable`), which is what step 8 closes. Owed, each an upstream behaviour the port keeps with what exists (the ledger key stays covered): `AssignTemplate*`/`TemplateAssign` (the port's definitions carry no `IwbDef.GetAssignTemplates`), `MoveUp`/`MoveDown`/`CanMoveUp`/`CanMoveDown` (the view's sort-order machinery), the view element states (`esSortKeyValid`, `esHidden`, `esParentHidden*`, `esTagged`, `esResolving`, `esNotSuitableToAddTo` -- the port models none of them, so they are never in a set and setting one is a no-op), `ContainerStates` and `IsSorted` (the creation/sort states live on the implementation and the phase-7 tree reads them), `InsertElement` (no `Container` method for an insert), `SetEditValue` of a main record (read as the FormID text, writes nothing), `FileWriteToStream` (the `Classes` unit's `TStream`, step 8), the DDS `wbDDS*ToBitmap` and `wbFlipBitmap`/`wbAlphaBlend` (the VCL `TBitmap`/DCs, step 9), `wbFormIDErrorCheckLock`/`Unlock` (the port validates on every write, no threadvar counter: both report 0), and the four `wbMD5*`/`wbSHA1*` (`excluded`, commented out upstream). Findings for the coordinator and the later steps, all outside these units: `twbMainRecord` elements of the port include the `Record Header` child (upstream keeps the header outside `Elements`), so `ElementCount(e)` is one larger and paths `Record Header\...` resolve directly; `FullPath` of the port has no `[position]` where upstream writes one (`GetIndexedPath` does write them -- my port of it does); a native value of a single-precision field comes back as a double of the raw `f32` (`3.49999999116335` where Delphi's `Single` prints `3.5`); an indexed *property* read of an object (`sl.Strings[0]`) is not dispatched by the interpreter (`check_args` sees no arguments and reports `Not enough parameters`; the call form `sl.Strings(0)` works), which matters because the corpus uses `sl[i]`/`sl.Strings[i]` and `TStringList` is step 8's; `Undeclared Identifier 'Assigned'` (step 7) makes `if not Assigned(x) then begin ... end` report `Not enough parameters` for a *global* variable where a local one reports the undeclared name; `SetESL.pas` fails the body parse at `except on Exception do` with two statements (`'end' expected but 'Result' found`), so the port's front end does not accept a multi-statement `on ... do` handler body; and `Skyrim - Find uncompressed records.pas` reaches `Process` with the *master* record of an overridden record only (the tree's node element is the group's first record, as upstream's `vstNavInitNode` does), so its `IsMaster(e)` guard skips every record the synthetic case had -- worth a real oracle case in step 10 to settle `IsMaster`'s meaning for the walk.
   *Notes (branch p6-step5b, 2026-10-10).* The fixups for the step-5 findings, each verified against the 4.1.5q pin (and, where the source was ambiguous, against the oracle's own script output through a temporary `parity script` probe on `Skyrim.esm`, since removed): **(1)** the indexed property of a `TStrings` dispatches: `Strings` is registered as the indexed pair `AddIGet`/`AddISet` and the default `AddIDGet`/`AddIDSet` (`JvInterpreter_Classes.pas:1268-1271`, dispatch `JvInterpreter.pas:4462-4475`, `:4875-4889`), so `sl.Strings[i]`, `sl[i]` and their writes behave like the oracle's (`sl0=alpha`, `slstr=beta`, `sl0b=gamma`); the plain `sl.Strings(0)` call form is gone, as upstream (no plain get of the name exists). **(2)** `FullPath` writes the upstream segments: per level `' \ '` + `'[<index>] '` + name and the `[<load order>] ` file prefix at the root, through the new `Element::get_full_path_name` (`wbImplementation.pas:19641-19652`, `TwbFile.GetName` `:4853-4859`); the oracle prints ` \ [00] Skyrim.esm \ [1] GRUP Top "GMST" \ [1582] fSunXExtreme [GMST:00012FC9]` and the port produces the same shape; command `path` fields and path-bearing error messages carry it too. **(3)** The `Record Header` finding is refuted with oracle evidence: the header is a child element upstream (`TwbElement.Create` adds every child, `:19316-19322`; `TwbMainRecord.Init` passes the record as its container, `:10453-10465`) and `ElementCount` counts it (`:7664-7669`); the oracle probe prints `count=3` and `by0=Record Header`, matching the port -- no code change. **(4)** The single-precision native finding is refuted: the oracle prints `399.999999990097` for a `400.0` game setting -- the ported `RoundToEx` six-digit round through the single-precision `10^digit` factor is upstream behaviour (`wbInterface.pas:16789-16796`, `:5499-5505`) -- no code change; a regression test pins the port's `3.49999999116335` for the synthetic `3.5`. **(5)** The `Assigned` finding is not reproducible: at the pre-step-7 tip every shape -- local, global and an `IInterface` argument inside `Process` -- reports `Undeclared Identifier 'Assigned'` (upstream's `ieUnknownIdentifier` path, `JvInterpreter.pas:6128`; a 19-script corpus sweep over every script that calls `Assigned` shows the same), so the `Not enough parameters` symptom never occurs; the argument/name path is pinned by tests that stub the registration (`a_global_variable_argument_passes_through_assigned`, `a_local_variable_argument_passes_through_assigned`, `a_global_interface_argument_passes_through_assigned_in_process`), which step 7's real binding replaces -- no code change. **(6)** The front end accepts a multi-statement `except on ... do` handler as upstream: `On1` interprets its first statement and skips the rest of the section (`:7496-7500`), and a clause that does not match reports `'end' expected` at the second statement's token (`:7520-7550`), which `DoFinallyExcept` swallows (`:7586-7594`); `SetESL.pas` parses through the full grammar (`the_setesl_multi_statement_handler_parses`) and `xedit script check` stays 150/150. New finding outside these fixes, owed: the record name inside the oracle's `FullPath` is `fSunXExtreme [GMST:00012FC9]` where the port renders `GMST - Game Setting [00012FC9] <fSunXExtreme>` -- the oracle-vs-port `GetShortNameInternal` naming difference needs its own step.
   *Handover (branch p6-step5b, checkpoint, 2026-10-10).* Per-defect status at the checkpoint: **(1) fixed + tested** -- the indexed `TStrings` registration (IGet/ISet/IDGet/IDSet); `script_adapters` covers `sl.Strings[i]`/`sl[i]` reads and the `sl[0] :=` write, and the oracle probe confirmed those exact behaviours. **(2) fixed + tested** -- upstream `FullPath` in the `element_common!`/flag bodies with `Element::get_full_path_name`; the five synthetic `full=` expectations and the corpus `List null FormID.pas` case pass in the port, the format matches the oracle probe, and `parity conflicts --game sse` re-ran green at the tip (equal: 11 files, 1,188,933 records, 20,782 listed, 0 different); `parity dump --game sse --file update.esm` was started and was still running at the checkpoint (the first attempt died building against the tree mid-rebase; left for the phase review to collect). **(3) refuted with oracle evidence**, no code change (oracle probe: `count=3`, `by0=Record Header`; upstream `TwbElement.Create` :19316-19322, `TwbMainRecord.Init` :10453-10465, `TwbContainer.GetElementCount` :7664-7669). **(4) refuted with oracle evidence**, no code change (oracle probe: `native=399.999999990097` for a `400.0` setting; the port's `3.49999999116335` for `3.5` is the same arithmetic, pinned by `the_native_value_of_a_single_float_matches_the_oracle`). **(5) not reproducible at the pre-step-7 tip, no code change** -- local, global and `IInterface`-in-`Process` arguments all name the then-unregistered identifier (JvInterpreter.pas:6128) and a 19-script corpus sweep concurs; the argument path is pinned by three tests stubbing the registration (`a_global_variable_argument_passes_through_assigned`, `a_local_variable_argument_passes_through_assigned`, `a_global_interface_argument_passes_through_assigned_in_process`), which step 7's real binding supersedes. **(6) fixed + tested** -- `parse_except` accepts the extra statements of an on-handler and skips them after the first (`:7496-7500`), `interpret_except` reports `'end' expected` for the unmatched case (`:7520-7550`), `SetESL.pas` parses through the full grammar (`the_setesl_multi_statement_handler_parses`) and `xedit script check` stays 150/150. Gates at the rebased tip (31f167a, on step 7): `cargo fmt --all --check` clean, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings` clean, `cargo test --workspace` all green (0 failures); `cargo xtask check` re-run in flight when this note was written (its pre-rebase run was green). The incidental record-name observation above is left for the phase review, not opened as a defect.
6. **Adapter: data format** (Sonnet 5.5, after 4): `xejviScriptAdapterDF` (992 lines, the 107 ledger entries owed by phase 5 step 3) over `xedit-assets`.
   *Notes (branch p6-step6, 2026-10-10).* Landed: `crates/xedit-session/src/script_df.rs`, the whole `xejviScriptAdapterDF.pas` unit over `xedit-assets` -- 173 of its 174 ledger entries bound and marked `covered` (`script.run`); the one left is `TdfDef.Create`, commented out upstream at line 767 and `excluded` in the ledger. Bound are the 41 classes of the `TdfDef`/`TdfElement` hierarchies (with the upstream parents: `TdfContainer` under `TdfElement`, `TdfStruct`/`TdfArray`/`TdfUnion` under it, `TwbNifFile` under `TdfContainer`, the value classes under `TdfValue`, `TdfMappedIntegerDef` under `TdfIntegerDef` and so on), the 25 `dft*`/`nfo*`/`nf*` constants (the ordinals of `TdfDataType`, `TwbNifOption` and `TwbNifVersion`), `dfFloatToStr`/`dfStrToFloat`/`dfCalcHash`, every `TdfDef` and `TdfElement` property and method (the indexed `Items`/`Elements`/`NativeValues`/`EditValues` readers and the two indexed writers, the default indexed property `el[i]` -> `Items[i]`, `LoadFromData`/`LoadFromFile`/`LoadFromJSONFile`/`LoadFromResource`, `SaveToFile`/`SaveToJSONFile`, `SetToDefault`, `Assign`, `FromJSON`/`ToJSON`/`ToText`, the tree methods), `TwbNiRef.Template`/`Ptr`, the 22 `TwbNifBlock` methods (`BlockType`, `NifFile`, `Refs`/`Strings`, `IsNiObject`, `AddChild`/`AddExtraData`/`AddProperty`, the `...ByName`/`...ByType` finders, `UpdateBounds`/`UpdateNormals`/`UpdateTangents`, the deprecated `GetAssetsList` that answers nil upstream) and the 23 `TwbNifFile` members (`Create`, `NifVersion`, `Options` as the two-member set, `InternalUpdates`, `Header`/`Footer`, `BlocksCount`, `Blocks`, `AddBlock`/`InsertBlock`/`ConvertBlock`/`CopyBlock`, `BlockByType`/`BlocksByType`/`BlockByName` and the four `Spell*`), plus the `Create` of the eight file classes (`TwbBGSMFile`, `TwbBGEMFile`, `TwbLODTreeLSTFile`, `TwbLODTreeBTTFile`, `TwbLODSettingsFO3File`, `TwbLODSettingsTES5File`, `TwbFUZFile`, `TwbDDSFile`). A DF object is a `DfElement` wrapper (a document `DfDoc` holding the phase 5 `Tree`, plus the element index) or a `DfDefObject` (the static definition); the script class of an element is derived from the port's element class and definition kind, so the dispatch sees the upstream hierarchy. `LoadFromResource` binds upstream's `dfResourceGetDataCallback` to `xedit_core::container_handler::open_resource_data`, with the two `Resource not found` texts; `LoadFromFile`/`SaveToFile` read and write the file directly. The `TList`/`TStrings` a `GetAssetsList` or a `...ByType` call fills go through `script::ScriptLists` on the run state (the JVCL `Classes` unit, step 8, sets the two closures; the five entries carry a ledger note saying so). *Runtime fix.* The indexed assignment of the interpreter (`eval.rs`'s `assign_target`) passed the *indexed* arguments to its probe read of `obj.Prop[index] := value`; upstream's `InternalSetValue` (`JvInterpreter.pas:6988`) reads with a bare receiver (`MyArgs`, no index, `Indexed` false), which is what lets a property registered as an indexed setter alone (`AddISet`: `NativeValues[...]`, `EditValues[...]`) reach its writer -- with the indexed probe the port found the matching reader, took the string-index arm and raised `Type mismatch`. The probe and the write-back now use the bare arguments; `cargo test -p xedit-script` (all green) and the DF tests cover it. *Tests.* Six unit tests in `script_df.rs` (the constants against the enums, the three helper functions, a NIF built, read and written through the registered surface -- `AddBlock`, `AddChild`, `Refs`/`LinksTo`, `Items[i]`, `el[i]`, `Def`/`DataType` -- the tree walk with the `Free` refusal, the `...ByType` list seam with a test sink and the empty trees of the eight file classes), and two `script.run` integration tests in `crates/xedit-session/tests/script.rs`: one script builds a NIF (SSE), sets `EditValues['Name']`, writes it with `SaveToFile`, dumps and reloads it with `SaveToJSONFile`/`LoadFromJSONFile`, writes and reloads a DDS header, and loads a written NIF through `LoadFromResource` (a folder container) while a missing resource aborts with upstream's `Resource not found (File "...")`; the other (gated on `XEDIT_ORACLE_DIR`) walks the corpus mesh `Edit Scripts\LODGen_flat_lod.nif` -- 6 blocks with their exact types, `Name` of the `NiNode`, `IsNiObject` for the node and the shape, the header magic, and the `el[0].EditValue` texture pattern of `Assets manager.pas`. Of the ten corpus scripts that touch the DF surface, seven build a form (`Assets manager.pas`, `Assets browser.pas`, `FUZer.pas`, the four `NIF - Batch`/`Convert OBJ` scripts) and the three form-free ones need other steps' units first -- `DDS - Find textures without mipmaps.pas` wants `TDirectory.GetFiles` (step 7) and `IncludeTrailingBackslash`/`Copy`/`Length` (step 8), `LOD Statistics.pas` the element and resource API (step 5: `GetElementEditValues`, `Signature`, `WinningOverride`, `wbNormalizeResourceName`, `ResourceExists`) plus `IntToStr`/`Format`/`TStringList` (step 8) and a `TSaveDialog` (step 9), `NIF - List blocks references.pas` a `TOpenDialog`, `TStringList` and `TList` and is interactive besides -- so no corpus DF script runs headlessly at this tip; the same calls run in the purpose-written scripts of the two tests above, and the corpus runs are step 10's. Gates at the tip: `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo test --workspace` (597 tests, 0 failures) and `cargo xtask check` (script 986 entries, 708 pending; units 157 tracked, 80 pending). *Owed.* (1) The `TList`/`TStrings` seam is unfilled until step 8 registers the `Classes` unit (`ChildrenByType`/`PropertiesByType`/`ExtraDatasByType` and the file `GetAssetsList` then work; the binding is unit-tested with a test sink). (2) `O2V(nil)` is `Unassigned` in the port where upstream carries a `varObject` with a nil pointer (both read as not assigned; the corpus scripts only pass such values on). (3) `Free` of the root releases the document (a later call fails with `The file has been freed`) where upstream is a use-after-free; `Free` of a non-root element does nothing (upstream detaches it and leaves a dangling pointer in its parent). (4) A raised DF error prints `[EJvInterpreterError]` as its class in the run log where the GUI prints the raised class (`[Exception]`) -- the step-3/4 external-error path, owed to `cargo xtask parity script` (step 10) together with the rest of the abort path. (5) `LoadFromResource` reads through the container handler, which every load now fills as the GUI's loader does (closed by issue #32: `parity resources` runs a probe script of the `Resource*` functions in the oracle's script mode and in the port through the same command line, 0 differences); the `wbLoadBSAs` script function itself is issue #25's. (6) A `LoadFromFile`/`SaveToFile` OSError carries the crate's `Cannot open file "<path>". <os error>` text where the GUI raises `RaiseLastOSError`'s bare text. No parity number is claimed here: the oracle comparison of the run loop and of these scripts is step 10's.
7. **Adapters: misc family** (Sonnet 5.5, after 4): `xejviScriptAdapterMisc` (938 lines), `MiscJson` (649), `MiscRegEx` (290), `MiscVcl` (696) and `xejviScriptAdapter.pas`'s own registrations (constants, `wbVector`, `wbGridCell`, `Assigned`, `FullPathToFilename`).
   *Notes (branch p6-step7, 2026-10-10).* Landed the misc family of the script API, with all 413 of its ledger entries set to `covered` / `command = "script.run"` (99 of `xejviScriptAdapter.pas`, 166 of `Misc`, 100 of `MiscJson`, 48 of `MiscRegEx`; `cargo xtask check` at the rebased tip, with step 6's data format adapter also landed: script 986 entries, 295 pending, against 881 before the two steps) and the four units marked `ported` in `upstream-map.toml` with their `rust` paths and the divergence lists. New modules: `crates/xedit-session/src/script_classes.rs` (the one shared `AddClass` holder -- `TStrings`/`TStringList`/`TEncoding` from step 8's JVCL units, the three `JsonDataObjects` classes and `TPerlRegEx`; a later step extends this struct instead of registering a second class of the same name, because `AddGet` answers a receiver by class identity), `script_strings.rs` (the `TStrings`/`TStringList` object model with Delphi's `CompareStrings`/`Find`/`Add`+Sorted+Duplicates, `Text`, `DelimitedText` and `AnsiExtractQuotedStr`, `ValueFromIndex`, the four set operations and `LoadFromFile`/`SaveToFile` over xedit-io's encodings; the `TEncoding` objects; and the *early slice* of the JVCL `Classes` unit the Misc surface needs: `Create`, `Add`, `AddStrings`, `Append`, `Assign`, `Clear`, `Count`, `Delete`, `Insert`, `IndexOf`, `Exchange`, `Text`, `Strings[]` with the default `sl[i]`, `Sort`, `Find`, `Sorted`, `Duplicates`, plus an `ansi_compare_str` in xedit-io's `collate` for `CaseSensitive`), `script_adapter.rs` (`xejviScriptAdapter.pas`'s own registrations: the `gm*`/`es*`/`dt*`/`cs*`/`res*`/`at*` constants with the upstream ordinals, `esDeciding` carrying `Ord(esResolving)`; the `wbInterface` external unit; `TwbVector`/`TwbGridCell` and their constructors; `Assigned`, `ObjectToElement`, `FullPathToFilename`, `EnableSkyrimSaveFormat`, `GetRecordDefNames`, `wbFilterStrings`, `wbRemoveDuplicateStrings`, `wbVersionNumber` = `$4010510`), `script_misc.rs` (the 76 constants, the 63 StrUtils/Variants/SysUtils/Math/WinAPI/clipboard functions and the `TStrings` registrations, the Windows-only ones over windows-sys with documented stubs elsewhere), `script_misc_regex.rs` (`TPerlRegEx` over the port's PCRE engine (`crates/xedit-core/src/perl_regex.rs` on `crates/xedit-core/src/pcre.rs`): the `State` defaults, `Match`/`MatchAgain` with the offset model, the group accessors with their 1-based offsets and `Successful match required` errors, `ComputeReplacement`'s JGsoft backreference flavor with the `i`/`I` modes, `Replace`/`ReplaceAll`, `Split`/`SplitCapture`, `StoreGroups`, `Study`, `EscapeRegExChars`; the RTL's call shape and `ERegularExpressionError` texts kept) and `script_misc_json.rs` (the `JsonDataObjects` surface over a shared-node model -- handles alias their parent's storage -- with the library's `ParseXxx returns nil for empty` rule, `null` as a nil `jdtObject`, the auto-creating `A[]`/`O[]` accessors, the default `V[]` property, `jdtInt`/`jdtLong`/`jdtULong` number typing, the `TJsonOutputWriter` line structure, `StrToJSONStr` escaping, `DoubleToText`'s added `.0` and the `SetJDO*` configuration).
