### Parity backlog (before phase 7)

A review of the 70% claim on 2026-10-11 found that the merged phases left functional gaps, filed as the "Parity:" issues #22-#40 with tracker #41. They close before phase 7 starts. This is not a phase: each issue gets its own branch (`parity-<N>`), worktree (`M:\projects\xEdit-rust-parity-<N>`) and pull request against `main`, so that each can merge as soon as it is reviewed. Shared agent rules live in the coordinator's brief (`M:\projects\xedit-parity-brief.md`).

- **Model:** the coordinator runs on Opus 5.5 and pins each batch agent's model: Fable 5.1 for interpreter and core-semantics work (#23, #24, #26, #28), Sonnet 5.5 for adapter volume (#30, #31, #36), Opus 5.5 for the rest.
- **Review:** no per-PR review (the user decided on 2026-10-15). Each batch agent leaves its pull request with green gates and oracle evidence; the coordinator marks it ready and merges it.
- **Concurrency:** up to four batch agents at once, each in its own worktree. Runs that need more than 4 GB or 15 minutes take the heavy-run lock described in the brief. Merges into `main` are serialized; every branch rebases onto `main` before its final push.

Order (a batch starts when its dependencies have merged, or earlier when the overlap is small and a rebase settles it):

1. **Wave 1, foundations:** #23 edit indexes, UpdateRefs and EDID bytes (Fable); #22 overwrite, partial-form, template and aligned-array copy (Opus); #28 JvInterpreter language differences (Fable); #32 archive discovery and resource lookup (Opus).
2. **Wave 2, independent ports:** #29 PCRE regex (done: the port's own PCRE engine, `crates/xedit-core/src/pcre.rs` + `perl_regex.rs`, and the `Regex probe` the script parity mode runs), #40 unit-scoped callbacks in the transpiler (Opus), #39 numeric and locale edges (Opus, PR #53), #30 JSON date-time and binary streams (Sonnet), #27 FilterScripted callbacks (Opus), #34 comparison, filter, element-check and flag operations (Opus), #37 legacy switches, load settings and session workflows (Opus), #38 asset operations and MOPP (Opus).
3. **Wave 3, dependent ports:** #24 worldspace moves, changing-tree walks and topic sorting (Fable, after #23); #33 Starfield perk references, complex FormIDs and delocalization (Opus, after #23 and #32); #31 system helpers, exception classes and clocks (Sonnet, after #30); #35 cleaning options and saved-byte differences (Opus, after #22 and #24); #36 mod-group presets and ordering (Sonnet).
4. **Wave 4, script host and interaction:** #26 form and dialog scripts (Fable) builds a headless VCL object model and an agent interaction bridge (CLI/MCP supplies dialog choices, file selections and form inputs, then the script resumes). **Landed in its pull request (`parity-26`): `crates/xedit-session/src/script_vcl/interactive.rs` is the object model behind the `Vcl` seam, `script.run` takes the recorded `answers` (`class`, `title`, `keys`, `button`, `text`, `control`, `repeat`) and reports `pending_interaction`, `script.resume` continues, a control's published properties answer through the `GetValueRTTI`/`SetValueRTTI` fallback, element values compare by element identity, and the eleven units' ledger entries are reclassified to `covered`/`presentation`; the parity cases that drive both sides with the same recorded inputs (`runs.json`'s `answers`, a `variant` for a second case of one script) are `Bookmark.pas`, `Find records.pas` (3,394 log lines and the written ini), `LOD Statistics.pas` (the statistics file), `NIF - List blocks references.pas` (over a fixture mesh), `Add prefix or suffix to Editor ID.pas` (the saved fixture master) and the `norefs` variant of `Fallout4 - Filter for precombined statics.pas`: 6 of 6 `equal` on 2026-10-16 (pull request #62).** Bugs found along the way (rule 14, each fixed in `parity-26` with its own commit and witnessed by the answered cases): the script harness passed `--answers` as a global flag although the CLI defines it on `script run` (every answered case was `port-failed`) and captured its own `answers.json` as a script output; `MessageDlg`/`CreateMessageDialog` read the button set with `to_i32`, so the open-array argument `[mbOk]` (`ReadOpenArray`) offered `&Yes` -- they read it with `v2s` now; the model's `Items` was `xedit_script`'s string list, which the host's `TStrings` members refuse with `Invalid cast` (the `Classes` unit's `StringListObject` now); the control classes `xejviScriptAdapterMiscVcl.pas` adds (`TCheckListBox`, `TLabeledEdit`) were not installed into the model (`the class TCheckListBox is not registered`); a published property the tables register on another class (`btn.ModalResult`, `TCustomForm` only) was an undeclared identifier -- the `GetValueRTTI`/`SetValueRTTI` fallback was missing; element values compared by wrapper identity, so `TStrings.IndexOfObject` never found an element added earlier (`LOD Statistics.pas` wrote 21,765 lines of `Uses` 1 where the oracle writes 776 -- `ScriptObject::identity`, merged-code bug in the `Classes` string list); an unset set-shaped property read as `Unassigned` (`dlg.Options := dlg.Options + [ofOverwritePrompt]` was a Type mismatch); a file dialog joined a relative name to a data path ending in two separators; the model's list cursor started at 0 where the control starts at -1 (`Find records.pas` checked the wrong entries); the data-format corpus test of `main` concatenated a string and a boolean, a Type mismatch since parity #28 (it only runs with `XEDIT_ORACLE_DIR` set); the three step-9 shim tests pinned the refusal the model replaces. No upstream bug that loses or corrupts data was met: the `Format` `EConvertError` of `Fallout4 - Filter for precombined statics.pas` is a script bug upstream reproduces (parity #28) and is kept. Phase 7 renders the same model in the GUI, so #26 does not wait for it; the oracle side drives the release dialogs with the same recorded inputs. #25 the 30 pending host registrations (Opus) follows #22, #32, #33, #34, #38 and #26 (frmMain, frmFileSelect and JumpTo use the bridge). The dialog parts of #36 move onto the bridge here.
5. **Close-out:** #41 (Opus) runs the full parse/run script corpus and every owed full-corpus comparison at the final commit, audits the ledger, rewrites README/PLAN/skills so verified, deferred and baseline-unsupported behaviour are distinct, and separates the phase bar from functional coverage. The manual menu walkthrough against the release is done by the user. #7 (Enderal) and #9 (Morrowind) stay tracked by #41 and need the user's game data or an oracle workaround.

#38 (branch `parity-38`, PR #56, in progress) landed the Sniff side and the archive target: `Update MOPP code`, the last unported operation, follows the released 4.1.5q `Sniff.exe`, whose 64-bit build raises `You are running 64 bits version of Sniff. This operation works in 32 bits (x86) Sniff only.` in `LoadMoppDll` before it looks for NifMopp.dll (checked by `cargo xtask parity sniff --case mopp-update`: the oracle's refusal dialog, no file written, and the port's message equal to the release's own UTF-16 string), with the DLL body left unported because no released binary can reach it; the mesh spells are reachable on one file as `assets.optimize` (`SpellOptimize` with the requested options, `stripify` and `triangulate` being `SpellStripify` and `SpellTriangulate`), with `StripifyTriangles` pinned against the strips a stripify run writes; `archive pack --target pc|xbox` carries `BSArchPro`'s "XBox 360 Archive" target with the release's platform messages, and `DDS2XBOX` (`xtexconv.exe -xbox <temp file>` next to the program, the temp file read back and deleted, upstream's messages verbatim) is ported behind the same `FileExists` check as the GUI, with the tool itself never shipped. The atlas routes are bound in the script host and witnessed: `wbBuildAtlasFromTexturesList` and `wbBuildAtlasFromAtlasMap` (the Lanczos resizes, the canvas brightness and the gamma) run in the `atlas` probe of `cargo xtask parity script` over the fixture textures of `tests/fixtures/parity/atlas`, which a run case places in its private data folder with the new `data_files` key of `runs.json` (both sides read them through the container handler). The full-corpus `parity sniff` run of the optimize and analyze cases is done at this tip: 357 archive-case runs over three cases, 124,482 equal, 1,829 equal-error (the oracle's own refusals and crashes), 860,032 unchanged, 0 differences and 0 failures, logs equal everywhere (exit 0; the optimize cases alone: 119 archives, 328,781 files, 124,482 equal, 1,655 equal-error, 202,644 unchanged). Still owed in this batch: the FO4 export/object-mesh fixture (the MNAM quirk of the 4.1.5q definitions stands). Finding: `BSArchPro.exe`'s autopacking mode does not start its work when it is driven without a user (the progress form opens and stays, no CPU time, no file written, with and without `SW_HIDE`, threading on and off), so `cargo xtask parity bsarchpro` reports `oracle-failed` and the GUI route's byte comparison is owed a run at a screen (needs a decision).

Decisions owed to the user are recorded in each pull request under "Needs a decision" (for example shipping `NifMopp.dll` for #38, or a PCRE dependency licence for #29).

**Fixes between batches.** *fix-sse-filters (2026-10-16, issue #64, branch `fix-sse-filters`):* `cargo xtask parity filter --file sse-filters` had been 36 of 37 since parity #23 merged: the `unnecessary-persistent` configuration kept `REFR` 0004783E and 0004782E of `OLDBluePalaceWing01` (Skyrim.esm filtered 869,186 of 869,687 records against the oracle's 869,189, `[Pass 2]` 40679 against 40677, 697 unfiltered nodes against 692) because their `ReferencedByCount` had dropped to 0. The cause was not `IsUnnecessaryPersistent`, which is upstream's line for line, but the reference index under it: the two references are held by the `VMAD` of `ACTI` 0005307A (`dunbluepalaceSkeeverGate`: `EDID`, `VMAD`, `OBND`, no `PNAM`/`FNAM`), whose subrecords all lie before `QuickInitLimit`, so a filter that reads the base record's name (`by_base_name`, `by_base_editor_id`, `by_base_signature`, the signature filters; configuration 19 alone reproduces it) quick-inits it, the tail adds the required members as an internal edit, #23's reproduction of upstream's quick init releases the elements with the storage invalid, and the full init the filter's `BuildAllRef` then runs collapses the storage from nothing: the record is left with no subrecords, its references are rebuilt from none, and the portcullises lose their referencer. The GUI never shows it because its load builds the references first (the oracle log's `Start loading or building reference info` lines), so every record's first init is a full one; a standalone `filter apply` of the one configuration was equal for the same reason, and the batch of 37 was not. The loss itself is upstream's (the `edid-quick-init` fixture of #23 witnessed it in the oracle: a `KYWD` without `CNAM` or an `ACTI` with `EDID` and `OBND` alone read by `Name` first ends with 0 elements), and under the user's rule of 2026-10-16 a data-losing upstream bug is not reproduced: `MainRecordImpl::quick_init` now collapses the invalidated storage from the elements it built (`collapse_storage_after_quick_init`: keys and references updated from the elements, the elements written as the record's `CollapsedData`, the parent storage invalidated) before it releases them, so the next init reads the record with its required members, as a record read through `ElementByName` first does on both sides (`UPSTREAM-BUG` comments at `quick_init` and `collapse_storage_in_init`). Evidence: `cargo xtask parity filter --file sse-filters` **37 of 37** equal at the tip (the oracle log from the cache; `fo4-filters` 8 of 8, `sse-reachable` 4 of 4 and `sse-scripted` 2 of 2 unchanged), `cargo xtask parity refs --game sse` and `conflicts --game sse` equal as before. The parity case `probes/edid-quick-init.pas` is reshaped so both sides end in the same state (every record read through `ElementByName` first; `cargo xtask parity script --phase run --script edid-quick-init` equal with the oracle re-run, 59 log lines and the saved bytes), the name-first read is pinned port-side by `the_quick_init_keeps_the_subrecords_upstream_loses`, and `runs.json` gained `accept` (a reason that makes a differing run case a `known-difference`), used for `Add Prefix Plus Name To EDID.pas`, whose oracle save writes the `EDID` alone for the 850 records the quick init emptied (`known-difference`: the port's save is 271,185,874 bytes against the oracle's 271,172,991, every log line the memo kept equal). Two more bugs found on the way, rule 14: (a) harness -- the run phase's `equal-log-loss` status excused *every* difference once the oracle's memo had lost lines, the written and saved files included, so a saved plugin whose bytes differed read as equal; the file differences are now compared apart and make the case `different` whatever the memo lost (`script.rs`, the `Add Prefix Plus Name To EDID.pas` save above is the first difference it shows); (b) merged code, **owed to an owner** -- `cargo xtask parity refs --game sse` has the referenced-by lists equal (189,336 records, 1,940,071 entries) but the `Skyrim.esm` cache file differs from the oracle's (59,557,451 bytes against 60,000,947, first at record 459 `[00000E85]`, the cached name): the oracle's refs session has no string tables in its private data folder, so its cached names read `Tamriel "<Error: No strings file for lstring ID 000020E6>" [WRLD:0000003C]` where the port's cache writer omits the full name (`... in Tamriel [WRLD:0000003C]`), although the port's script-mode `Name` of the same worldspace over the same folder gives the oracle's error text; identical at `main` 65366d0 (run on the parity-26 build), so not this fix's, and the phase-4 "70 of 70 cache files equal" predates it. **Fixed by parity #33** (PR #60, its bug 1: `localization_get_value` answers as upstream's empty handler before the session installs its own): at #33's rebased tip b3ec451 the three `Skyrim.esm` caches (TES5, SSE, TES5VR) are equal after decompression and record 459 carries the oracle's error text. (c) merged code (parity #39), fixed here -- `FormatFloat` of a value with more than 18 digits before the decimal point and no exponent in the picture printed every digit, where the RTL's `InternalFloatToTextFmt` hands such a value to `FloatToText` in the general format (`FormatFloat('0.#####', 1E22)` is `1E22`, the picture ignored; `script_rtl/format.rs`, unit-tested); found by the probe phase run for #26 at `main`, where `numerics.pas` was `different` at its line 221. The probe phase at this tip (`cargo xtask parity script --phase probe`, 892 probes now): 852 `equal`, 32 `known-difference`, 7 `equal-error`, 1 `oracle-dialog` (`numerics-parse.pas`, whose oracle run never finished and is cached as failed, a class), **891 of 892**, exit 0. Gates: `cargo fmt --all --check`, `cargo clippy --workspace --all-targets -- -D warnings`, `cargo xtask check` green, `cargo test --workspace` 732 passed, 0 failed, 5 ignored across 59 binaries (without `XEDIT_ORACLE_DIR`; with it, main's `a_corpus_nif_walks_through_the_data_format_adapter` fails on its own string-plus-boolean concatenation, which PR #62 fixes).

*fix-numerics (2026-10-16, issue #67, branch `fix-numerics`):* the numerics follow-ups of #31 and #39, each ported from the machine code of `xTESEdit.exe` 4.1.5q. (1) `FloatToDecimal` rounds in the script's `SetRoundMode` mode: every operation of the RTL's (`Power10`'s paired products at `0xE4C0`, the `cvtsd2si` `Round` at `0xCB10`, the division by ten) is an SSE operation in the `MXCSR` mode, and the port replays each in that mode (`delphi::Arith`: the round-to-nearest result corrected by the sign of its exact error, run under round-to-nearest; the mode is per thread, as the `MXCSR` is, where it was a process-wide static). `0.3` prints `2.9999999999999994E-1` under `rmDown`; `sys-round.pas` prints digits in all four modes (`FloatToStrF`, `FloatToStr`, `Format`, `FormatFloat`, a denormal, `1E300/7`) and is equal. (2) `FormatFloat` is the RTL's `FloatToTextFmt` (`delphi::float_to_text_fmt`, from `FindSection`/`ScanSection`/`ApplyFormat`), replacing the hand-written picture formatter and #66's fallback: the digits are `FloatToDecimal`'s, rounded half up on the digit string (`FormatFloat('0', 2.5)` is `3`, `FormatFloat('0.00', 0.125)` is `0.13`), the general fallback starts at `FloatRec.Exponent >= 18` (`FormatFloat('0', 1e17)` is `1E17`, so #66's threshold of `1e18` and its unit test's 18 digits were wrong), the minus sign is written only by the first section, the exponent of a zero is minus the integer placeholders (`0.00E-01`) and pads to at most four digits; the new `fmt-float-pictures.pas` (412 lines) is equal. (3) `StrToFloat` is the RTL's `InternalTextToFloat`, replacing #39's fitted `Val`: the text ends at a `#0`, only spaces are blanks, the digits go into an unsigned 64-bit accumulator up to `$1999999999999990` and then into a double as `(v * 10 + Ord(ch)) - 48` (two roundings, which is where 21-digit mantissas were an ulp or more off), the exponent read stops above 1024 (`1E1000` is `INF`, `1E9999` refused, `1E-1000` 0), the overflow flag fails the parse, and the words are `NAN`/`INF`/`+INF`/`-INF` as a whole. (4) `numerics-parse.pas` had never finished on the oracle: its `#0` cases put a NUL into the message log memo, and the harness reads the memo's text up to its first zero, so the closing line never came; the probe logs control characters as `#<code>` (the `.dat` keeps the raw text) and is equal in the probe and run phases, the `.dat` byte for byte (387 records). Its first reshape indexed a `string` parameter, which upstream refuses (`Array type required`: a parameter holds a `varUString`, which `GetElement` does not index) and the port's one string kind allows: `e-ordidx-01..04.pas` are `known-difference` under the documented one-string-kind divergence, `e-ordidx-05/06.pas` equal. (5) `ContainerStates` keeps `csInitOnce` per member (`ContainerBase::is_init_once`) and follows upstream's reset rules: a main record or subrecord is held while one of its elements is referenced (`cntElementRefs`), a value while it is referenced itself (`eExternalRefs`); a record's `EDID`, which the record's own `Init` reads, is 1026, a record only `RecordByIndex` reached 1024, one held through a subrecord 1031 (`el-states.pas`, 174 lines with 23 `ContainerStates` lines, equal). Not reproduced: the port does not reset a value on a release, so a value read, let go and asked again answers 1031 where the oracle may answer 1026. (6) `dfStrToFloat('NaN')` (`f64::from(SingleNaN)` folded at compile time into `0x7FF8...`) and the `ArcTan2` table's NaN (`ArcSin(2)`, `ArcCos(-2)`) are the RTL's `0xFFF8...`; `numerics.pas`'s run phase `.dat` is equal (349 records). The probe phase at the tip (`cargo xtask parity script --phase probe`, 908 probes, port `547308f`): 864 `equal`, 36 `known-difference`, 7 `equal-error`, 1 `oracle-dialog` (`sys-showexception.pas`), **907 of 908**, 0 `different`, exit 0. Owed: `0.0/0.0` raises in the port where the oracle gives NaN (#28); a script that logs a `#0` cannot be compared (the memo read stops there).

Results: #22 (branch `parity-22`, PR #45) closed the *Copy* owed from phase 3. `records.copy` now copies over a record the target has already (`overwrite` and `remove_deleted`, the `wbCanOverwrite` callback of `copy.rs` and the master reporting of the GUI's `CopyInto`), partial forms are made with `--allow-make-partial` (`MakePartialForm`), and the template elements, `IwbMultipleElements` and the aligned arrays are ported behind the commands `elements.assign`, `elements.templates` and `elements.move`, `files.new` module templates and the script adapter's `AssignTemplate*`, `TemplateAssign`, `ElementAssign`, `MoveUp`/`MoveDown` and `InsertElement`. Evidence at the tip: `cargo xtask parity oracle-edit --game sse` **24 of 24 saved files equal** over the eleven Skyrim SE sequences (the nine the batch adds: an existing override with its string table, a deleted source copied over and removed, partial cells and worldspaces, the module templates, aligned and template assigns, moves and insert-element, and the existing `sse-flst-orderedlist` and `sse-strings-*` ones, report `target/parity/oracle-edit.json`), and `cargo xtask parity script --phase run` runs the three shipped conversion scripts: `Make new records from overrides.pas` is **equal** (`Update.esm`'s overrides are what it converts; its corpus case loads `Update.esm` now, where a load of `Skyrim.esm` alone has none — and both sides abort at the same line on a reference the conversions have not made yet, `[0103B681] <Error: Could not be resolved>`, so the abort is compared too), while `Skyrim - Convert BOOK to SCRL.pas` and `Fallout4 - Convert ALCH to SPEL.pas` save the oracle's plugins and string tables byte for byte and differ in the closing log line alone (`Processed Records` 870538 against 869717 and 1549682 against 1549451: the oracle's walk re-visits the nodes its script's removals and additions churn, which the port's element walk does not model, the class recorded under phase 6's churn cases). The batch found and fixed two gaps on the way: `Encoding::name` gives the system's code page name (`GetCPInfoEx`'s `CodePageName`, `crates/xedit-io/src/encoding.rs`), so the load says `Using encoding (from language): 1252  (ANSI - Latin I)` as the oracle's does, and the storage merge builds a container's data in the **memory order** of its elements (`TwbContainer.UpdateMemoryOrder` and `MergeStorageInternal` ported in `crates/xedit-core/src/implementation/edit.rs`), so a container the tree sorted (a VMAD's `Properties` by `propertyName`) is written back in the order its data had — without it one `SCEN` record of 21,386 in `Update.esm` came out with its properties in sort order. Owed and recorded in the pull request: the non-Skyrim-SE edit sequences have to be re-run against the oracle, because `crates/xtask/oracle/edit.pas` is part of every generated script and the batch changed it, so their cached oracle saves are stale by construction (a heavy run, `Fallout4.esm` and `Starfield.esm` in the GUI; the close-out #41 runs it), and `GetIsInSK` of a subrecord or a value stays unported (the definitions never use a sort-key structure definition as a subrecord or value definition). #40 (branch `parity-40`, PR #52) closed the transpiler's unit-scoped callbacks owed from phase 4 step 3. `Symbols::add_decl` keeps a routine of a unit that repeats another unit's name and parameter list apart and `Symbols::resolved_overload` (and `Resolver::resolve_call`, which takes the unit of the call now) resolves a name as Delphi does: the unit's own copy first, else the copy of the unit it uses last, which the symbol table added first because the units are added in the reverse of the `uses` order. `overload_names` names a copy that no parameter selects after its unit, so Fallout 76's older `wbLGDIFiltersToStr` is `wb_lgdi_filters_to_str_fo76` in callbacks/fo76.rs (the game-mode dispatch of callbacks/common.rs is gone), Fallout 3's and New Vegas's own `wbSOUNAfterLoad` are `wb_soun_after_load_fo3` and `wb_soun_after_load_fnv` over a shared body in callbacks/common.rs, Starfield's own `wbOBND` is the generated `wb_obnd_sf1`, and Common's own `wbNormalizeToRange` (equal to the `wbInterface` copy every other unit names) is a second hand port. `cargo xtask port-defs duplicates <upstream checkout>` lists the 119 routine names more than one unit declares with whether the bodies differ; the four that share an emission with `wbInterface` or `wbDefinitionsCommon` are those. Focused fixtures: `tests/fixtures/parity/soun-after-load-fo3.esm` and `-fnv.esm` (one sound record per branch of the AfterLoad) with `probes/soun-after-load.pas` in the run corpus, `cargo xtask parity script --script soun-after-load` 2 of 2 cases equal against the released oracle (25 log lines and the saved bytes each), asserted in process by `the_soun_after_load_probe_gives_the_oracle_lines`; `cargo xtask parity oracle-edit` 54 of 56 equal (the one difference the known sf1 delocalize limit, the one failure an oracle-side stop cached before the branch).

#30 (branch `parity-30`, PR #51) closed the JSON stream and date-time items: `TJsonBaseObject.LoadFromStream`/`SaveToStream` are wired to the JVCL `Classes` stream family (the read from the position to the end, the byte order marks and the encodings, the write at the position, a nil stream's no-op, the `EJsonParserException`/`EEncodingError` classes (a JSON parse error's message carries the reader's ` (line, column)`, and the `Unexpected end of file where value was expected (1, 9)` of the half-written UTF-16 file matches; the port's direct parser approximates the token an expectation mismatch names, so the fixtures compare the class there)), `TBinaryReader`, `TBinaryWriter` and `TBytesStream` are ordinary stream operations now (the 18 `xejviScriptAdapterMiscVcl.pas` ledger entries are `covered`; the port's VCL seam no longer touches them), and the date-time surface follows the library: the machine's time-zone bias through `TzSpecificLocalTimeToSystemTime` (windows-sys), `DateTimeToISO8601`'s offset form, the whole `JSONToDateTime` parse (the `/Date(ms)/` form, the offsets, the invalid input with `EncodeDate`'s `EConvertError`) and the `jdtUtcDateTime` distinctions. Evidence at the tip: `cargo xtask parity script --phase run --script probes/`, **5 of 5 equal**, the two new fixtures `crates/xtask/oracle/scripts/probes/streams.pas` (57 log lines, 9 files compared byte for byte) and `datetime.pas` (51 log lines) equal against the GUI's own runs (report `target/parity/script.json`); `crates/xedit-session/tests/script.rs` asserts the oracle's lines for both probes (the datetime one keeps its time-zone dependent lines as relationships, because CI runs in UTC). What the oracle pinned and the port now follows, each with an `UPSTREAM-QUIRK` or a porting comment: `DecodeDate` takes the truncated day and `DecodeTime` the distance from it read as a positive time (so `-1.25` is 1899-12-29 06:00), `SystemTimeToDateTime` *subtracts* the time of a date before 1899-12-30 (the fraction of a negative `TDateTime` is negative; this is what makes `1899-12-29T13:00Z` a `+07:00` local offset), `TEncoding.Unicode` writes `FStringBuffer.Len` **bytes** of the UTF-16 buffer (half the text, an 18 byte file where 34 would fit, and a load of it is an `EJsonParserException`), `FlushToMemoryStream` truncates a `TMemoryStream` at the end of what it wrote (`TBytesStream` and the file streams do not), `TBinaryWriter` has no buffer (the file grows on the first `Write(42)`) and writes nothing for a variant type its dispatch does not name (`Write(True)` is 0 bytes), `TBinaryReader.Read` reads one byte through the reader's encoding (`varInteger`, position +1) and refuses a non-ASCII byte with `EEncodingError` **without consuming it**, `TBinaryReader.Create(nil)`/`TBinaryWriter.Create(nil)` raise `EArgumentNilException`, a save into a nil stream writes nothing, and a freed stream's use is the port's `EAccessViolation` where upstream reads freed memory. Divergences recorded, not compared: a `TBinaryWriter.Write(string)` of a non-ASCII script string (the GUI's writer encodes the character as UTF-8, 2 bytes for U+00E9; the port's `#233` escape is one byte that is not valid UTF-8), the port's access violation message carries zero addresses where the GUI prints the faulting ones, and the JSON parser's expectation messages: the oracle's tokenizer names its tokens (`Expected "{" but found identifier (1, 2)` for the mid-stream load, `Unexpected end of file where "{" was expected (1, 1)` for the empty stream), which the port's direct parser approximates. The batch found and fixed `TFileStream.Create(path, fmCreate)` on the way (it opened read-only and created nothing).

Results: #27 (branch `parity-27`, PR #54) closed the scripted filter. `xedit_analysis::filter::ScriptedFilter` is the callback seam the `CheckFilterNode` chain of `mniNavFilterApplyClick` calls for every main record node its walk reaches (`CustomScriptFilter`), `RunScriptedFilter` in `crates/xedit-session/src/script.rs` binds it to the run's own interpreter (one implementation for one-shot, batch, serve and MCP), and `filter.apply` carries the flag (`scripted`, the dialog's `cbScriptedFilter`, and the `--scripted` CLI flag; without a script every record is rejected, as the GUI's nil `Script` does). The flag is read before every record, an unassigned or 0 result is False, a raise aborts the walk (`Error during Applying Filter: <message>` then the run's `Exception in unit ...` and `Aborted:` lines), and after a filter the `Process` walk sees only the nodes the filter kept (`apply_filter_scripted` answers them, as `ApplyScriptToSelection` walks the filtered tree). Evidence at the tip: `cargo xtask parity script --phase run --script "Apply custom scripted filter"` **equal** (7 log lines: `[Skyrim.esm] Filtered 868,496 of 869,687 records` and `[Pass 1] Processed Records: 920209, [Pass 2] Processed Records: 1312, Remaining unfiltered nodes: 1193` the oracle's; the port's 869833/869833 and missing per-file line are gone), the fixtures `tests/fixtures/parity/filter-scripted.esm` with `crates/xtask/oracle/scripts/probes/filter-scripted.pas` (acceptance/rejection by EDID, the visitation order, an edit made during the callback, `FilterScripted := False` mid-walk, the node set the later `Process` walk sees; the unit test asserts the oracle's 37 lines) and `probes/filter-scripted-error.pas` (the raise, the `Error during`/`Exception`/`Aborted:` shape with `unit FilterScriptedError line 20`, the messages of the statements after the call dropped) both **equal**, and `cargo xtask parity filter --file sse-scripted` (the scenario `crates/xtask/oracle/filters/sse-scripted.json`: `FilterScripted` with no `Filter` function rejects every record, only the file and group nodes stay, and the flag's reset lets the next configuration run unscripted) 2 of 2 configurations equal. Two neighbouring gaps the walk found and fixed: the per-file line prints the base name (`TwbFile.GetFileName` is `ExtractFileName` of the loaded path) and the filter's lines belong at the `ApplyFilter` call, under `wbHideStartTime` (no `[mm:ss]` prefix). The port-side re-run of the pre-existing filter scenarios at the tip and at `origin/main` `7d792b9` (the oracle logs from the cache) found a divergence of its own, **not** this batch's: `sse-filters` was 36 of 37, the `unnecessary-persistent` configuration filtering 3 fewer records of Skyrim.esm than the oracle (`869186/869687` against `869189/869687`; `[Pass 2]` 40679/697 against 40677/692); fixed by `fix-sse-filters` (issue #64, the quick-init data loss, see the Fixes paragraph below), 37 of 37 again. Limits recorded, owed with them: the port applies a deferred filter when the call into the script returns, so a statement after `ApplyFilter` that reads state the `Filter` function changed (the script's own variables, edits made during the walk) sees the state before the filter ran, and the messages of statements after a failing `ApplyFilter` are dropped while their edits are not (upstream aborts inside the call); the `Boolean(V)` cast of a string result (`EVariantTypeCastError`) is implemented and documented but not reachable through the corpus shape, because the port's interpreter converts `Result` to the function's declared type at the assignment (`Type mismatch` there where upstream lets the variant through to the native cast) -- the coercion surface is #28's.

#34 (branch `parity-34`, PR #55) closed the comparison, filter, element-check and flag operations: the "Compare to..." load is the `--compare-to <path>` option (`wb_file_compare`, the compare load takes the load order of the file it compares to, `AddMaster(flCompareTo)`, `fsCompareToHasSameMasters`, `IsCompareToSame` with `ContentEquals`/`IsSameData` ported from a stub), "Compare Selected" is `records.compare_selected` (the sibling view of `DoSetActiveRecord` with several records, `view_for_sibling_records`, over records of different FormIDs), the hidden-record state of the tree is `records.set_hidden` with `is_hidden` (`esParentHidden`/`esParentHiddenChecked`), `NodeDatasForMainRecord` and the only-one filter leaving hidden records out, the dormant `wbCompareRawData` (nothing in 4.1.5q assigns it) is the `raw_data` option of the two compare commands with `sortable::get_raw_data_as_string` and the raw display sort key, the element Check is `elements.check`, the tree's "Filter by filename" is `files.list --name-filter/--regex`, the script function `ConflictAllForElements` is `conflicts.for_elements` (and `ConflictContext::conflict_level_for_pair`), and `records get`/`elements get` list the flags of a flags value as children under `wbFlagsAsArray` (which `sortable::view_elements` already built for the view). The editor's sessions now set `wbDisplayShorterNames` as `FormCreate` does, so a name is `EditorID "Full Name" [SIG:FormID]`, and `GetDisplayName` gained the Morrowind LAND/PGRD grid cell names. Evidence: `cargo xtask parity conflicts --record` now runs the probe script on both sides and diffs the lines (statuses, element rows by index, display names, names, extended sort keys and element checks) — over `DOBJ:00000031`, `CELL:00000D74`, `FLST:00000163`, `AVIF:000005E9`, `QUST:00013EB3`, `QUST:01000803` and `REFR:01000801` in `Skyrim.esm`, `Update.esm` and `Dawnguard.esm`: 2203 oracle lines and 2203 port lines, **0 different, 1 known-difference** (the `DOBJ` name of a record whose EDID only the internal edit adds: the port caches the name when the added subrecord's element is built, the release only while the file's subrecords are parsed), and with `--hidden 00013EB3 --hidden 01000803` 2203 lines with the same counts; `cargo xtask parity conflicts --game sse` (11 files, 1,188,933 records, 20,782 listed) stays equal. The flags `FilterOnlyOne`, `FilterConflictOnly` and `FilterNoGameMaster`, and the compare-to load, have no oracle route: the switches that set the first two run in `tmEdit` only (`xeInit.pas:1216`, the script mode is `tmScript`), the script host exposes no variable for them (`xejviScriptHost.pas`), and no script function creates a compare load — those behaviours are pinned to the release's code paths and the port's own runs (compared above).

#36 (branch `parity-36`, PR #58) closed the mod-group gaps: the selection presets of the mod group dialog (`PresetLoad`, `PresetSave`, `PresetDelete` and `PresetsUpdate` with `GetPresetsSection` and `IsPresetDifferent`) are the commands `modgroups.presets`, `modgroups.preset_load`, `modgroups.preset_save` and `modgroups.preset_delete` over the section `frmModGroupSelect_ActiveModGroups_Presets` of the settings file (a comma text per preset, `<Last>` for `[ModGroups] Selection`, the overwrite/update/delete questions as `confirm` parameters whose default is the dialog's default button, `preset_differs` for the question the dialog asks on close), with `TMemIniFile.ReadSection`, `ValueExists` and `DeleteKey` ported in `crates/xedit-loadorder/src/ini_files.rs`; the modules of the data folder are ordered as `wbModulesByLoadOrder` orders them (`_ModulesLoadOrderCompare`: the blueprint flag, `miOfficialIndex` of the game master, the game's executable, `Update.esm` of the Skyrim family and `wbOfficialDLC`, `miCCIndex`, the ESM flag of the extension of the later games or of the header flag before them, the plugin list index, the write time and the name), so unloaded modules and their `.modgroups` files come in upstream's order (the `.ccc` Creation Club list is not read, and a loaded module's session load order stands in for its `Plugins.txt` index, which #37 owns); and a name several groups have resolves to the first in the dialog's list order (`modgroups.show` and `modgroups.edit` take it instead of refusing). The harness drives the release GUI for the flows that had no scenario (`gui.rs`: `unloaded` plugins linked into the run folder without being loaded, `jump_to` showing a record whose loaded override chain the view's "Create ModGroup" takes, an answer's `text` in the control of a class, `repeat` for a window answered step by step, and scenario answers matched before the harness's own module-selection OK). Evidence at the tip: `cargo xtask parity modgroups` **15 of 15 scenarios equal** (`target/parity/modgroups.json`), the 9 of step 6 and the six the batch adds: `sse-create` (the view's Create ModGroup over the override chain of the record `JumpTo` shows, the CRC32 question answered Yes, the name typed into the edit form, the store-file module selection by Enter, the reload; 189 records hidden, the settings and mod group bytes equal), `sse-edit-duplicate` (a name Update.modgroups and Dawnguard.modgroups both hold; Update.esm is an official and *unloaded* module, so its file comes first although Dawnguard sorts before Update by name — the rename writes Update.modgroups, the other file is untouched, 3 hidden), `sse-edit-items` (the first item deleted with the Delete key on the focused node and its question answered Yes, 216 hidden), `sse-presets` (the preset name typed into the combo box, Load, then OK: the settings file keeps `Mine`/`Other` and writes the loaded selection into `[ModGroups] Selection`, the preset's group is the one that hides, 2 hidden), `sse-preset-save` (a new name and an existing one written with Save, the overwrite question answered Yes, then OK; 49 hidden) and `sse-preset-delete` (Delete and its question answered Yes; 49 hidden), each comparing the settings file, the mod group files, the validation messages and the status of every record (904,233 to 1,064,912 records per scenario). The unit tests: `crates/xedit-session/tests/modgroups.rs` 8 of 8, with `unloaded_modules_keep_the_upstream_load_order` (the modules of the data folder in `_ModulesLoadOrderCompare` order, unloaded ones included), `duplicate_group_names_resolve_by_file_order` (the edit takes the first of two same-named groups in the dialog's order) and `presets_load_save_and_delete_as_the_selection_dialog` (the settings bytes of save/load/`<Last>`/`after_modal`/delete and `unknown_preset`).

#37 (branch `parity-37`, PR #57) closed the legacy switches, the startup discovery, the save-definition export and the session workflows: `LoadSwitches`/`apply_on_load` carry `-PseudoESL`/`-IgnoreESL` (and the medium/update pairs), `-dontremoveoffsetdata`, `-reportinjected` (the load logs the injection notes, `FileImpl::inject_main_record`), `-TrackAllEditorID`, `-EnforceAllMasters`, `-moreunknown`, `-speed`/`-memory`, `-cp:`, `-StripMasters:<name>,...` (`FileImpl::strip_master`), the Starfield red-pill trio, and every one of them has a flag of its own on the CLI so CLI, batch, serve and MCP load alike; each switch the port does not act on is classified (`SwitchKind`: the release's own no-ops, the GUI-only ones, the unported `-moreunknown`, `-devmode`, `-speed`/`-memory`, `-moprofile`), with the audit's finding that `-PseudoMedium`/`-IgnoreMedium`/`-PseudoUpdate`/`-IgnoreUpdate` can do nothing in the release at all (medium and update modules are Starfield's, whose block then turns the six flags off); `DoInitPath` is ported past the inis (`game_paths.rs`: `CheckAppPath`, the Steam library through the ported `wbSteamVDFParser`, the registry keys, the game's own `Plugins.txt` in the local application data folder, the save folder from `SLocalSavePath`, the settings file, the backup and cache folders, `-T:`/`-S:`/`-l:`/`-cp:`), and a legacy run prints the `Using ...` lines of the GUI's startup log; the export walks the save definitions (`ProfileChapters`/`wbFileChapters`, `wbFileHeader`, `tool run export --source saves`, `<AppName>ExportSaves.txt`); `session.load` applies a tool mode's load settings to a running serve/MCP session (the quick clean workflow); and the named pipe's concurrency is defined and tested. The batch found and fixed three export defects: `check_pass` handed `ExportElement` the container text instead of the empty profile `CheckPass` clears, the structure text used LF where the release's `WriteLn` writes CRLF, and `TwbEnumDef.GetDefTypeName`/`TwbFlagsDef.GetDefTypeName` wrote a class name where the release's bare `inherited` leaves an empty result. Owed: the Fallout 4 export text is the same lines in another order (`known-difference`), `-moreunknown`/`-devmode`/`-speed`/`-memory` set their global with nothing reading it, and `-moprofile` leaves the Mod Organizer hook unloaded.

#31 (branch `parity-31`, PR #61) closed the system helpers, the exception classes and the run clocks. Ported: `DiskFree`/`DiskSize` over `GetDiskFreeSpaceExW` with the JvInterpreter wrapper's `Integer` truncation and wrapped `Byte` drive, `GetLocaleStr`/`GetLocaleChar` over `GetLocaleInfoW`, `Beep` as `MessageBeep(0)`, `ShowException` through the `Vcl` seam (the oracle's modal box ends its run, so the case is an `oracle-dialog` class), `CreateProcessWait` as `CreateProcessW` with upstream's `STARTUPINFO`, `SetRoundMode` (the `MXCSR` rounding bits) and `SetPrecisionMode`, the locale-derived default format settings, `FileWriteToStream` into a `Classes` stream with the `TwbResetModified` of its third argument, the main record `SetEditValue` gates, `IntToEsState`'s whole mapping and `ContainerStates`, the data-format adapters' exception classes and OS texts (`EOSError`, `EFCreateError`, `EFOpenError`, the bare `Exception` of a material's `ToJSON`), the local-time save suffix and the load-start/action-start clock origins. Evidence at the tip: `cargo xtask parity script --phase probe` **898 of 901 equal** (859 equal, 32 known-difference, 7 equal-error, 1 different, 2 oracle-dialog; the nine #31 probes `sys-helpers`, `sys-round`, `sys-process`, `sys-showexception`, `df-load-missing`, `df-save-missing`, `df-file-errors`, `file-stream` and `el-states` are 8 equal and the one `oracle-dialog`; the `different` is `numerics.pas`, the `FormatFloat('0.#####', 1e22)` item #39 recorded as owed, and the second `oracle-dialog` is `numerics-parse.pas`, whose oracle run does not finish) and the unit tests `the_sys_round_probe_gives_the_oracle_lines` and `the_el_states_probe_gives_the_oracle_lines` pin the oracle's lines. The run phase over the cases the clocks, saves and df adapters touch (`--phase run`, 9 cases): **6 equal** (`BASH tags autodetection`, `Regex probe`, `Check for circular previous INFOs`, `Detect conflict between elements`, `Add enable parent to the refs of specific base object` with its saved plugin, `Apply custom scripted filter`), **1 equal-log-loss** (`Add Prefix Plus Name To EDID`: 869,722 lines, 271 MB save compared, the oracle's memo lost 76 lines), 1 `oracle-interactive` (`Change persistent flag`, a `Confirm` dialog) and 1 `oracle-failed` (`Check for errors`: the oracle did not finish in 20 minutes); the full 117-case run phase was not repeated at this tip. Still incomplete, and recorded in the ledger and the `writing-xedit-scripts` skill: the digits `FloatToStrF` prints under a directed rounding mode (the RTL's float-to-text arithmetic follows the mode and the port's digit generation does not replay it), `ContainerStates` of a member the oracle's lazy read initialised once (`EDID` answers 1026), `GetLocaleStr` of a value outside ASCII (the string model) and the audible effect of `Beep`.

Bugs found (#31, rule 14), all fixed in the branch unless noted: (1) the port's text-to-number conversions followed the `SetRoundMode` mode (Rust's literal parse divides in `f64`, so `0.1` under `rmDown` became the double below it and `0.1 + 0.2` came out two ulps low) where the oracle's conversions do not; they now run through `xedit_io::fpu::with_nearest_rounding` (literals, the variant string reading, `StrToFloat`, the JSON number tokens), witnessed by `sys-round` equal in all four modes. (2) `DateTimeToString` matched its specifiers in lower case only, so the locale's `yyyy-MM-dd` printed `2026-M-16`, and it printed the AM/PM string in the case of the format; it now reads specifiers case-insensitively and the `AMPM` token prints the locale's string (`sys-helpers` `fmt` lines). (3) `TwbBGSMFile.ToJSON`/`SaveToJSONFile` serialised a material where upstream raises `Exception` `Not implemented` before touching the file (`df-file-errors`). (4) `SetEditValue` of a main record gave a bare `Exception` with a quoted text for a value that is no FormID where the oracle gives `EConvertError '$<text>' is not a valid integer value` (a script run turns `wbDisplayLoadOrderFormID` on; the doc comment that said otherwise was wrong). (5) `ContainerStates` answered the file mask for every container; a member below a record answers 1024 (`el-states`). (6) The unit test `parsing_follows_the_format_settings` read the machine's locale (it failed on an en-CA machine since the settings became locale-derived); it now passes an explicit `ShortDateFormat`. Not fixed and handed over: the directed-mode digits of `FloatToStrF` and the per-member `csInitOnce` above (port gaps with a named cause, owner: a later numerics batch for the first); `numerics.pas`'s `FormatFloat` item belongs to #39. All three were closed by fix-numerics (issue #67). No upstream bug that loses or corrupts data was found in this batch.

#33 (branch `parity-33`, PR #60) closed the Starfield items. The perk activity JSON is ported (`wbPerkActivityAfterLoad`, `wbPerkActivityAfterSet`, `wbVirtualPerkActivityUpdateJSON`, the virtual `NULL` root's and Stream FormID's `AfterSet`, and `SerializeElementToJson` with its helpers over `xedit_core::json`), so the `ATAV` text becomes the virtual tree whose Stream FormIDs give the references, and a change of the tree, or a masters update of a Stream FormID (`fsMastersUpdating`), writes the text back. `cargo xtask parity refs`, all games, re-run on 2026-10-16 at the branch's tip after its rebases onto #26, #31, the sse-filters fix and the numerics fix (port b3ec451): 10 of 10 equal, 7,013,961 referenced records with 61,219,249 entries built and loaded from the cache, 79 of 79 cache files equal after decompression (Starfield 1,184,831 records with 27,990,420 entries, 9 of 9 cache files); the same numbers as at the port commit 33c2ca4 before the rebases. The complex (light and medium) FileIDs are checked by the edit sequences `sf1-light-masters`, `sf1-medium-masters`, `sf1-light-formid-change` (a FormID change with the reference information and a compact renumber of a light plugin), `sf1-perk-activity-edit` (with the referenced-by lists after the edits, a new `refs` comparison of `oracle-edit`) and `sf1-perk-activity-masters`; `cargo xtask parity oracle-edit` over all 34 sequences: 63 of 63 saved files equal (the 20 sequences with string tables 47 of 47, 27 tables). The localization sequences now run the GUI's own handler, `mniNavLocalizationSwitchClick`, on the oracle side: the script `JumpTo`s the file header, sends the navigation tree a Left key (`Perform(WM_KEYDOWN, VK_LEFT)`, which focuses the file node) and clicks the menu item; the harness answers the "Localize plugin" dialog, and the plugin compared is the one the handler's closing `SaveChanged` writes (the six localization sequences 14 of 14 on fresh oracle runs). The earlier emulation through `SetEditValue` could not set a text with `NoTranslate` while the file is still localized and trimmed one `sf1-strings-delocalize` response text the handler keeps; the port keeps it too. The protected official modules are still refused as upstream refuses them (`parity roundtrip --game sf1` at b3ec451: 7 equal, 7 refused, 0 different, 0 failed of 14). Deliberate divergence (UPSTREAM-BUG, user decision 2026-10-16): after a record copy upstream loses the first edit of a perk activity's virtual tree (Delphi releases the copied record's elements and `wbPerkActivityAfterLoad` builds the tree again from the old `ATAV` text); the port keeps the edit, and the sequences set every tree value twice so both sides end in the same state (the probe `probes/perk-activity.pas` shows the loss). Bugs found and fixed: (1) a localized string read before the session installs its localization handler answered an empty text where upstream's handler, made at unit initialization, answers `<Error: No strings file for lstring ID ...>`; with the official-file name caches of #24 the `Skyrim.esm` reference cache named 4,348 cells and references in Tamriel without the worldspace's full name (TES5, SSE, TES5VR; now equal: at b3ec451 the port's cache record 459 `[00000E85]` names `in Tamriel "<Error: No strings file for lstring ID 000020E6>" [WRLD:0000003C]` and the worldspace's own record carries that text as its full name, as the oracle's do; this is item (b) of the fix-sse-filters entry above). (2) The copy's `CollapseStorage` released the elements without undoing the init, which left a copied record without elements. (3) The masters-update subrecord sort ran for every record the update modified; upstream builds again (and sorts) only the records whose elements the update rewrote. (4) Harness: the `refs` comparison's CRLF normalisation literal had lost its CR; the port built the reference information only for sequences with `refs` though the GUI builds it for every `build_refs` sequence; a staged sequence without `strings` gave the port the tables of a renamed plugin the GUI never sees (the copied strings got IDs after the renamed plugin's last one); the GUI's plugin copies kept the data folder's file times, which a copy of the game data had reordered (`fnv-caravanpack` loaded `CaravanPack.esm` before `TribalPack.esm`); and the port read and wrote the reference cache in the game's data folder, so a later run loaded a cache instead of building (`sse-update-topic` differed in 4,103 records on the second run). Each is fixed and witnessed by the runs above. (5) Harness: `conflicts::private_data` removed the unwanted entries of a reused `Data` folder with `remove_file`, which fails on a folder (a reference cache folder a port run left would have failed later conflicts, merged and modgroups runs); it now removes folders too. No harness path writes a reference cache into a game's data folder any more (every port run that can build references loads a scratch copy or gets `--cache-path`, `-C:` or `--dont-cache`). (6) This branch had mapped the script indexes 14 and 26 of `IntToEsState` to `esConstructionComplete` and `esInternalLoad`; upstream maps every index past 12 to `esDummy`, which #31's mapping does, and the rebase keeps #31's.

### Phase 7: GUI

- **Model:** Opus 5.5 for the main window and the virtualised tree and grid. Sonnet 5.5 for the dialogs.
- **Port:** Main window (navigation tree, view tab with conflict colours, referenced-by, messages, information), all dialogs, themes, bookmarks, settings files. Every action calls a session command.
- **CLI:** No new commands. Any GUI action without a command is a bug against design rule 1.
- **Skills:** None new. The coverage ledger must show every GUI binding as covered or presentation-only.
- **Gate:** Ledger complete. Manual walkthrough of the upstream menus against the Delphi build.

### Phase 8: Performance

- **Model:** Fable 5.1. SIMD, `unsafe` and concurrency changes must keep output identical, and errors here are silent.
- **Work:** Profile with the benchmarks collected since phase 1. Add SIMD with runtime dispatch (SSE2 baseline, AVX2, AVX-512 where the CPU supports it) to hot loops: byte comparison in conflict detection, string and signature scanning, checksums, decompression, numeric conversion. Tune allocation with arenas and string interning. Parallelise remaining serial stages.
- **Rule:** Every optimisation keeps a scalar fallback and must pass the full parity harness. Output never depends on thread count or CPU features.
- **Gate:** Load, reference build, conflict scan, clean and archive pack are each faster than the Delphi build on the corpus, with numbers published in the repository.

### Phase 9: Release and cutover

- **Model:** Sonnet 5.5.
- **Work:** Release packaging with the per-game executable names (`FO4Edit.exe`, `SSEEdit.exe` and the rest). Migration notes. Point the agent plugin skills at `xedit mcp` and retire the `xEdit-llm` daemon route.

## Upstream sync skill

`syncing-upstream` is drafted in phase 2 and extended in every later phase. Procedure:

1. Fetch upstream and find the newest release tag that has published binaries. List the commits between the tag recorded in `upstream-map.toml` and that tag. Commits after the newest release are not merged, because nothing can verify them.
2. Classify each changed unit through the map: definitions, core, GUI, scripting, tools.
3. Port the change. Definition changes go through `xtask port-defs`; other changes are ported by hand with `porting-pascal-unit`.
4. Add or update the session command and CLI subcommand when the change adds an operation, and update the coverage ledger.
5. Point `XEDIT_ORACLE_DIR` at the binaries of the new release and run the parity harness.
6. Advance the recorded tag and commit with `cargo xtask sync <upstream checkout> <commit>` and open one pull request per upstream release.

A unit that is not yet ported gets its upstream change recorded as pending in the map, so nothing is lost while the port is in progress.

## Decisions

1. **GUI toolkit:** egui with a custom virtualised tree and grid.
2. **Game order:** Fallout 4 and Skyrim SE first. Every game mode upstream supports is in scope.
3. **Script compatibility:** full JvInterpreter Pascal compatibility. Compatibility with existing xEdit behaviour, scripts and files is the most important goal of the project and wins over every other concern.
4. **Platforms:** Windows first, then Linux and macOS. Windows-only code stays in thin platform modules.
5. **Side tools:** BSArch, BSArchPro, Sniff, xDump and LODGen are all in scope.

## Risks

- **Undocumented behaviour.** Much of xEdit's behaviour lives in definition callbacks and special cases. The parity harness on real masters is the main defence, so it is built in phase 0, before the port.
- **Definition volume.** 131,000 lines of definitions are not practical to port by hand. The transpiler is on the critical path of phase 1.
- **Scripts that use VCL forms.** 69 host registrations expose VCL classes. The form shim in phase 6 must cover what shipped and popular community scripts use.
- **Upstream drift during the port.** Handled by the pending entries in `upstream-map.toml` and by running `syncing-upstream` from phase 2 on.
